Absolute Regression
===================

Chapter 701: Everyone Knows Their Place, Right?
-----------------------------------------------
Sipchilgyo was afraid.

The ones who had captured them were none other than the Faceless Guests, the very followers of the Wicked Little Demon.

If they were dragged away by these people, pain worse than death would be waiting for them.

Ordinary assassins hid poison pellets in their molars in case they were captured, but the assassins of Gaingyo did not.

It was the will of their leader, Cha Iran.

If you have the strength to bite down on poison, then grit your teeth and struggle to survive to the very end!

‘I shouldn’t have let my guard down.’

No. This was not a matter of carelessness. Even if she had not let her guard down, the result would have been the same. An assassin exposed in the open was no different from an ordinary martial artist. And on top of that, they had been ambushed.

‘How on earth did they find us?’

Was there a traitor? Even if someone wanted to betray them, they could not. They did not know where one another lived. And yet these people had recognized and come for them with precision.

It was then.

Piiiiii, boom boom boom!

The sound of firecrackers bursting came from somewhere not far away. They were firecrackers that made a distinctive sound.

Sipchilgyo and Ogyo exchanged glances. Those firecrackers just now were the signal used among Gaingyo’s assassins to announce an emergency.

That meant these Faceless Guests were also moving against them elsewhere.

A Faceless Guest stuck his head out the window and looked toward the direction from which the firecrackers had sounded.

‘How can they not even think to look at us once?’

If they were men, surely they would glance at least once. Were they truly people raised by the Demon Cult to feel no emotion whatsoever?

Of course, she did not know. If the Faceless Guests did look, just how persistently they would stare.

The carriage slowly reduced its speed.

No matter who they were targeting this time, the two assassins had only one wish.

‘Please, let them have heard the signal!’

Just as the carriage entered a deserted street on the outskirts.

Thud, thud.

The Faceless Guest in the driver’s seat seemed to strike the rear wall hard, and then the carriage picked up speed.

Thwack! Thwack! Thwack!

They felt something fly in and embed itself in the carriage in quick succession.

Just as the Faceless Guest was about to open the curtain on the opposite window and look outside.

Swish!

A hidden weapon flew in through the window and shot out through the opposite one.

Thwack! Thwack! Thwack!

Again came the sound of hidden weapons embedding themselves in the carriage.

The carriage lurched as it was hastily turned. One wheel lifted, and it tilted to the side.

The Faceless Guest moved to the opposite side and drew up his internal energy.

The carriage, whose wheel had lifted, had just barely regained its balance when—

Bwaaaang!

A hidden weapon shaped like a short arrow pierced through the carriage wall and flew inside. It was no ordinary hidden weapon. It was powerful enough to penetrate the carriage wall. And the place where that hidden weapon flew was right where Sipchilgyo and Ogyo were sitting.

The Faceless Guest struck down at the hidden weapon flying toward them with his dagger and knocked it aside.

Clang.

The force of the incoming weapon was so powerful that his wrist hurt as if it would snap, but thanks to that, the hidden weapon changed direction.

The hidden weapon flew through the space behind the heads of the two seated women.

If the Faceless Guest had not knocked it away, both of them would have lost their lives.

At the same time.

Slash!

Another hidden weapon flew in and tore through the arm of the Faceless Guest, who had exposed himself at the window to shield them.

Blood sprayed from his arm, but there was no time to tend to the wound.

Another hidden weapon flew in.

Swaaaish!

A longer hidden weapon than before shot toward the place where the women were sitting. It was faster and stronger than the previous one.

The Faceless Guest grabbed the two women and pulled them to the seat where he had been.

Bwaaaang!

The hidden weapon pierced through the place where they had been sitting and flew on.

Outside, a merciless attack was being unleashed. Sipchilgyo and Ogyo knew who the woman attacking them was.

Palgyo, she was the most skilled in hidden weapons in all of Gaingyo.

Of all people, it had to be her.

Palgyo’s nature was selfish and ruthless. Even if she knew allies were riding in the carriage, she was the sort of person who would still launch an indiscriminate attack.

Clang! Clang!

The Faceless Guest knocked aside the hidden weapons that pierced through the carriage again. One of them was a weapon that would have struck the two women, and he blocked it despite the danger.

‘Why is he going this far to keep us alive?’

Sipchilgyo was inwardly puzzled. If one spoke of cruelty and ruthlessness, these Faceless Guests should have been worse, not better.

Hidden weapons poured in, but the carriage ran well. The Faceless Guest in the driver’s seat drove while knocking away the incoming weapons with finger wind so they would not hit him or the horses.

Both his skill at driving the horses and his skill at firing finger wind were superb.

After he struck the rear wall with a thump, thump to give a signal, he stopped the carriage beside a place with trees for cover.

The moment the carriage stopped!

The Faceless Guest opened the carriage door, threw the women outside, and then hurled himself out as well.

Papapapak!

Because the carriage had stopped, the hidden weapons flew in with even greater precision.

The Faceless Guest who got out of the carriage first checked the driver’s seat. Perhaps he had thrown himself somewhere, but his companion who had been driving the horses was nowhere to be seen.

Ting! Whoosh!

Just as a hidden weapon that had struck the wall and bounced off flew toward Sipchilgyo’s face.

Swish.

The Faceless Guest sent out a finger wind and knocked the hidden weapon away. He was faithfully keeping Geom Mugeuk’s joke that they must not strike the face.

Just as he was about to hide the two women behind a tree.

Swaaaaaaaaaa!

Hidden weapons poured down on them like rain.

Clang clang clang clang clang clang!

The Faceless Guest could have thrown himself away and dodged alone, but he blocked in front of Sipchilgyo and Ogyo and struck the weapons down.

Blood splattered across his white mask, but the Faceless Guest did not dodge.

Sipchilgyo and Ogyo silently stared at his back as he stayed in place until the very end, trying to protect them. It was truly a situation where they did not know whom they should be cheering for.

‘He can’t block it! It’s impossible!’

What Palgyo was unleashing now was no ordinary hidden weapon technique. It was a Golden Dragon hidden weapon of the Murim Alliance, the Annihilating Downpour, which she was said to use only in the most urgent moments.

Blood sprayed from the Faceless Guest’s shoulder and waist. Even if his arms and legs were injured, he somehow avoided his vital points and blocked them.

Clang!

With one final clear ring, the Faceless Guest let his arm drop.

He breathed roughly. He was too exhausted to block any more hidden weapons. Even a single dagger flying in would be the end of him.

But no more hidden weapons came.

When the Faceless Guest raised his head, he saw the Faceless Guest from the driver’s seat standing on a distant rooftop across the way, looking toward him.

Beside him, a woman lay collapsed.

While he had been blocking the hidden weapons, his companion had succeeded in subduing her.

A short while later, three assassins sat side by side in the tattered carriage.

Sipchilgyo, Ogyo, and Palgyo as well.

Only then did the Faceless Guest properly treat his injuries.

Though none were critical wounds, several were torn quite deeply. With an indifferent expression, he pressed his acupoints to stop the bleeding, applied wound medicine, and wrapped them in bandages.

“Ugh.”

Seeing the Faceless Guest’s eyes twist in pain, Sipchilgyo felt strangely unfamiliar with the thought that this man, too, felt pain.

There was a great deal of blood on his mask. Most of it was blood he had shed to protect her.

This time, Sipchilgyo thought that Faceless Guest would surely not endure it. After all, Palgyo had nearly killed him by using the Golden Dragon hidden weapons in succession. Even if he did not kill her, Sipchilgyo thought he would beat her half to death.

But the Faceless Guest did not even hurl a single common curse. He simply looked out the window.

‘What on earth is he thinking?’

Sipchilgyo also silently looked at the scenery flowing past through the holes in the broken carriage.

* * *

The women arrived at the reception hall.

The women gathered today to receive their seating assignments were those who had advanced to the main round but had failed to make the final seven.

Those who knew one another exchanged greetings and chatted.

“To think I’d come here again.”

“I thought I’d never be able to come back.”

This was the very reception hall where they had waited during the main round of the competition.

“We were sitting here, weren’t we?”

“Back then, I thought I would make it to the finals.”

Laughter broke out here and there. The atmosphere was good. There was a strange sense of camaraderie that came from having fallen short together.

Once all the women had entered the reception hall, a warrior wearing a bamboo hat pulled low followed them in and closed the door.

Chief Jo was waiting in the reception hall.

“The reason I have gathered all of you today is to arrange the seating for the final stage.”

One side of the reception hall had been decorated like a stage, with chairs arranged behind it for them to sit in.

“Please make sure you clearly confirm where your seat is today so that there are no mistakes on the day itself. Now, I will begin assigning seats.”

Chief Jo called out each person’s name and assigned them a seat. For some reason, he seemed more tense and rigid than usual, but everyone was busy confirming their own seats and did not pay attention to that.

“Now, come forward and sit where the numbers are written. On the day itself, the chairs will not have numbers on them, so you must not get confused.”

In that way, one by one, he had them sit.

All the women’s seats were decided, and they sat there looking at the stage.

The stage had been made only as a mock-up for seating arrangements, but their regret was real.

A sigh escaped someone’s lips. Everyone knew it was a sigh filled with regret—perhaps that stage could have been hers. After all, they were all letting out the same sigh in their hearts.

At that moment, a shrill voice rang out. The words were hard to believe even after hearing them.

“You stupid bitch, why are you sighing? You got eliminated because you’re ugly.”

Everyone’s gaze turned toward the woman who had hurled the insult. She was even sitting beside the woman who had sighed. At her words, the woman who had sighed widened her eyes in shock.

“What did you just say?”

“Are you deaf too? You were acting so high and mighty from the preliminaries. Serves you right.”

Slap!

The woman who had sighed slapped her across the face without mercy.

“You bitch, you hit me?”

The two women grabbed each other by the hair and began fighting.

Of course, it was a situation that should have been stopped, but another woman watching them shouted.

“Kill her!”

What was this now? The women’s gazes turned toward the one who had shouted to kill her. As if it had not been a joke or idle remark, she shouted again in a sharp tone for her to kill her.

The woman who had insulted her, the woman who had immediately slapped her, and the woman who was watching them and shouting for someone to be killed—all of them were abnormal.

Even three abnormal women appearing would have been a lot, but another emerged.

“How low-class. No wonder pathetic bitches like you got eliminated. I can’t stand being with you.”

At another woman’s words, a woman in the row behind her struck her hard on the head.

“You bitch, you’re the most pathetic one here!”

This time, two women clung to each other and began fighting.

Chief Jo ran over in shock.

“Stop! What is wrong with all of you?”

He tried to stop them, but the excited women could see nothing else.

The fight spread in an instant. It was not as though someone had incited them into fighting. Nor were they split into two sides.

They simply fought with the person beside them, and with those toward whom they had accumulated resentment during the earlier rounds. Some even fought with people they had never exchanged a single word with.

Shouts and curses erupted from every direction. As if they had all been waiting for this very moment, everyone screamed and hurled abuse at their opponents.

They swung fists, scratched, and tore clothes. They grabbed hair and dragged one another around.

There were women who bit others, and women who sat down and burst into tears.

There were women who cursed their parents while lamenting their lot in life, and women who screamed that they were the greatest beauty under heaven. Truly, they were dragging out and saying everything they had hidden away and never been able to say.

Chief Jo’s mouth fell open at the sight that unfolded in an instant. He had truly never seen anything like it in his life.

No matter how much resentment they had over being eliminated before the finals, could people really become like this?

It was impossible to believe these were the same elegant and beautiful women he had seen when they entered this place. It was as though they had suddenly been struck by madness and gone collectively insane.

Of course, there were also women standing perfectly fine and watching the scene. So it was not a contagious illness.

In any case, he could not simply leave this pandemonium be.

“Stop! Stop this at once!”

Chief Jo’s shout rang out.

As if it were a lie, the noisy space began to grow quiet.

Even Chief Jo, who had shouted, was startled.

Everyone had suddenly stopped fighting cold.

The hands grabbing collars let go, and the hands tugging hair released their grip.

The women looked around in bewilderment. They straightened their torn clothes, fixed their disheveled hair, and wiped away nosebleeds.

They did not look like people who had been fighting like madwomen moments ago.

Rather, their expressions said this:

What did I just do? Was that really something worth fighting over to this extent?

Everyone looked embarrassed.

“What in the world are you doing?”

At Chief Jo’s rebuke, the women who had fought finally lowered their heads.

“We’re sorry.”

Some women bowed their heads and apologized, while others, ashamed, hid behind people. Some even apologized to the very opponents they had been spitting at and cursing moments before.

At that moment, the warrior in the bamboo hat who had been standing by the entrance walked forward.

The women were suddenly frightened. They had caused a disturbance and even fought before an important stage. Sinhwabang could punish them or spread rumors about them.

The bamboo-hatted warrior raised his hand and pointed at the women. He herded the women he pointed out to one side. They were all women who had become excited and fought just moments ago.

Not only that, he also pointed out several among the women who had not fought and had merely watched.

After separating the women in that way, the bamboo-hatted warrior said to Chief Jo,

“Take them out.”

Even as they left, the women were puzzled. Normally, should he not send back the women who had not fought and keep the ones who had fought to scold them?

Yet he had sent out all the women who had fought.

Ah, of course, there were also a few women who had not fought among them. No one could tell what standard he was using to send them out.

Without a word of complaint, Chief Jo took them outside.

Now, only twenty women remained there.

The remaining women looked at one another with grave expressions.

Naturally so, because the people who should not have been left together in a place like this remained.

They were none other than the assassins of Gaingyo.

The highest-ranking among them was Samgyo. She could not understand this situation. Among so many women, in the midst of that pandemonium—

‘How on earth did he leave only us behind?’

Samgyo and the other assassins turned their gazes toward the bamboo-hatted warrior.

The bamboo-hatted warrior walked forward.

He set upright a chair that had fallen during the fight and sat down on it.

When he slowly removed his bamboo hat, the Wicked Little Demon appeared, wearing a face veil.

“You all know your seats, don’t you?”

The Wicked Little Demon’s low voice echoed through the hall filled with assassins.

“For now, sit down.”

Chapter 702: I’m Not Here to Kill You
-------------------------------------
The women had not fought because the anger that had built up inside them had exploded. A single energy had shaken their very nature.

Soaksim.

Geugak Soma’s unique demonic energy, which awakened humanity’s primal violence.

When exposed to Soaksim, one’s heart began to pound and one felt the urge to kill someone. In fact, if someone with weak internal energy was strongly exposed to Soaksim, they might even stab the person beside them to death.

But assassins were people who controlled their emotions better than anyone. Even with the same internal energy and skill, they were far less affected than ordinary martial artists. The way they endured and revealed their anger and violence was also subtly different.

Exposed to Soaksim, the women could not escape the keen eyes of Geugak Soma, whose understanding of evil was deeper than anyone’s.

Third Gyo recognized the man who had taken off his bamboo hat. Yes, this was a man fully qualified to remain at ease before twenty of them.

“Geugak Soma.”

She already knew that the man guarding the mistress of Cheonhwaru was Geugak Soma. What she had not known was that today, when the seats for the finals stage were being assigned, he would make the first move like this.

She did not know about Soaksim, but at the very least, she could be certain of one thing. Geugak Soma had used some special method to pick them out from among the women.

Geugak Soma looked at a chair a short distance away. It meant for her to come and sit.

It was a situation where she had to hear what he intended to say. Third Gyo walked over and sat down.

The remaining assassins spread out to the left and right with Third Gyo at the center. They took positions from which they could immediately launch a joint attack if a fight broke out. But Geugak Soma did not seem to care at all.

‘Seeing him up close…’

She could properly see Geugak Soma’s eyes behind the veil. She had glimpsed him during the main tournament, but this was the first time she had seen him this close.

Before meeting Geugak Soma this time, she had misunderstood him.

She had thought Geugak Soma must look so evil that it would be hard to face him, or that he must have hideous scars or burns. That was why he wore a mask, she had assumed.

‘To think he was this handsome.’

She could tell just from his eyes and forehead. He was truly a handsome man.

‘Perhaps his nose or mouth is hideous.’

In any case, his first impression made her curious about his real face.

Geugak Soma asked her,

“Do you have the authority to decide right now?”

Geugak Soma was asking whether she had to move only as her organization had instructed her, or whether she could make some decision on her own.

“What if I do?”

“Tell your subordinates not to take their own lives.”

She had expected the words that followed to be something like, “Because I will kill them brutally myself!”—words meant to break their spirit.

But Geugak Soma’s next words were completely unexpected.

“I did not come to kill you.”

Third Gyo stared silently at Geugak Soma. She was confused because it did not seem to be a lie. He had found all of them, yet he was saying he would not kill them? Geugak Soma, of all people?

Third Gyo answered in a low voice.

“We do not commit suicide.”

As if that was enough, Geugak Soma nodded.

Third Gyo slowly rose from her seat. He was probably trying to confuse them with the claim that he had not come to kill them, weakening their will to fight.

Riiip.

When she tore the lower part of her palace dress in a long line, her thigh was revealed. A dagger was tucked into the leather strap wrapped around it. Drawing the dagger, Third Gyo said to the other assassins,

“We must kill him at all costs. There is no way Geugak Soma will spare us.”

The assassins all drew their weapons at once.

Some women drew hidden daggers like Third Gyo, while others wound sharp fishing lines around both hands and pulled them taut. One woman drew a flexible sword that had been disguised as a belt.

When an ornamental pendant was taken apart, small hidden weapons came out, and there was even a woman who pulled out a hairpin. Blue-edged hidden weapons emerged from inside a bracelet as well.

Twenty beautiful women each drew the concealed weapons they had hidden. It was not a sight one could easily see, even after living a lifetime as a martial artist.

Geugak Soma, who had risen from his seat, put his hand into his robes. Not knowing what would come out, all the assassins tensed.

What emerged in Geugak Soma’s hand was his mask.

The mask covered his face, and the veil he had been wearing slipped down as softly as a falling flower.

Now what covered Geugak Soma’s face was not a veil, but a white mask.

Third Gyo and the assassins grew tense. It was the first time they were seeing with their own eyes the white mask of Geugak Soma, which they had only heard of in rumors.

The feeling he gave off when wearing the veil and when wearing the mask was different. Until just a moment ago, she had thought, How can his eyes be so handsome? But when those eyes entered beneath the mask, the impression changed completely.

Those unfamiliar, chilling eyes smiled from within the mask. Looking at twenty assassins who had drawn their unique weapons, he smiled.

There was yet another pressure added to that.

Geugak Soma had drawn a ten-thousand-year cold iron dagger.

As far as Third Gyo knew.

‘I thought Geugak Soma used finger winds and palm techniques?’

That was why she had intended to fight this battle while being careful of finger and palm techniques. But he was drawing a dagger?

The pressure that dagger gave off was completely different when held in Geugak Soma’s hand than when held in someone else’s.

Normally, Third Gyo would have said this before a fight.

Even if it’s you, you won’t be able to face all of us.

But before Geugak Soma’s overwhelming presence, Third Gyo had this thought.

‘I should have grabbed those women by the hair and gone out with them earlier.’

She could tell that her subordinates were feeling the same intimidation. Having greater numbers did not necessarily mean they had an advantage.

Fundamentally, assassins moved alone or under a thoroughly prepared plan. Right now, they had no plan whatsoever, and naturally, there had never been a time when twenty of them had launched a joint attack together.

Still, there was one thing she could hope for.

“If each person inflicts just one wound, the twentieth person will walk out of here.”

In a situation where their momentum had been suppressed, those words would be more helpful than vaguely saying they could win. If they fought to the death, someone could survive. That someone could be you.

As befitted a leader, Third Gyo was the first to launch herself toward Geugak Soma.

Swish! Swiiish! Swish!

Third Gyo’s dagger sliced through the air in rapid succession. Every strike flew only toward Geugak Soma’s vital points.

It was an attack that anticipated many things. How she would respond if he blocked or dodged, how she would unfold the next move, and how she would react if he counterattacked and knocked the dagger from her hand.

But she had not expected Geugak Soma’s movements to be this fast.

She could not even tell how Geugak Soma had avoided her attack. In that flashing instant, Geugak Soma brushed past her. He was so fast that she did not even have time to raise her protective qi.

She felt something skim past her neck.

Slice.

‘Damn it! I’ve been cut!’

Just as she despaired, thinking her neck would split open and spray blood,

she did not fall.

‘Why?’

She checked her own neck. A little blood stained her hand. It was only the blood that had beaded on her neck.

She quickly reflected her neck in her dagger.

A single line of blood drawn across her neck. He had not cut deeply, only skimmed it ever so slightly.

‘Was it a mistake?’

But it was not a mistake.

Eleventh Gyo and Twentieth Gyo, who next exchanged a move with Geugak Soma, were also standing a short distance away, each touching her own neck.

They too had received attacks that would have killed them if the cuts had been just a little deeper, yet they stood there with only faint lines of blood drawn on them.

‘If he had meant to kill us, we would be dead.’

Only then could Third Gyo objectively see the way Geugak Soma fought.

She had thought his attacks struck because he was simply fast.

That was not it. Geugak Soma’s attacks were tracing trajectories she could never have imagined. Even if they could handle that speed, they would never be able to block those peculiar attacks.

The hidden weapons thrown by Nineteenth Gyo were all deflected by Geugak Soma’s dagger, and Sixteenth Gyo and Thirteenth Gyo, who faltered while dodging the ricocheting weapons, had their necks cut one after another.

‘How can he do that?’

What was astonishing was that regardless of the opponent’s level of skill, the location and depth of the wound were the same.

It was a realm far more difficult than simply cutting their necks and killing them all.

If we leave twenty wounds on him, we can survive?

Hearing those words, how laughable must Geugak Soma have found them? They could not leave even a single wound on Geugak Soma.

And not all the assassins submitted to this overwhelming difference in skill.

Slice!

Nineteenth Gyo, whose neck had already been cut once, launched herself forward to attack again. Though she knew he had spared her, she had launched another surprise attack.

Whoosh.

Of course, it did not work. Geugak Soma lightly avoided Nineteenth Gyo’s ambush, grabbed her by the neck, and slammed her into the floor.

Bang!

Just as Nineteenth Gyo, driven into the floor, was about to charge in again,

“!”

Nineteenth Gyo saw the eyes inside the mask looking down at her.

Goosebumps rose all over her body. She had never feared death, but this was not a matter of life and death.

Death was not frightening, but those eyes were. The fear that if she wronged this person, she might suffer pain worse than death—Geugak Soma’s gaze stirred a primal terror.

Geugak Soma left her as she was, looked at Third Gyo, and asked,

“You said you wouldn’t take your own lives, didn’t you?”

The words also implied that if they acted like this, he would kill them all.

Third Gyo could say nothing.

In the midst of that, this time Seventh Gyo was ambushing Geugak Soma.

Swish!

But when her dagger cut through empty air, what returned was a line of blood drawn across her neck.

When she too tried to charge again, ignoring Geugak Soma’s earlier warning,

Third Gyo launched herself forward and grabbed her wrist.

Third Gyo looked at Seventh Gyo and shook her head. She knew Seventh Gyo’s temperament was no ordinary thing, but if she did not stop her, she would die a dog’s death. If they provoked Geugak Soma’s temper the wrong way, he might kill them all.

Thus, a single wind called Geugak Soma blew through Gaingyo.

What the wind left behind was a single line of blood.

Silence flowed through the hall.

The women looked at the bloodlines drawn across one another’s necks with disbelieving eyes.

Third Gyo could tell.

If Geugak Soma had truly meant to kill them, this would not have been a fight, but a massacre.

‘He’s this strong?’

She had long known that Demon Venerables were terrifying, but she had not known his skill was to this extent.

If this had been Geugak Soma before Geom Mugeuk’s regression, he would not have produced such an overwhelming result. Many women would have died, and this place would have become a sea of blood.

But compared to then, Geugak Soma’s internal energy and martial realm had both risen. On top of that were the experiences of several desperate life-and-death battles that had brought him to the brink of death. And added to that was the Sohyang Secret Art he had newly mastered.

Geugak Soma slowly walked back and sat in the seat where he had been sitting.

“Now, all of you sit.”

Third Gyo let out a sigh and sat down. She made her subordinates sit as well.

“Everyone, sit.”

Now the remaining assassins also sat in their own seats.

Then, as if he had been waiting, the door opened and a masked Mumyeongaek entered.

Mumyeongaek went around one by one and sealed the mute and paralysis acupoints of the women seated there.

At that moment, one assassin launched a surprise attack in an attempt to take Mumyeongaek hostage. It was Seventh Gyo, who earlier had tried to rush at Geugak Soma before being stopped by Third Gyo.

The next instant.

Crack!

Seventh Gyo, struck in the chest, was sent flying.

Mumyeongaek had evaded her ambush and struck her hard with his elbow in the same motion. She collapsed to the floor and coughed up blood, but she did not die.

This Mumyeongaek, who had remained alone to assist Geugak Soma, possessed the strongest martial arts among the ten Mumyeongaek who had come this time. That was why the surprise attack did not work.

Mumyeongaek sealed the mute and paralysis acupoints of the fallen woman, then set her upright again. With her head hanging low, unconscious, no one stepped forward anymore.

In that way, the mute and paralysis acupoints of all twenty assassins were completely sealed.

‘Not everyone has been captured yet.’

The remaining assassins would be able to help Cha Iran and complete the original plan.

Just as Third Gyo clung to that final thread of hope, the sound of a carriage arriving came from outside.

When Mumyeongaek opened the door of the guest hall, the carriage that had arrived outside came into view. Hidden weapons were embedded in it like the quills of a hedgehog, and it had been attacked so severely that it was astonishing it still maintained its shape, with holes and broken sections everywhere.

Third Gyo’s eyes trembled as she looked at the carriage.

‘Surely not?’

The carriage door opened, and Seventeenth Gyo, Fifth Gyo, and Eighth Gyo got out. Seeing that even they had been captured and brought here, Third Gyo was greatly shocked.

‘How did they find them too?’

On the other hand, as Seventeenth Gyo entered the guest hall, she saw the figures of her comrades. She did not see Geugak Soma standing by the window or Mumyeongaek standing behind the door.

Instead, seeing her comrades sitting unharmed in their seats, she inwardly cheered. Third Gyo was there, and she could see Seventh Gyo as well. Their number was as many as twenty.

‘I’m saved!’

At the same time, a worry for the Mumyeongaek following behind her brushed through Seventeenth Gyo’s mind.

‘You saved me, but…’

She would not be able to save him. The number seventeen was not a number that could decide such great matters.

‘You are going to die.’

Chapter 703: If You Don’t Answer After I’ve Asked Twice
-------------------------------------------------------
Sipchilgyo’s steps slowed.

The Mumyeonggaek followed behind her, knowing nothing.

She hadn’t known about the others, but she had wanted to save this Mumyeonggaek at least.

But she could not. Her vital points had been sealed, so she couldn’t even warn him, and had she truly tried, she would have lost her life.

It had been a life spent solely contemplating how to kill another.

Thus, harboring the desire to save someone felt truly unfamiliar. No, it was the first time she had ever felt it.

Just then, the Mumyeonggaek behind her pushed her back. Her thoughts had deepened so much that her steps had grown too slow.

Sipchilgyo turned around.

They had been together the entire ride in the carriage, but this was the first time their eyes had met like this. Because he had only been staring outside.

Sipchilgyo turned back and moved her steps.

And so they entered the guest hall. The moment she stepped inside, Sipchilgyo flinched in surprise. Behind the open door, a single Mumyeonggaek was standing.

And by the window, there was another Mumyeonggaek. He was leaning against the wall, looking into the guest hall, and Sipchilgyo could tell at a glance that he was no ordinary Mumyeonggaek.

The very presence he exuded was different from that of the other Mumyeonggaeks. That presence naturally brought a single name to mind.

‘Geukak Soma!’

Was it because of his pure white mask?

The moment she saw Geukak Soma, a massive, snow-white wolf came to mind. A lone wolf standing on a snowy plain where snowflakes flew.

Only then did her comrades properly come into view. They were all staring this way with rigid expressions.

Only now did she understand. That Samgyo and the twenty assassins of Gaein-gyo had all been subdued.

Why hadn’t she considered that they might have been subdued as well?

Then Geukak Soma asked her.

“Are you alright?”

Sipchilgyo briefly wondered if he was asking her, but at that moment, someone answered from behind.

“Yes, I am fine.”

Startled, Sipchilgyo turned around.

She realized it was the Mumyeonggaek who had saved her, answering with a respectful fist-and-palm salute. Geukak Soma had been asking if he was alright—he who was wounded in several places, his martial robe soaked in blood.

At that sight, Sipchilgyo was surprised once more.

‘So he could speak after all!’

She had thought all along that Mumyeonggaeks were people who couldn’t speak. She had thought they were ones who had their tongues pulled out and even their emotions removed.

He even exchanged glances with the Mumyeonggaek who had originally been in the guest hall. Surprisingly, they smiled at each other.

‘What, they know how to smile too?’

The Mumyeonggaek took Sipchilgyo and seated her in a chair. Sitting there, Sipchilgyo finally realized her predicament—their predicament of being captured by those terrifying Mumyeonggaeks and Geukak Soma of the Magyo.

Her gaze turned toward her senior and junior assassins.

Since they couldn’t open their mouths anyway, they quietly exchanged glances. Then she saw it. A single line of blood drawn across everyone’s neck.

‘Could it be?’

Sipchilgyo could tell. That they had all suffered wounds from a single person.

‘It was Geukak Soma.’

The Geukak Soma she knew was someone who did not suit saving people at all. Someone renowned as the most cruel among the Demonic Lords.

‘Then why on earth isn’t he killing us?’

Meanwhile, other carriages arrived one after another.

From them too descended subdued assassins. All the assassins of Gaein-gyo mobilized this time were being brought in captured.

The Mumyeonggaeks seated the captured assassins in empty spots. The assassins brought in later were increasingly astonished by the greater number of captured comrades.

Were we such a powerless organization?

They were women who had been confident they could kill any man in the world if they set their minds to it.

But to those masked ones, their beauty did not work. It was blocked by those masks. As if they had met their natural enemy.

The Mumyeonggaeks who had gone out like that all returned after subduing the assassins.

Looking at the captured, Samgyo could tell.

‘Everyone except Ilgyo and Igyo has been captured.’

It was fortunate they weren’t being killed immediately, but she did not entertain the naive thought that the Magyo would simply let them live.

Since they had been targeted, Chairan, Ilgyo, and Igyo might also be in danger.

She also worried that they might be using them as hostages to exploit Chairan.

Her gaze turned toward the carriage parked outside.

Now there was only one thing to believe in.

Whether he received a report that a broken carriage had arrived at the Sinhwabang guest hall, or a report that a fight had broken out among women at the guest hall, she hoped the Sect Leader of Sinhwabang would come here leading experts himself.

No matter how fearsome Geukak Soma was, with the grand meeting only two days away, he wouldn’t be able to touch the Sect Leader.

She hoped he would come and create a variable.

But no matter how long they waited, the Sect Leader of Sinhwabang did not come.

* * *

Three people were sitting in the Sect Leader’s hall of Sinhwabang, drinking.

The one sitting in the middle was the Sinhwabang Sect Leader, An Cheonggwang, and the other two were Yeom Sageom and Cheol Hando.

The two masters who had helped An Cheonggwang build Sinhwabang into what it was today.

“Only two days remain now.”

Chairan had conveyed the plan that she would kill the Sado Alliance Leader on the final stage. Originally, the plan was to kill him at the celebratory banquet a few days later, but with the Sado Alliance Leader arriving early, the plan had been moved up.

“Don’t forget the promise you made us.”

At Yeom Sageom’s words, An Cheonggwang nodded.

“Have I ever broken a promise to you?”

There had been many times. He probably couldn’t remember. Or perhaps he was pretending not to.

And there was a bigger reason than getting confirmation of the promise. It was because they knew well that An Cheonggwang’s ambition was a blaze that burned into another conflagration the more it was fulfilled.

With such a man, one had to seize what was offered with certainty when it was given. Because he would be unable to remember again.

What An Cheonggwang had promised the two was immense wealth and power.

If the plan to pin that assassination on Heukcheonmun after killing the Sado Alliance Leader succeeded, the Heukcheonmun would suffer annihilation at the hands of Bisa-in, who would become the next Sado Alliance Leader.

If that happened, Sinhwabang would rise to become the foremost faction in Anhui, and would be able to swallow all the forces that the Heukcheonmun had held.

If only that came to pass, becoming the foremost sect of the Sado would not be a vague dream either.

“Come, let us drink.”

The three men toasted.

Just as the cups clinked with a cheerful sound—

Creeeak.

The door there opened and someone entered.

Having confirmed who had entered, the three men jumped up from their seats in shock.

Amazingly, the person who entered was Baek Jagang, the Sado Alliance Leader.

If the Sado Alliance Leader had come, there should have been a signal from the martial artist guarding outside; the fact that there had been no signal until he arrived here meant he had come in secret.

Something felt ominous, but An Cheonggwang welcomed him with a bright expression and rushed forward.

“Welcome, Alliance Leader.”

An Cheonggwang greeted him with a respectful fist-and-palm salute.

“What brings you here without sending word?”

Baek Jagang stared at his face silently, then abruptly asked.

“Why did you do it?”

“!”

In an instant, An Cheonggwang’s heart sank. Could it be that the Alliance Leader had come knowing everything?

Not only him, but the spines of Yeom Sageom and Cheol Hando, standing behind, grew cold.

“What do you mean?”

A brief, heavy silence fell. An Cheonggwang, not knowing what this was about, did not avoid Baek Jagang’s gaze, his eyes proclaiming his innocence.

Baek Jagang’s small eyes formed a smile.

“If there is a drinking party, you should have invited me too.”

Only then did An Cheonggwang inwardly breathe a sigh of relief.

“I feared it would be an imposition on one so exalted and did not invite you. My apologies, Alliance Leader.”

Yeom Sageom and Cheol Hando, standing behind, greeted him respectfully with a fist-and-palm salute.

“We greet the Alliance Leader.”

Baek Jagang looked at them. Then he repeated exactly what he had said to An Cheonggwang earlier.

“Why did you do it?”

Hearing the same question again, Yeom Sageom and Cheol Hando were inwardly flustered. They had committed a crime, so they felt pricked with guilt, but Yeom Sageom asked with a smile.

“What do you mean?”

Then, as if it had been a joke again this time, Baek Jagang spoke with a laugh.

“If you came, you should have greeted me first.”

“You seemed busy, so I planned to greet you at the banquet. My apologies.”

After exchanging greetings, the four sat around a round table again.

An Cheonggwang poured wine for Baek Jagang.

“Alliance Leader, won’t you have a drink first?”

Baek Jagang accepted the wine and then poured for the three men. As he poured the first cup for Cheol Hando, who was sitting on the right, he asked him.

“Did I not ask why you did it?”

The three men flinched again. They had thought that question had been answered and passed like a joke earlier, but now he was demanding an answer again.

“What do you mean—”

Before his words could finish, a gust of wind was heard.

Shiiiiik!

Shk!

It was also the sound of wind that carried his answer to a place where it could never be heard.

Thud.

Cheol Hando’s head fell onto the table and rolled around before stopping beside the wine bottle.

“Shit!”

Spewing a curse, Yeom Sageom knocked over his chair and retreated backward, while An Cheonggwang sat frozen in place, his eyes wide.

He hadn’t imagined the Alliance Leader would cut down Cheol Hando so suddenly without warning. And with a single stroke at that.

Click.

Baek Jagang sheathed the sword he had extended into the air back into its scabbard.

Then he spoke as if nothing had happened.

“These days my anger seems to have worsened; twice is my limit. I asked twice, and when I received no answer, I could not stand it.”

Baek Jagang looked at Yeom Sageom, who had retreated far back, and lifted the wine bottle. It was a gesture meaning to come and receive wine.

Yeom Sageom could tell. That Baek Jagang had come knowing everything. There was no way he would have done something like this otherwise.

‘If I accept that wine, I die.’

The terror of death flooded in. A life lived traversing the Anhui martial arts world, thinking there was nothing to fear. But now, faced with impending death, his whole body trembled.

Yeom Sageom looked at An Cheonggwang. An Cheonggwang sat without saying a word. But then, what clever solution could he have in this situation? He must be wracking his brains to somehow escape this situation.

Yeom Sageom slowly walked over and sat down.

He was prepared to draw his sword at any moment. Even if he died, he wouldn’t die so futilely.

On the table flowing with blood, he received wine from the Sado Alliance Leader.

Was it because he thought it might be his last drink?

The wine that had been sweet until a moment ago was bitter.

“...The wine tastes fine.”

Baek Jagang asked again.

“Why did you do it?”

The second question asked of him—he had to answer.

He answered honestly.

“For money and power—was there any reason not to?”

Baek Jagang nodded with an expression that said he understood up to that point.

“You thought it was me?”

“No, I did not know that.”

The moment he answered, a chill crept up Baek Jagang’s ear.

Baek Jagang stared at him quietly, then spoke calmly.

“Have one last drink and be on your way.”

Yeom Sageom’s eyes widened. Was he sparing him? It was hard to believe, but he had to escape this place first. No matter what lay outside hell, could it be worse than hell?

Yeom Sageom poured wine for Baek Jagang.

Clink.

In that very instant!

Shiiik!

Puh-uk.

In an instant, Baek Jagang’s sword, which had split the air, was piercing Yeom Sageom’s heart.

His blood trickled down the blade, while the wine from the bottle still filled Baek Jagang’s cup.

Clink.

Baek Jagang drank all the wine the dead man had poured, then pulled the sword embedded in the heart.

Thud.

Yeom Sageom’s corpse fell backward.

An Cheonggwang could understand.

That the words “have one last drink and be on your way” had not meant to leave this place.

They had meant to leave this world.

Baek Jagang wasted no time. He didn’t try to test his opponent. He only asked exactly what needed to be asked.

“Why did you do it?”

It was the second question asked of him. He too could no longer delay or read the room.

“I wanted to devour Anhui’s martial world, and all of the Heretical Path. I dreamed the dream that the Alliance Leader once dreamed.”

An Cheonggwang rose from his seat and walked toward the armchair at the back, recounting the day Chairan had come to him. Though he rose and moved, Baek Jagang simply listened.

“...If I hadn’t accepted their proposal, I would have gone to Heukcheonmun. The one falsely accused would have been our Sinhwabang.”

He spoke as if wronged, but it didn’t work on Baek Jagang.

“That would never have happened. The Heukcheonmun leader is loyal to me. They wouldn’t have approached you in the first place.”

Faith in the Master of the Heukcheon Sect and contempt for An Cheonggwang flowed out at the same time.

“Temptation always chooses the easier path, after all.”

An Cheonggwang’s face twisted where he sat in the grand armchair, and he shouted as if in desperation.

“You are far too arrogant.”

The moment An Cheonggwang manipulated the armrest of the chair—

Srrrng.

The walls around where Baek Jagang sat rotated, revealing hundreds of tiny holes. The ceiling opened as well, and hundreds more holes appeared there.

It was the mechanism he had built for moments of crisis like this. A device made in secret over a very long time, at enormous expense.

Baek Jagang recognized the mechanism.

“The Death-Extinguishing Ten Thousand Needles Gate (死滅萬針關). A fine mechanism indeed. Did you know? It was made by a technician from this Alliance.”

His composure grated on him, but An Cheonggwang trusted this mechanism. He trusted the money and time poured into it.

“Do not pretend to be at ease! Even you cannot come out unscathed.”

Originally, he had never intended to use this mechanism on Baek Jagang. He had meant to use it if Cha Iran betrayed him.

“Damn it!”

Even if the Lord of the Sado Alliance died here and he escaped through the secret passage behind the grand armchair, he would spend the rest of his life hiding his identity and face while being hunted.

“You were not supposed to die here.”

It should have been the Heukcheon Sect that killed him.

“How could you not endure two mere days and shatter my dream like this!”

Hundreds of hidden-weapon apertures were aimed at him, yet Baek Jagang was not shaken in the slightest.

“If you had used the money spent on making a mechanism like this to raise the Shinhwa Gang, you would have realized your dream. You simply chose the easy path.”

At that moment, An Cheonggwang could bear it no longer and activated the mechanism.

Clack!

But the mechanism did not activate.

Clack, clack!

No matter how many times he pressed the device, only metallic sounds rang out; the mechanism did not attack.

Baek Jagang rose from his seat and said leisurely,

“When we knew exactly what sort of man you were, do you think my guards would have simply sent me in here?”

His guards had already infiltrated the walls and roof in advance and dismantled the mechanism.

“Damn it!”

An Cheonggwang sprang to his feet. Should he flee through the secret passage? Or should he launch an attack?

Before he could decide what to choose, a flash of light split that momentary gap.

Shuuuuung!

Puaaak!

Blue sword energy that flew like light pierced through his chest.

He could not rise into the air and simply collapsed back into the grand armchair.

Thud.

He looked down at his own chest, now punched wide open, and vomited a gush of blood.

It was far too futile.

When he had first chosen to take Cha Iran’s hand, it had not taken even fifteen minutes. That decision had changed everything.

Life was long, but decisions were always made in a single moment.

What would have happened if he had refused back then?

Baek Jagang turned and walked away as he said,

“Dream your dream in your next life.”

Then An Cheonggwang’s voice came from behind him.

“……That woman once said this.”

Baek Jagang stopped and looked back at that final moment.

From An Cheonggwang’s mouth, words he truly had not expected spilled out along with blood.

“……She said that only if you died would the murim not be destroyed.”

With those words, An Cheonggwang’s head dropped.

For the first time since entering this room, Baek Jagang’s small eyes narrowed to pinpoints and gleamed sharply.

No chill had prickled near his ears as he heard those words.

Which meant she had truly said them.

If I am alive, the murim will be destroyed?

Chapter 704: It Was Hard Keeping My Mouth Shut
----------------------------------------------
Sipchilgyo could tell them apart now.

At first glance, the ten Faceless Guests were impossible to distinguish, but on closer inspection, each had their own individuality. Their heights were different, and so were their builds.

Above all, there were markings on their masks. Small pictures had been drawn along the edges of the masks: some bore tiny foxes, while others had clouds or flames, venomous snakes or spiders.

If those were their individual symbols, then perhaps, she guessed, each of them also had a mask with that image covering the whole thing. A fox mask, for instance, or a flame mask that made the entire mask look as though it were burning.

And on the mask of the Faceless Guest who had saved her, there was a small crescent moon.

As Sipchilgyo stared at the Faceless Guest, her eyes met Ogyo’s. She had been captured together with her, and Ogyo was a senior she respected.

Ogyo looked at her and shook her head slightly.

Sipchilgyo knew what that gesture meant. It meant she should not give away needless personal feelings.

It meant that, both while they had ridden here together in the carriage and even here, she had been looking at him so openly that her inner thoughts might be seen.

Sipchilgyo gave a slight nod.

Yes, what kind of madness was this, in a situation where she might die at any moment? Since it was Ogyo, she would let it pass, but if anyone else found out, she could receive severe punishment for colluding with the enemy.

Just as she resolved not to look at him anymore—

As though fate truly existed, the crescent-moon Faceless Guest, who had been looking out the window, tapped the wall twice and drew everyone’s attention.

It was a signal that someone was coming this way.

Sure enough, a moment later, the door opened and someone stepped inside.

“Is Chief Steward Jo here?”

It was An Hupyeong, the young lord of Shinhwabang. He had heard that the women who had advanced to the main round today were gathering because of the final stage, and had come to look around.

For an instant, An Hupyeong flinched in surprise.

The women were seated in chairs. The thrill of having the gazes of dozens of beautiful women all fixed on him at once!

The problem, however, was the men standing around them. Masked figures stood throughout the guest hall.

Some leaned against the walls, and some crouched in corners. Some stood beside the women with their arms folded. All of them were staring at him. Their appearance alone was enough to send a chill down one’s spine.

At this point, he should have immediately realized that something was wrong.

“Chief Steward Jo is preparing something interesting.”

He had mistaken it for a performance being prepared for the final stage.

“Villains and beauties! Not a bad idea. Who is it? Who’s playing the leader?”

An absurd silence flowed through the hall.

At that moment, Samgyo turned his head and looked at Geukak Soma, and the other assassins followed suit. Samgyo was hoping that his arrival might create some kind of variable in the situation.

An Hupyeong walked fearlessly up to Geukak Soma.

He looked at Geukak Soma and exclaimed in admiration.

“Whoever made it, that mask is quite well done.”

The two eyes behind the mask curved into a broad smile. If anyone there had understood what kind of smile that was, Sacho would never have left in the first place.

Seeing that smile, An Hupyeong even issued a warning.

“These are precious guests, so don’t even think of trying anything foolish.”

As though he had forgotten, he turned back toward the women and revealed his identity.

“Ah, I haven’t introduced myself. I am An Hupyeong, the young lord of Shinhwabang. If anything makes you uncomfortable, come to me at any time and tell me.”

The women merely looked at him, and no one said a word. Even if their mute acupoints had not been sealed, what could they possibly say to this utterly oblivious man?

At the continuing silence, An Hupyeong felt slightly displeased. But soon, he found the reason for this strange atmosphere in himself.

Yes, it would not be easy to speak rashly to the young lord.

After sweeping his gaze over the women one last time, An Hupyeong bid them farewell.

“Well then, practice hard.”

An Hupyeong went outside. No matter how he looked, there were no beauties like Ian or Cha I-ran.

‘Since I’m thinking of it, should I go see her?’

He set off to find Ian.

Then, far in the distance, a figure caught his eye.

An unfamiliar martial artist was walking in front, and Shinhwabang warriors were following behind him. They were trailing after him with their heads lowered, as though they had committed some crime.

“I’ve never seen him before.”

In truth, it was not the first time he had seen him. He was one of the Sado Thirteen Wolves he had seen before when he met Bisa-in.

Because he had only paid attention to Bisa-in and the women that day, he did not remember their faces at all.

At that moment, a martial artist shot out from the building on the opposite side using lightness skill. Seeing An Hupyeong, he shouted urgently.

“Hurry to your father!”

In that instant, a member of the Sado Thirteen Wolves sprang out of the building even faster and subdued him in the blink of an eye.

After that, Shinhwabang warriors filed out of the building one after another with their heads lowered, and another member of the Sado Thirteen Wolves came out last.

Startled, An Hupyeong ran toward the Lord’s Hall.

The Sado Thirteen Wolves martial artist who had subdued the man who shouted merely glanced at An Hupyeong once and did not pursue him.

When An Hupyeong had run all the way to the Lord’s Hall, Bisa-in was standing at the entrance. Behind Bisa-in, the Alliance Lord’s guards were lined up.

“Young Alliance Lord! Please help!”

He ran toward Bisa-in.

“Suspicious people have invaded our sect!”

Before An Hupyeong could even approach Bisa-in, those suspicious people sealed his mute and paralysis acupoints and dragged him away.

Then the door opened, and Baek Jagang emerged from inside the Lord’s Hall.

He walked out with his hands clasped behind his back as though returning from a casual outing, but with him came the thick stench of blood.

The scene inside entered Bisa-in’s eyes.

The lord of Shinhwabang sat dead in the grand preceptor’s chair, and Cheol Hando’s head rested on the table where they had been drinking. Behind that table, Yeom Sageom could also be seen lying as a corpse.

With those corpses at his back, Baek Jagang asked,

“Where is the assassin woman who devised this matter now?”

Bisa-in could guess that the Alliance Lord had heard something important related to her.

“The young cult leader should be meeting her now.”

* * *

When Geom Mugeuk went to find Cha I-ran, she was buying new clothes.

This was the very place where he had once bought clothes with Ian.

“How did you know I was here?”

“Have you forgotten already? When you were drunk and crawling around on the ground, I was beside you.”

Right now, instead of the splendid clothes she had worn earlier, she was wearing an ordinary black martial robe. Was it because it was his first time seeing her in martial attire? If anything, this look seemed fresher and more attractive.

“Fine. Since you’re here, pick one for me.”

Cha I-ran showed him the two outfits she had chosen from the shop.

“Which one do you think would suit me better?”

Both outfits were splendid.

“The martial robe you’re wearing now is fine, though.”

“My martial robe is this outfit. Hurry and choose.”

When Geom Mugeuk pointed to the outfit on the left, she chose the one on the opposite side.

“Because you’ll want Miss Lee to win.”

Geom Mugeuk watched her pay the shopkeeper for the clothes.

‘You won’t be wearing that outfit to the competition.’

After paying, she said to Geom Mugeuk,

“Wait a moment. I’ll change and come out.”

As she was about to go inside, she took something out and placed it on the table.

“Hold on to this for a moment.”

Then she went inside to change.

Geom Mugeuk silently looked at the small box she had left behind.

Wung.

The Bige reacted again. Astonishingly, she had left behind the box containing the bead.

What exactly was she thinking, doing this? Did she really want him to take it?

Last night and now as well, she was blatantly tempting him with the bead.

When Geom Mugeuk lightly waved his hand, the lid of the box opened through remote object manipulation.

Inside was a red bead, the Red Essence.

Wooooong.

The Bige reacted even more strongly.

As he looked at the bead, a powerful temptation arose in him, urging him to absorb its energy at once. The existing energies sleeping within his body tried to awaken.

But Geom Mugeuk waved his hand again, and the lid closed.

There was one thing she did not know.

How difficult it had been for him to obtain the previous beads. This bead was not something that could ever be obtained this easily.

‘So, Bige, let’s endure.’

As Geom Mugeuk calmed his mind, the energy that had been about to awaken fell asleep again, and the Bige also settled down.

A moment later, she came out after changing clothes. Wearing the beautiful outfit, she looked like a completely different person from just before. Instead of asking about the clothes she had changed into, she asked about the box.

“Did you open it?”

“I did.”

“How was it?”

“I wanted it.”

“I wanted it. What sweet words.”

Cha I-ran picked up the box and sat in a chair placed in the corner, crossing her legs. The thigh that was revealed was unbearably sensual.

“Why didn’t you take it?”

“I already did. That box is empty.”

Cha I-ran put it away without checking inside.

“Do you trust my conscience?”

“No, it’s a box that explodes if opened.”

Their gazes became entangled in midair. Their eyes were so deep that by looking like this, neither could ever know what the other was thinking.

“I didn’t know you would come looking for me this early.”

They had drunk all night yesterday, parted in such a drunken state. She had thought she would see him tomorrow at the earliest.

“After you revealed your identity, I suppose my heart felt at ease somehow.”

Of course, that was not why he had come.

The operation had begun. At this very moment, Geukak Soma and the Sado Alliance would each be carrying out their assigned tasks.

What he had to do first was find out whether Ak Gunhak also liked her.

Geom Mugeuk looked after her stomach first.

“Did you have something to cure your hangover?”

“I did.”

“Well done. You have to protect your insides from when you’re young.”

At that, Cha I-ran asked with a strange smile,

“Do you think you or I will live until we grow old?”

“I will live until I’m old.”

“How greedy.”

“That’s right. Greedy for martial arts, greedy for people, greedy for money, greedy for health, greedy for lifespan. I’m the man known as the incarnation of greed.”

Then Cha I-ran said with a laugh,

“I heard the rumors say you’re greedy for words.”

At that, Geom Mugeuk moved his jaw from side to side, loosening his mouth.

“My mouth has been itching so badly all this time I nearly went mad.”

As Cha I-ran talked with Geom Mugeuk, she thought she might come to understand why Ak Gunhak had not killed him.

Apart from her mission, there was one line connecting Geom Mugeuk and herself: Ak Gunhak.

The moment that line snapped, there would be no common topic left between them. Only the matter of whether one killed or was killed, whether the mission was completed or not, would remain.

“Why couldn’t he leave?”

Geom Mugeuk now asked the question he had not asked last night.

“What other reason could there be? The organization wouldn’t let him go.”

“Is there any need to be loyal to an organization like that?”

At that, Cha I-ran raised both eyebrows slightly.

“Loyal? I’m not loyal to anyone. I work and receive payment.”

Her words were sincere. Even in his life before regression, she had died as an assassin until the very end. The fact that her life had only been revealed after her death was surely because of this way of thinking of hers.

The organization had never liked her.

“That sounds as though if I pay the price, I could hire you.”

“If you have the ability to do so.”

“I’ll hire you right now. State your conditions.”

Of course, he had said it knowing she would never accept.

Sure enough, she refused at once.

“If I were the kind of person who abandoned an assignment I had taken and quit midway, I wouldn’t be worth hiring to this extent.”

That was why she would certainly try to carry out the mission she had been given. She would certainly try to kill the Sado Alliance Lord, and perhaps even him.

“Do you know where he was trying to leave for?”

Instead of answering, Cha I-ran asked in return,

“You would know that, wouldn’t you?”

He could tell from her gaze and expression. She truly did not know where he was leaving for. If anything, she was the one curious about it.

“So he didn’t know you liked him.”

If they had truly liked each other, they would certainly have talked about it. No, perhaps he would not have thought of leaving in the first place. Unless they had planned to leave together.

“That’s right. He doesn’t know.”

Cha I-ran did not try to hide that fact. She did not try to use it as a shield to protect her own life.

“Why didn’t you tell him?”

To think she would end up having this kind of conversation with Geom Mugeuk.

“We were both busy. And the organization wouldn’t have liked it.”

But because he was an enemy she had to kill, she could speak more comfortably instead.

“Why would the organization have hated it?”

“Because then there would have been two people saying they were leaving.”

Geom Mugeuk liked her honesty. Her true charm did not come from those smooth thighs.

Cha I-ran asked as though curious,

“Why do you care so much about him? He already left you.”

From Geom Mugeuk came an answer she had not expected at all.

“He was a remarkable man.”

“I admit that.”

“I like remarkable people. When I see people like that, I want to resemble them and learn from them.”

Geom Mugeuk perched on the display stand directly in front of her.

“In that sense, you were remarkable too.”

At her gaze asking what he meant, Geom Mugeuk answered honestly.

“I don’t have the courage to get that drunk in front of an enemy.”

It was a joke, but she did not laugh.

“Why can’t you be honest?”

“What do you mean?”

“The reason you’re paying attention to him isn’t because he’s remarkable. It’s because you want to keep such a powerful master at your side, isn’t it? And since he’s even an enemy, the effect would be doubled, tripled. Am I wrong?”

After staring at Cha I-ran for a moment, Geom Mugeuk suddenly asked,

“Could you keep him at your side?”

Cha I-ran could not answer yes. Because part of why she had liked him was that he had been like the wind.

“From the first time I saw him, he had taken off his shoes. How could you keep at your side a man who walks around barefoot?”

If that was true? If he did not want to possess him, then what reason could there be?

“Then why?”

After a brief pause, Geom Mugeuk said,

“Because I want to let him leave.”

As though those words were sincere, an astonishing proposal followed.

“Let’s help him leave.”

Chapter 705: Isn't That Life Too Boring?
----------------------------------------
Chairan's expression hardened.

Naturally, she assumed Geom Mugeuk was lying. To make Akgunhak leave, she would have to fight them.

"You're saying you'll fight them just to make him leave?"

At her disbelief, Geom Mugeuk asked in return, as if turning the question back on her.

"It seems reason enough to fight."

"It's not enough."

She, who had been sitting, rose from her seat.

"What fool in this world would stake their life just to make someone leave?"

Their gazes tangled in the air.

"Calling someone a fool to their face?"

He spoke as if jesting, but the Young Cult Leader before him was clearly speaking in earnest.

Because she could feel that, Chairan was confused. She could believe it of someone else.

But the other party was the Young Cult Leader of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult. A person who possessed power, martial arts, wealth, and honor—a person who could have the entire murim if they wished.

Such a person would stake their life for someone who is leaving? Asking me to believe those words?

Just then, Ilgyo urgently entered.

She flinched in surprise at the sight of Chairan with Geom Mugeuk. To her, who hesitated to report, Chairan spoke.

"It's fine. If it's something you'd contact me about so urgently, this man must have orchestrated it."

But she hadn't imagined the matter would be this serious. And because it was this kind of content, Ilgyo reported right in front of Geom Mugeuk.

"Except for me and Igyo, everyone has fallen to Geukak Soma and the Faceless Guests."

A silence flowed for a moment.

Chairan quietly closed her eyes.

Though it was a situation where anger should have exploded, she neither displayed rage nor emitted killing intent.

She was demonstrating that silence is sometimes the most powerful expression of emotion.

One could feel her anger being forged in the silence.

She slowly opened her closed eyes.

An incomparably transparent and clear gaze.

"So you're telling me you came here to say these things to me after killing all my subordinates?"

Geom Mugeuk felt immense anger from her words, which remained calm and unchanged.

It was also the first time seeing that side of Chairan as an assassin.

Geom Mugeuk immediately cleared up the misunderstanding.

"They are alive."

It wasn't something he had checked directly, but Geom Mugeuk trusted Geukak Soma and the Faceless Guests.

But Chairan did not believe those words. When she looked at Ilgyo, the remaining report continued.

"Judging by the emergency signals that came from several places, it appears they were attacked simultaneously and taken captive. Currently, their life or death cannot be confirmed."

Since he was not one to invite unnecessary misunderstanding, Geom Mugeuk spoke again emphatically.

"They have not died."

"Where are they now?"

"Lord Soma has them."

Judging by how he had acted until now, Chairan felt that Geom Mugeuk was speaking the truth. At the very least, he was not a person who would lie about such matters.

For now, Chairan sent Ilgyo out.

"Wait outside."

"Yes."

With only the two of them remaining, Chairan asked.

"You could have killed them all. Why did you spare them?"

Geom Mugeuk answered, gazing at her.

"Because of you."

It was an unexpected answer, but Chairan, without wavering, gazed at Geom Mugeuk.

"What does that mean?"

Geom Mugeuk spoke honestly.

"Because I feared that he liked you."

"!"

Only now did she understand why Geom Mugeuk had come faster than she had expected. He had a clear purpose.

"So you came to find out if Akgunhak likes me. Because if we liked each other, you couldn't kill my subordinates."

When Geom Mugeuk nodded, Chairan felt relief that her subordinates might be safe, along with another thought.

If that was really true, then his words about fighting to make Akgunhak leave might also have been sincere.

'You would really stake your life, Young Cult Leader, for that person?'

Is this really possible? To go this far for someone you met as an enemy and parted with?

And the biggest problem at this moment was this.

"Since you've confirmed that he doesn't like me, you'll kill them all now."

"Wouldn't I have to, to stop you?"

The air around the two grew cold, and a taut tension coiled around them.

"Didn't you come here to kill the Martial Lord? Even if the final stage doesn't open, you'll try to kill him somehow."

Chairan admitted readily.

"Because that is my mission."

She was astonishingly frank.

"I cannot sit by and watch that."

Geom Mugeuk slowly gripped the hilt of his demonic sword. Even in a situation where the sword seemed ready to be drawn at any moment, she did not retreat a single step.

"Aren't you afraid of dying?"

At Geom Mugeuk's question, her transparent eyes shone even brighter.

"If you kill people for money and fear dying yourself, isn't that life too boring?"

Geom Mugeuk could feel it.

That to her, life and death were already one.

She did not fear death. Perhaps that was why she could get so drunk and act so rowdy before him.

"What are you doing? Not cutting me down."

Geom Mugeuk took his hand off the hilt.

Then, a captivating smile formed on Chairan's face, which had been nothing but cold until then. Because she changed her emotions in an instant, it was as if a different woman stood before him.

"You can't cut me down because I'm pretty, right?"

She had returned to being the Chairan who seduced.

"Blood shouldn't stain new clothes, should it?"

Geom Mugeuk had no intention of drawing his sword in the first place.

"Kill me when you can. Don't regret it later."

"Couldn't I feel relieved later, thinking I spared you?"

Geom Mugeuk turned around, showing his back.

"Let's go out and walk a bit."

Chairan stared quietly at Geom Mugeuk, who allowed his back to face her in this situation, then followed him out.

The two walked side by side down the street. The scent of dumplings wafting from ahead covered the tension that had existed between them.

"Aren't you hungry?"

"If your subordinates were being held hostage, you wouldn't say such things."

Geom Mugeuk stopped in front of the dumpling cart, but she continued walking.

Geom Mugeuk followed behind her and spoke.

"Even if you don't eat dumplings, let's make him leave."

To compare eating dumplings with such a matter.

"Fine."

"Let's do it together."

"Why do you keep trying to drag me into this?"

"Because you like him."

Chairan stopped walking. Naturally, Geom Mugeuk stopped as well.

"What about him?"

He doesn't even know that I like him.

"If you help him, he'll think something like this: 'Huh? Why is this woman helping me?'"

Since it would be a matter of staking one's life, that surprise would be even greater.

"Then smile first. Not the smile you show me, but your real smile."

'My real smile?' Wondering what he meant, Chairan looked at Geom Mugeuk with puzzled eyes.

"When I said it wasn't that I saved him and sent him away, but that he had saved me and left, you smiled. It was an awkward and stiff smile. Yet that smile suited you so well. Just as that plain martial uniform from earlier suited you better than these clothes."

It was something she had never once thought of. That she had such an awkward smile? That the ordinary martial uniform from earlier suited her better than these clothes?

"You're trying to make me fall at the final stage."

Geom Mugeuk smiled and continued what he was saying.

"You must smile that smile. Because he will realize at once that the captivating smile you give me is fake. While giving your real smile, say this."

She wondered what on earth he was going to say.

"Since this is a connection too, shall we have a cup of tea?"

Chairan let out a hollow laugh, absurd as it was.

But Geom Mugeuk said what he had to say, serious until the very end.

"If he says he dislikes it, take off your shoes and chase after him."

In the end, she spat out,

"Are you crazy?"

It was something said to the Young Cult Leader, and also something Akgunhak would likely say to her if she did such a thing.

"I hear that often."

Geom Mugeuk laughed aloud.

Chairan recalled Geom Mugeuk standing in an alley last night when they parted, smiling as he looked at her.

It seemed this person hadn't just been itching to speak all this time. He smiled so well she wondered how he had endured it.

"The most dangerous people in the murim are those who smile as well as you."

"Then you're in big trouble. This time, I've come with someone who truly smiles well."

Chairan could tell. That he was speaking of Geukak Soma.

"We'll have to see how cheap that smile is as well."

It was a statement that she would check with her own eyes whether her subordinates were safe.

* * *

Despite Ogyo's remark, Sipchilgyo looked again at the Crescent Moon Faceless Guest.

This time too, it was not of her own will.

He, who had been looking out the window, struck the wall again to draw the surroundings' attention.

This time it was different from before. The knocking sound was peculiar.

At that sound, all the Faceless Guests reacted simultaneously. The Faceless Guest who had been crouching in a corner, dozing, sprang up; the Faceless Guest leaning against the wall in the other corner also leaped and came this way. The Faceless Guest tending to weapons moved as if war had broken out.

Thus, all the Faceless Guests who had been doing their own work lined up behind Geukak Soma.

Soon after, the door opened and Geom Mugeuk entered.

"I greet the Young Cult Leader."

At Geukak Soma's greeting, the Faceless Guests also matched their movements and cupped their fists in salute.

Clack-clack-clack-clack-clack-clack!

To the point where one might wonder if they were the same Faceless Guests who had been blankly staring out the window or crouching and dozing, their movements were so disciplined.

Geom Mugeuk also cupped his fist respectfully to Geukak Soma.

"I greet Lord Soma."

Soon after, Chairan entered inside. The assassin women were startled. They wondered if she had finally come to rescue them, for Geom Mugeuk had entered so nonchalantly and exchanged greetings.

At the thought that even she had been captured, everyone was shocked and despaired. They couldn't believe it. That amazing Chairan had been captured.

Then Geom Mugeuk spoke to them.

"Do not misunderstand. It is I who have been captured by this person."

While everyone was puzzled by the incomprehensible words.

Chairan walked over to them.

Her gaze turned toward her subordinates' necks.

Blood lines drawn at the exact same spot, at the exact same length.

Those lines spoke of the skill of the one who drew them in a single stroke.

Because she knew their skill well, the lines were all the more vivid.

Chairan spoke to her subordinates.

"Look. You didn't do anything rash like killing yourselves, just gritted your teeth through it. That's why you're seeing me again, isn't it?"

At those words, the assassins' expressions slightly relaxed. It didn't seem like Chairan had been captured?

Then Geom Mugeuk quickly claimed credit.

"You really brought them without anyone getting hurt. As expected! Truly impressive!"

Of course, there had been three or four assassins injured from resisting until the end, but considering the total number, one could say they had been escorted here with utmost care.

Chairan's gaze turned toward Geukak Soma.

He must have done so on the Young Cult Leader's orders, but she was grateful that they had been subdued and brought here without injury. And by none other than Geukak Soma.

She conveyed her gratitude with her eyes.

She had come saying she wanted to see Geukak Soma's cheap smile, but she had no desire to see his smile. She knew all too well when he smiled.

A decision had to be made.

If she didn't help Geom Mugeuk, they would all die. Even if the Young Cult Leader tried to save them, that Geukak Soma wouldn't just stand by.

To save them, she would have to fight the organization.

As she was worrying, Geom Mugeuk's voice transmission flew in.

—Take your subordinates and go.

The surprised Chairan turned to look at Geom Mugeuk. Geom Mugeuk had a serious gaze, as if it were no joke.

Releasing them without any conditions?

—Are you serious?

The reason was so like Geom Mugeuk as well.

—Indeed. The leader came in person. How could she leave her subordinates behind and go, without losing face?

It meant he would protect her authority.

—I could assassinate the Martial Lord together with the freed subordinates.

—Then most of the subordinates here would die. Regardless of success or failure.

—I would use them as sacrificial pawns to succeed.

Geom Mugeuk nodded.

—Perhaps you might.

But she could feel it. That Geom Mugeuk was certain such a thing would not happen.

—Why do you think I couldn't do it?

—Because if you were such a person, you wouldn't have braved danger to come here to check on your subordinates' lives.

That was evident just from the ambush on the Martial Lord that had been planned. At the celebration the Martial Lord was attending, only she, who had become the most beautiful woman under heaven, could attend.

Because she couldn't understand, she had to ask again.

—Why do you show me such kindness?

—Because I want to look good to you.

—And after you look good to me?

—I have to make him leave.

—You're truly persistent.

—Aren't madmen originally persistent?

Chairan stared into Geom Mugeuk's two eyes. Everyone knew the two were conversing through voice transmission, so they all watched the two without even breathing loudly.

Geom Mugeuk spoke to the Faceless Guests.

"Release their blood points."

Then the Faceless Guests came forward and released their blood points and demonic points.

Chairan didn't say anything else to them.

"Let's go."

The assassins also quietly followed her out. Whatever deal had been made, Chairan had rescued them. Thinking she had probably paid a great price, none of them could show joy.

At the entrance, Chairan turned back to look at Geom Mugeuk.

—You lost out on this matter. I would never have abandoned my subordinates.

It meant she would have listened to any demand. There was no way that sharp-witted Young Cult Leader hadn't known that.

—I have much, so I can bear this much loss.

Geom Mugeuk added with a smile.

—If one takes hostage what the opponent can never abandon and fights, wouldn't that be too boring a life?

Chairan, who had gazed at Geom Mugeuk for a moment, turned wordlessly and left that place.

Once they had all left, Geom Mugeuk apologized to Geukak Soma.

"I'm sorry, even though you went through the trouble of capturing them."

Geukak Soma did not ask why he had released them. He smiled silently at Geom Mugeuk, then looked back at the Faceless Guests standing behind him.

Because they knew the Demon Lord's will, the Faceless Guests all cupped their fists.

Before them, Geukak Soma also cupped his fist respectfully and spoke.

"Just give the order. I will capture them a hundred times over."

Chapter 706: If Killed by the Handsome Young Cult Leader
--------------------------------------------------------
“Warrior.”

At Cheonhwaru Ju’s call, Ian, who had been standing by the door surveying the situation outside, turned her head.

“Rest for a bit.”

Since receiving the mission to protect her, Ian had not let her mind wander for even a single moment. She checked the windows, the ceiling, and the walls. She listened for movement in the hallway beyond the door. She repeated this over and over.
