Ian pressed her ear to the door again and spoke.

“There is a saying passed down among guard warriors. Accidents always happen the one time you turn your head.”

Only after checking the outside did she turn back to Cheonhwaru Ju.

“I’m fine, Ju.”

“Protection is truly no easy task.”

“Thank you for your concern.”

Ian felt at ease when she was with Cheonhwaru Ju.

When she had first met her, she had been suffering from side effects in her body. Because Cheonhwaru Ju had looked upon her with such warmth since then, seeing her felt like being with an older sister or a friend.

That was why she guarded her all the more carefully. She could not endure the thought of losing her to that one moment of turning her head.

“Don’t worry. I am, after all, originally from the guard of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult’s Young Cult Leader….”

The next moment!

Ian quickly turned and drew her sword.

Swhiiiiick!

Her sword flashed like lightning and split the air.

Srrrk, thud! Thud-thud!

The door was cut in a slanted cross shape and collapsed.

There was no one behind the collapsed door.

“Come out.”

At Ian’s single word, something leaped out from the wall beside the door.

Swish-thwack.

What unfurled as it emerged was a fan painted with a beauty. As if waving a flag of surrender, it fluttered lightly.

The one who poked his face in was none other than Hwado Myeong. He greeted Ian as cheerfully as if greeting an old acquaintance.

“Warrior, congratulations on advancing to the finals!”

His makeup was so thick as to be ridiculous that it was impossible to tell what kind of face he had or what expression he was making.

But there was one thing she knew.

*He is no ordinary expert.*

When the door was cut, he had definitely been standing behind it. He had avoided her attack in that split second.

The mere fact that she could not gauge his strength meant he was by no means inferior to her.

“Of course, you all overturned everything and flipped the tournament upside down in the process.”

Hwado Myeong examined the cross-section of the door lying severed on the floor.

“To have reached such a realm at that age.”

Only then did he fan himself lightly and step inside.

Ian focused all her nerves on her sword and her opponent as she slowly retreated.

“With such looks and such skill to boot. You would have every right to be arrogant. Truly impressive!”

He folded the unfurled fan and struck his palm with it repeatedly as if clapping.

Ian took something from within her clothes and placed it in her mouth. It was a pidokju. Those who used fans sometimes folded and unfolded them while releasing poison, so she had prepared for that.

In moments like these, one did not first look after the person they were meant to protect. If she fell, Cheonhwaru Ju would certainly fall as well, so she had to bite the pidokju herself first.

Only then did she send a signal behind her back with her free hand to Cheonhwaru Ju. Because the opponent might take this moment to launch a surprise attack, she used a hand signal rather than words.

At that, Cheonhwaru Ju placed in her mouth the pidokju Ian had prepared in advance.

At that sight, Hwado Myeong waved his fan left and right and shook his hand.

“Don’t worry. I don’t use poison. I’m not a meticulous person by nature. I’m so clumsy I’d end up poisoning myself with my own poison.”

Ian, who had been listening, asked tersely.

“Who are you?”

Since the opponent was observing manners, Ian also asked with basic courtesy.

“Let’s say I’m an errand boy for today. A congratulatory guest works too.”

“You’ve already congratulated me, so state your business.”

“Before that, let’s put down the sword. My stomach churns just looking at a blade.”

But Ian did not lower her sword. She wished she could act as leisurely as her opponent, but he was not someone she could let her guard down against.

Just then, Cheonhwaru Ju spoke calmly from behind.

“Were you the one who called me here?”

She was asking if he had summoned her as a tournament judge. Somehow, she had a premonition that this strange man had come looking for her.

Her guess was indeed correct.

“You could say it’s something like that.”

Cheonhwaru Ju, who had been watching him, walked to the table in the center and sat down first.

“Please sit.”

Cheonhwaru Ju looked at Ian and said.

“It’s all right, Warrior.”

Under normal circumstances, she should have prevented this unidentified man from approaching Cheonhwaru Ju, but she judged that Cheonhwaru Ju was acting boldly because she had something in mind.

Ian stepped back and allowed Hwado Myeong to sit.

Hwado Myeong, now seated, stared at Cheonhwaru Ju and asked.

“You can see things that we cannot, can you not?”

Cheonhwaru Ju gave no answer and only looked at him.

“Do you see anything regarding me?”

Cheonhwaru Ju, who had been staring at him intently, shook her head.

“I see nothing.”

Whether that was true or not was something only she could know.

This time, Cheonhwaru Ju asked.

“Why did you seek me?”

“I have words to deliver.”

“Go on.”

Within the thick makeup, his two eyes chilled and sank. Though only the light in his eyes had changed, the man who had seemed so lighthearted just moments ago now felt like a completely different person.

Swish.

Hwado Myeong unfurled the fan and completely covered his face. From behind it, a voice unlike his original one flowed out—cold and sinister.

It was such a natural voice that one might think this was his true voice, not an imitation.

“The Sado Maengju must die. If this fate is defied, the martial world will be destroyed.”

In that instant, Cheonhwaru Ju’s eyes opened wide in shock. Ian, who stood between them listening, was also greatly shocked.

The moment that astonishing message ended.

The fan fluttered lightly.

Srrrrk.

His figure suddenly vanished as if a ghost had disappeared.

Ian raised her qi and surveyed her surroundings, but Hwado Myeong had already completely vanished.

“Are you all right?”

At Ian’s question, Cheonhwaru Ju nodded and rose from her seat.

“I must see the Young Cult Leader immediately.”

* * *

“The Sado Maengju must die?”

Geom Mugeuk, who had heard the story, looked at Geugak Soma standing beside him. Geugak Soma also received that gaze with a surprised expression.

What followed was an even more astonishing story.

“He said that if that fate is defied, the martial world will be destroyed.”

The one who relayed the message was Ian. Cheonhwaru Ju was lost in deep thought for a moment.

“What kind of person was he?”

At Geom Mugeuk’s question, Ian described him. He was so distinctive that describing him was easy.

“He was a man wearing thick makeup and flashy clothes adorned with many accessories. He held a fan painted with a beauty, and his stealth and movement techniques were extraordinary. I could not tell how great an expert he was.”

Geom Mugeuk looked at Geugak Soma. At the glance asking if he knew the person, Geugak Soma shook his head.

He had been someone Geom Mugeuk did not know in his life before regression. Meaning he was at least not another of the Twelve Zodiac Kings.

Not knowing someone with such skill and characteristics meant he was a figure who had not been active on the front lines of the martial world. Or else he had died before the era of the Twelve Zodiac Kings.

In any case, one thing was certain.

“The reason they are targeting the Sado Maengju seems related to the words that man delivered.”

As if in agreement, Geugak Soma and Ian nodded.

Just then, the silent Cheonhwaru Ju opened her mouth.

“What he said was a prophecy.”

An astonishing fact was revealed from her.

“The moment I heard it, I knew. That those words were a prophecy.”

There must have been something only she could feel.

If so, then the question that naturally arose.

“In this generation, besides you, is there anyone else who can make prophecies?”

At Geom Mugeuk’s question, Cheonhwaru Ju nodded.

“The Palace Lord was capable of delivering prophecies. I left the Palace long ago, so I do not know who can make prophecies now.”

Geom Mugeuk could guess from her words. That she had been the one to become the next Palace Lord.

She had refused the destiny bestowed upon her and left the Palace.

From that, there was one more fact that could be naturally known.

The reason Cheonhwaru Ju had been invited as a judge to this tournament was precisely related to the prophecy the man had delivered.

Cheonhwaru Ju looked at Geom Mugeuk with worried eyes.

Her heart was heavy because she knew that Geom Mugeuk and the Sado Maengju maintained a good relationship.

If the man’s words were truly a prophecy as she felt, then that prophecy too would come to pass.

Though he read her anxiety, Geom Mugeuk gave an easy smile.

“It’s all right. It is not that I do not believe in your prophecy, but I believe I can definitely change it. Otherwise, wouldn’t I be fated to have my chest pierced by Soma’s dagger?”

Geom Mugeuk sensed her lingering worry behind the smile Cheonhwaru Ju forced.

Yes, until now, that prophecy must have come true. That was why she was so worried.

But Geom Mugeuk also had something he believed in.

If a prophecy showed the path, it was humans who walked that path.

*I will definitely find another path!*

Geom Mugeuk left the room to meet someone.

“I must see the Sado Maengju.”

* * *

An estate not far from Sinhwabang.

Assassins of the Gain Cult stood guard all around.

Chairan was alone in the room.

She sat, silently staring at the box containing a pearl on the table.

The words Geom Mugeuk had spoken came to her mind.

—The chief came personally. How could I leave my subordinates behind and lose face?

In the gazes of her subordinates looking at her, her dignity was certainly restored. One might ask what great meaning that held, but it clearly had meaning. Since she had become the chief who led them out of Geugak Soma’s den.

—If you fight by taking hostage what the opponent can never give up, wouldn’t that be too pathetic a life?

She recalled Geom Mugeuk’s words as she fiddled with the box.

Just as her thoughts deepened, someone’s voice was heard.

“Aren’t assassons supposed to be heartless?”

Hwado Myeong was standing in front of the door, having entered unnoticed.

“Assassins are supposed to be heartless by nature. Emotional assassins have a way of dying early.”

Hwado Myeong walked over and sat facing her.

“What did the Young Cult Leader demand in exchange for releasing your subordinates?”

Chairan continued to look down at the box as she spoke.

“He told me to make sure to kill any rude person who enters a room without the master’s permission.”

Hwado Myeong unfurled his fan and covered his face.

“Don’t forget I’m a spectator, not an enemy.”

The moment he appeared before Cheonhwaru Ju, he had become an actor on stage rather than a spectator. Yet before Chairan, he still called himself a spectator.

“I asked what demands were made while releasing your subordinates?”

When she still did not answer, Hwado Myeong guessed.

“He asked for the pearl, didn’t he? Or? To give up killing the Sado Maengju? Or else to tell him who is above you?”

Still keeping her eyes on the box, Chairan answered.

“He made no demands at all.”

Hwado Myeong could feel from her expression that she spoke the truth. Derision overtook surprise.

“He’s toying with you. The Young Cult Leader is an arrogant man.”

“Shouldn’t I be grateful he’s bothering to toy with me?”

“What?”

“If he weren’t, we’d all be dead. Back there.”

Silence fell for a moment, and Hwado Myeong fanned her as if flattering her.

“You aren’t someone who dies easily, are you?”

“The moment the Young Cult Leader, who had been spouting nonsense, put his hand on his sword hilt, I thought, ‘Ah, I might die.’”

“Were you scared?”

She only stared at the box and shook her head.

“If I die to a handsome Young Cult Leader, will a handsome evil spirit push me into a fire pit even in hell?”

At her words, Hwado Myeong laughed and spoke.

“Then have you kill me.”

Then Chairan raised her head and looked at Hwado Myeong.

“Not now.”

Hwado Myeong avoided her gaze, turned his body to sit sideways, and fanned himself lightly.

Then, without looking at her, he suddenly asked.

“When will you do it?”

From Chairan’s mouth flowed the time that would change everyone’s fate.

“Tomorrow.”

Hwado Myeong rose from his seat.

“If I want to enjoy a good spectacle tomorrow, I should sleep early today.”

Then Chairan asked.

“Where is that person, Akgunhak, right now?”

Hwado Myeong’s eyes sank.

“Why do you ask?”

“I thought I might confess once before dying.”

“Who before dying? You? Or Akgunhak?”

“In your view, who looks like they’ll die faster?”

Silence flowed between the two.

Eventually, Hwado Myeong turned and spoke.

“Confessions don’t suit you. An assassin who falls in love becomes miserable.”

The moment Chairan bit her red lip slightly.

Their gazes turned toward one wall at the same time.

An immense presence felt from far beyond the wall.

The next moment.

Swish.

Unfurling his fan, Hwado Myeong vanished in an instant.

Chairan retrieved the pearl box on the table. Then, turning her back to the wall, she raised her protective astral barrier.

Kwaaaaang!

Along with an ear-splitting explosion, one wall was blown away entirely. An enormous astral energy raged there.

Kwaaaaaaaa!

Everything was swept away by the astral energy.

When the rising dust settled.

The room she had been in had been blown away entirely, and nothing remained.

Only Chairan, standing with her back turned, stood there alone.

“It was a new outfit, and now I’ve lost it all again.”

She turned around slowly.

In the distance, a man in black martial attire was walking this way.

He was none other than Sado Maengju Baek Jagang.

It was not a surprise attack. He had announced his arrival beforehand with his momentum, so this was his greeting.

Ilgyo flew in leading the assassins and blocked his path.

Baek Jagang warned in a low voice.

“That child went to the trouble of saving you. Don’t make me kill you with my own hands.”

Chairan could feel it. Baek Jagang was determined to kill everyone who stood in his way.

Chairan gave Ilgyo an order.

“Stand down.”

Ilgyo hesitated for an instant but soon accepted the order. The Sado Maengju was not an opponent he and his subordinates could stop.

Baek Jagang strode up to where she was.

“I will ask you now.”

Baek Jagang’s sharp gaze pierced into her.

“I will not ask you twice.”

Chapter 707: Will this be of help?
----------------------------------
“Welcome, Alliance Leader.”

After greeting him respectfully, Cha Iran looked around at the ruins.

“I’d like to invite you to sit, but as you can see, there’s nowhere to sit.”

Even with her life in danger, she was composed. Having erased the signpost that marked the crossroads between life and death, she had no fear.

The day her steps halted while walking the path she wished to walk—that would be her last day.

“Are you not afraid of dying?”

To Baek Jagang’s question, asked like a death god, Cha Iran answered politely.

“I am afraid.”

At that moment, a chill prickled at Baek Jagang’s ear. She was someone upon whom his sense for detecting lies worked. The fact that it reacted to her saying she was afraid meant she was lying.

She did not fear death?

Ordinarily, he would have thought this woman had a hidden trump card, or that she was unafraid because someone was there to save her.

But Baek Jagang had seen it. In that fleeting instant, the detachment that had passed through her eyes.

Among those he had met before, there had been people with eyes like that.

Eyes he had seen in those for whom the boundary between life and death had collapsed.

They were people who, having abandoned everything, could do anything.

It was unexpected. With such a beautiful and splendid appearance, she had no lingering attachment to life?

Just what kind of life had she lived?

A thought suddenly occurred to Baek Jagang.

Could the reason Geom Mugeuk was trying to resolve this without killing her be because he, too, had seen what Baek Jagang had just seen?

“I know the Young Cult Leader regards you as special. But that is a matter between you and him. It has nothing to do with me.”

After making it clear that he had no intention of showing mercy because of her relationship with Geom Mugeuk, Baek Jagang asked her directly.

“Why are you trying to kill me?”

His earlier warning that he would not ask twice meant that if she did not answer when asked once, he would kill her.

“As you must already know, I am an assassin. So I accepted a commission.”

Though she belonged to the organization behind this, strictly speaking, it was not a superior-subordinate relationship in which she received orders.

The organization had paid her a commission and entrusted her with the task. She had tried to keep even one step more distance from them. Of course, she knew the organization found that displeasing.

“You accepted the commission even knowing that I was the target.”

It was a sensitive point that could provoke the greatest anger, but Cha Iran charged straight through it.

“How could I refuse? For an assassin, it was the commission of a lifetime—one that might not come even once.”

At her honesty, his questions continued without hesitation.

“What did you mean by what you said to the Leader of Sinhwa Gang?”

In truth, the reason Baek Jagang had come to see her today was to ask this question. As the Leader of Sinhwa Gang was dying, he had left behind these words.

“He said you told him that if I did not die, murim would be destroyed.”

When she had said that, she had not known events would unfold like this. At the time, she had not expected Geom Mugeuk to intervene.

In truth, the person who had said those words to her was Hwa Domyeong.

That hateful man was probably hiding far away, watching this situation unfold.

If Baek Jagang tried to kill her, would he simply watch then as well? Or would he step forward to save her?

“I think that is something I should be asking you.”

Baek Jagang’s gaze sharpened, but she calmly said what she had to say.

“Do you intend to destroy murim?”

A brief silence flowed.

Baek Jagang truly had not expected to receive this question from this woman.

Perhaps that was why. Baek Jagang accepted her provocation.

“And if I do?”

She had not asked that question in order to provoke Baek Jagang. Rather, it was the opposite.

“If you were such a person, you would not have come to me and asked like this.”

Cha Iran continued calmly.

“I suppose they said that to persuade me. There can hardly be a better justification for killing someone.”

Finally, Baek Jagang asked the decisive question.

“Even so, do you intend to kill me?”

This answer did not come immediately.

After a moment, she replied.

“Yes. I have already accepted the commission.”

Because no chill prickled at his ear, he knew she was speaking sincerely. Her courage was admirable, but it was not a reason for forgiveness.

“Tell me why I should not kill you.”

Cha Iran sighed and said,

“I don’t think there is one.”

The death god who had only asked questions all this time finally delivered his verdict.

“Then die!”

His hand grasped the hilt of his sword.

As Cha Iran watched him, she recalled the moment Geom Mugeuk had been about to draw his sword. She felt even greater tension than then. Geom Mugeuk had not drawn his, but this Baek Jagang would certainly draw. And he would certainly kill her.

Cha Iran raised her internal energy. Could she really defeat the Alliance Leader of the Evil Dao in a head-on fight? Impossible.

‘Hwa Domyeong, are you just going to keep watching? If I die, this matter fails too.’

But Hwa Domyeong did not appear.

Right. You were only a spectator.

She sensed that her final moment had come. In truth, she was not afraid of death. Rather, it was a moment she had been waiting for since some time ago.

The Alliance Leader of the Evil Dao, her final target.

There could be no one better to deliver her end.

The only regret was that all her subordinates would die as well.

“My subordinates…”

Before the words asking him to spare them could come out, Baek Jagang cut her off.

“I am not a man who leaves future troubles behind. And yet you think I would leave assassins as future troubles?”

A vast energy flowed from Baek Jagang’s body and spread in every direction.

She had never felt such a powerful presence before. It was as if a colossal beast that had been bound was now unleashed, roaring toward the heavens.

Facing that presence, Cha Iran drew her internal energy to its limit. She did not fear death, but neither was she a woman who gave up on life easily.

She drew a dagger. The dagger gleaming keenly resembled the look in her eyes.

Ilgyo and the other assassins, who had been watching from afar, all launched themselves at once and landed around the two of them. Each of them drew their hidden weapons.

At Baek Jagang’s powerful presence, all of them sensed death, but not one woman fled in fear.

Shrrrng.

It was just as Baek Jagang’s sword was being drawn.

Whoooosh.

Someone who had flown in as fast as light landed between the two of them.

The one who had arrived with Swift Steps was none other than Geom Mugeuk.

At his appearance, Cha Iran was startled. She had not expected Geom Mugeuk to appear at this moment. Not only she, but the assassins around her were also surprised.

Baek Jagang’s sword had not yet been fully drawn.

“Fortunately, I made it before you drew your sword all the way!”

At Geom Mugeuk’s relief, Baek Jagang asked,

“Did you come running here so breathlessly because you thought I might kill this woman?”

Geom Mugeuk let out a sigh of relief and answered briefly.

“Yes.”

It was only a single word, but a faint surge of emotion passed over Cha Iran’s face. Even Hwa Domyeong, who called himself her friend, had not stepped forward, yet Geom Mugeuk, her enemy, had done so for her sake.

Geom Mugeuk looked at Baek Jagang. In those small eyes of Baek Jagang’s, Geom Mugeuk read a certain intent.

—Surely not? Did you know I was watching?

At Geom Mugeuk’s sound transmission, one corner of Baek Jagang’s mouth lifted slightly. That meaningful expression seemed to say:

As expected, you understand my intent.

Geom Mugeuk could now be certain. Baek Jagang had noticed that he was watching from afar and had deliberately drawn his sword.

From the beginning, Baek Jagang likely had no intention of killing her. Originally, he had probably meant to ask what he was curious about and simply leave, but the reason he had tried to draw his sword like this was…

To make her owe Geom Mugeuk her life.

He had even made all her subordinates step forward so that Geom Mugeuk could save their lives as well.

Yes, he was not such a reckless man as to thoughtlessly interfere and kill her when Geom Mugeuk had taken charge of Cha Iran. Even if he were to kill her, he was the kind of man who would do it in Geom Mugeuk’s presence.

—Thank you very much, Alliance Leader.

Baek Jagang also answered through sound transmission. As expected, Geom Mugeuk’s guess had been correct.

—Will this be of help?

—Yes. Enough to change the entire board.

—Then that is enough.

Now, not through sound transmission, but in a loud voice for everyone to hear, he pleaded with Baek Jagang.

“For my sake, please forgive her just this once.”

“A woman who says she will kill me?”

“Please endure it just this once, on account of my face.”

Baek Jagang seemed to ponder for a moment. Then he slowly released the hilt of his sword.

“I will consider the debt I owed you repaid with this.”

In truth, saying it like this was an even more certain way to make her indebted.

“Yes. Thank you for your magnanimity.”

Geom Mugeuk bowed his head and sent Baek Jagang a sound transmission.

—You are truly magnificent!

—More than your father?

—……

—Save the word magnificent for then.

After reminding him once again who his rival in his heart was, Baek Jagang strode away from the place. As if saying that it was now Geom Mugeuk’s matter, he did not spare Cha Iran or the assassins even a single glance.

Geom Mugeuk silently watched his back.

Today, for some reason, his black martial robes looked even more impressive. Cha Iran was an assassin who was trying to kill him. Yet Baek Jagang, who disliked leaving future troubles behind, had yielded this much.

‘I will be sure to repay this grace.’

Once Baek Jagang had completely left, the taut tension that had hung among the assassins loosened. Earlier, when they had been captured, they had thought they would die at the hands of the Extreme Evil Little Demon; this time, they had nearly died at the hands of the Alliance Leader of the Evil Dao.

When Cha Iran gave an order with her eyes, Ilgyo led the assassins and withdrew far away. Before retreating, they glanced at Geom Mugeuk.

It was not what Geom Mugeuk had intended, but thanks to the Alliance Leader of the Evil Dao, he had become the benefactor who saved their lives.

Cha Iran looked at Geom Mugeuk with incomprehension in her eyes.

“Why did you save me? If I died, everything would be neatly settled. Without even needing to stain your own hands with blood.”

Only then did Geom Mugeuk look at Cha Iran. She was standing there covered in dust.

“In the end, you ended up wearing the clothes I chose for you.”

At the shop, she had chosen clothes other than the ones Geom Mugeuk had picked out, and now that her clothes were ruined, he was joking that she would have to buy and wear the ones he had chosen after all.

Cha Iran’s eyes trembled slightly. Even knowing that she was trying to kill him, he could still do this much?

“Whew, all this dust.”

Geom Mugeuk waved his hand through the dust that had risen around them as their auras and killing intent collided.

“Let us walk a little.”

The ruined place was not suitable for conversation, so the two of them slowly moved their steps toward the rear garden. There was a small flower garden there.

“You must have been very tense. Sit for a bit.”

Geom Mugeuk sat first on a broad rock placed in front of the flower garden. Cha Iran sat beside him, side by side.

For a while, the two of them looked at the flowers. As if the urgency from moments ago had been nothing at all, the flowers swayed in the wind, indifferent to the affairs of the world.

“By the way, there is someone I want to ask about.”

“Who?”

“A man with makeup on his face appeared before the Lord of Cheonhwa Pavilion.”

For an instant, Cha Iran’s expression stiffened slightly. She had not known that fact either.

“He came and told him that if the Alliance Leader of the Evil Dao was not killed, murim would be destroyed.”

Hwa Domyeong had done that, yet when he had met her, he had not said such a thing. He was clearly hiding some scheme.

She had not told Baek Jagang, but she told Geom Mugeuk about him. Though he demeaned himself as a mere spectator every time.

“He is an important person in the organization.”

Because of that, one more sentence was added.

“It would be better not to touch him.”

Geom Mugeuk glanced at her and said,

“Then does it seem safe to touch me?”

At Geom Mugeuk’s abyss-deep gaze, Cha Iran could say nothing.

But soon, Geom Mugeuk smiled as if it had been a joke and said,

“A joke, a joke. I will take your advice to heart. I should avoid frightening fellows ahead of time. I am not the sort of person who pokes around in grass where a snake is hiding with a stick. I also dislike people who stir things up for no reason and cause trouble.”

Cha Iran asked again.

“You still have not answered. Why did you save me?”

“I told you, didn’t I? I like impressive people.”

Cha Iran snorted. She had already guessed the reason.

“Because if I am alive, the chances that I can make that person leave become greater.”

As if she thought this was her chance, Cha Iran spoke her honest feelings.

“It does not feel good. It makes me feel as though I am just a tool or a sacrifice for that person.”

Then unexpected words flowed from Geom Mugeuk.

“Are you not living as a tool and a sacrifice right now?”

“!”

Cha Iran’s expression stiffened slightly, struck where it hurt. Geom Mugeuk paid no mind to her reaction and continued what he had been about to say.

“If you have to be one anyway, would it not be better to be a tool and a sacrifice for someone you like?”

“What do you know about me?”

“I know enough. You have bad drinking habits. And you do not even know what clothes suit you.”

In the end, Cha Iran laughed. In all the world, only this Young Cult Leader could make a person laugh while saying such unpleasant words as tool and sacrifice.

“And you cherish your subordinates tremendously.”

“Why do you think I cherish my subordinates?”

There was a definite reason he thought so.

“Because I heard your subordinates do not take their own lives even if they fail in an assassination and are captured.”

She truly thought the Young Cult Leader was clever. This opponent really missed nothing.

And beyond that,

“Does that principle of not taking one’s own life apply even if you fail?”

He stabbed at a point she had not expected at all.

“And if it does?”

“Let us make a promise.”

The promise Geom Mugeuk was about to propose was truly something she had never imagined.

“If I stop your assassination, accept my commission.”

“!”

It was a request for her to leave them and join hands with him. It was different from the proposal to help make him leave together. Because it was saying he would give her a chance even after she failed her assassination.

Like this, Geom Mugeuk came one step closer to her.

“Are you saying you will spare the person who tried to kill you just now?”

“I am.”

“Are you an idiot? Or do you think I am enough of an idiot to believe that?”

But she could feel it. Geom Mugeuk was saying it sincerely.

That Geom Mugeuk really seemed as if, if she failed in her assassination, he would smile brightly and say this to her:

Now then, as promised, let us join hands!

Without any lingering resentment at all.

“What if I succeed in the assassination?”

Geom Mugeuk lifted his head and looked up at the sky.

The clouds continued as if about to scatter, slowly moving wherever the wind led them.

As if this, too, was sincere, Geom Mugeuk answered calmly.

“I will go to hell first and wait for you.”

Chapter 708: Are You the Assassin from Back Then?
-------------------------------------------------
“Aren’t you afraid of death?”

Cha Iran asked Geom Mugeuk the very same question the Sado Alliance Leader had asked her.

Because his words about waiting for her in hell had sounded sincere.

Geom Mugeuk shook his head.

“I am afraid.”

She had thought someone on the level of the young cult leader of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult would answer that he wasn’t afraid of something like death.

But as if he had meant it, Geom Mugeuk added,

“I’m a man with anxiety. The sort who worries in advance about every possible thing. How could someone like that not fear death? I fear my own death, and I fear the deaths of those around me even more. You asked me earlier if I was a fool, didn’t you? I am. Even though I know perfectly well that the more bonds I form, the more worries I’ll have, I keep increasing the number of people who must not die.”

As Geom Mugeuk stared straight at her, Cha Iran asked,

“Am I included among those people who must not die?”

To her surprise, Geom Mugeuk nodded.

“What if that man looks at me later and says this? Hey, you’re supposed to be the young cult leader of the Demonic Cult, and you couldn’t even save one woman who liked me? What the hell were you doing? Then I’d be too ashamed to raise my head. I wouldn’t be able to act superior in front of him ever again.”

It was such a simple reason that, if anything, it struck home.

“There’s that kind of loyalty between men. A friend’s woman is someone you protect. Though in your case, you’re only half his woman.”

Cha Iran let out a small laugh at the words “half his woman.” He meant it that way because only she liked Ak Gunhak. But still, half his woman?

“There’s a reason men in this murim die young.”

Though she said that, Cha Iran could feel it. This young cult leader sitting beside her was not such a simple person.

This time, Geom Mugeuk asked,

“What are you thinking so hard about?”

Looking at the flowers in the garden, Cha Iran answered,

“I’m thinking about whether you’re a fool or a madman.”

Geom Mugeuk laughed aloud. Was it because she thought he had saved her life? He could clearly feel her heart wavering.

“How much time do we have?”

“What time?”

“Until the assassination.”

The assassination was set for tomorrow. Only a few sichen remained until midnight.

“Why do you ask?”

“If we have time left, let’s at least have dinner together one last time.”

“So the madman part is certain, then.”

A gust of wind blew in, brushing past the two of them and the flower garden they were gazing at.

The sunset carried on the wind spread through the garden. The flowers bestowed a new-colored beauty upon the two watching them.

Looking at Geom Mugeuk admiring the sight, Cha Iran thought inwardly.

It should have been the opposite. He should have been staring spellbound not at the flowers, but at her face, and falling for every word she said.

Yet the one being dragged along was her.

“Let’s go eat. It’s my request! They say even the wishes of the dead are granted, so can’t you grant the wish of the living? And am I just any living person? I’m your savior, no less. Do you think I wasn’t afraid of the Sado Alliance Leader? And is that all? When you were drunk and vomiting and crawling around…”

As if she could not bear to listen any longer, she sprang up from her seat and said,

“Wait while I go change.”

* * *

Several splendid garments hung in the wardrobe.

As Cha Iran was choosing what to wear, her gaze moved to the corner of the wardrobe. A black martial uniform was folded there.

After hesitating for a moment, Cha Iran picked up the uniform and said,

“How long are you going to keep hiding?”

Then Hwa Domyeong appeared behind her.

“Wouldn’t that red court dress hanging on the left be nice?”

But Cha Iran shut the wardrobe with a snap.

“Are you angry?”

“Angry? Hardly. I’ve known for a long time that you were a coward.”

“You’re angry.”

“I’m going to change. Turn around.”

Hwa Domyeong turned his back, and Cha Iran began changing.

“The Sado Alliance Leader wouldn’t have killed you. The Sado Alliance owes the young cult leader a great deal. A man as clear about debts and grudges as he is would not have killed you recklessly.”

Cha Iran changed clothes without a word.

“This plan was all calculated. So don’t misunderstand and think I abandoned you.”

“I didn’t say anything.”

“You’re angry right now!”

Yes, she was angry. But the reason for that anger was not Hwa Domyeong. Nor was it because he had not stepped forward to help.

The reason she was angry was…

Once she had finished changing, she even tucked the box containing the bead into her robes.

“I’ll be back.”

At that moment, Hwa Domyeong said,

“Don’t go.”

Without turning around, Hwa Domyeong spoke to her.

“You’ll be persuaded by the young cult leader. Just like Ak Gunhak was.”

Cha Iran gave no answer.

Click.

The sound of the door opening came.

When Hwa Domyeong turned around, Cha Iran had already left the room.

He did not leave the room right away.

Because of his heavy makeup, it was impossible to know exactly what emotion lay across his face, but at the very least, it was neither regret nor anger.

Fwoosh.

He sat with his feet propped on the table, gently fanning himself as he gazed at the setting sun outside the window.

* * *

While Cha Iran changed, Geom Mugeuk waited there.

Then someone cautiously approached him. The person who came after agonizing a hundred times over whether to come or not was none other than Sipchilgyo.

“Young Cult Leader, I have a request.”

“Speak.”

Earlier, when they had faced off against the Sado Alliance Leader, Sipchilgyo had been prepared to die.

In that moment, she had felt only one regret.

She regretted that she would die without being able to tell the crescent-moon Faceless Guest who had saved her that she was grateful. Truly, to her own surprise, he had come to mind then.

That was why she had mustered her courage like this.

“Among the Faceless Guests, please tell the one with the crescent moon design that I am grateful he saved my life, and give him this dagger. I felt that if I didn’t say it today, I might never get to tell him.”

Sipchilgyo respectfully offered a dagger. It was meant as a token of gratitude for the Faceless Guest.

Geom Mugeuk immediately returned the dagger to her.

Realizing her mistake, Sipchilgyo quickly lowered her head and apologized.

“I was rude to an honored person.”

Come to think of it, this was asking the young cult leader to run an errand for her.

“I must be insane.”

For a moment, she had lost her senses. All she had thought was that she could not let this chance slip by. In her urgency to have the message delivered before the young cult leader left, she had committed such a grave mistake.

She had even asked the young cult leader directly without receiving Cha Iran’s permission. She would have nothing to say even if she were killed.

Then Geom Mugeuk’s voice came.

“Tell him yourself later.”

When Sipchilgyo raised her head in surprise, Geom Mugeuk smiled and said,

“Once this matter is all over, I’ll give you a chance to meet him in person. Give it to him yourself then, and say it yourself.”

Sipchilgyo lowered her head in relief.

“Thank you, Young Cult Leader.”

She ran back, soothing her startled heart. As she went, she regretted that she should have thanked him more properly. That was how flustered she was.

Soon after, Cha Iran appeared there.

As she came, she had heard the conversation between the two.

“Will you really let them meet?”

“I will.”

At her expression of incomprehension, Geom Mugeuk explained why.

“When else in life would one ever get to meet a Faceless Guest separately through the young cult leader’s arrangements? And when else would a Faceless Guest ever receive thanks from an assassin? With just a single word from me, I can give them an experience others may never have in their entire lives. So is there any reason not to let them meet?”

Cha Iran said nothing. Because it was a heart and a way of thinking she did not possess. If she had been Geom Mugeuk, she thought the result would have depended on her mood in that moment.

How dare you ask me to deliver a message? She might have grown angry. Or she might have said she would deliver it and then forgotten. Yes, perhaps she might even have passed it along in a good mood.

But to let the two meet? Yes, that was something that did not exist in her world.

“And who knows what happens between people? Those two might fall for each other and elope in the night, and one day, while walking down the road, we might meet them again as they walk holding a child’s hand. Since they are your people and mine, when we wave to them, they will not kill themselves. They will smile.”

“!”

That was something that did not exist even in the dreams within her world.

But because she knew these words were sincere, dealing with Geom Mugeuk was not easy.

“If they’re alive until then.”

“They will be alive. We will, and so will they. That’s what we’ve been struggling so hard for all this time.”

He was not merely speaking. The fact that Geukak Soma had spared all the assassins of the Gain Sect would also be included in what he had just called struggling.

“I’ll pay for today’s meal. Since you saved my life.”

“Buy me something expensive. Maybe because I was tense, I want meat!”

She walked out first, and Geom Mugeuk followed after her.

“That outfit suits you.”

Cha Iran quickened her pace even more and answered,

“I simply had nothing suitable to wear right now.”

* * *

The two ate dinner together.

At least while they were eating, they did not discuss anything serious. They talked about the weather and the seasons they liked. They even debated over the dishes that came out, saying that there was a place that made this dish exceptionally well, or no, another place was tastier.

Like that, the two finished their meal.

“Thanks to you, I ate well.”

“I’m glad.”

She, too, had eaten what felt like a proper meal for the first time in a very long while. She could not even remember when she had last eaten and conversed with someone without such tension.

“Now then, has your mind changed?”

Cha Iran gazed quietly at Geom Mugeuk and asked in return,

“Did you put some medicine in the food that changes a person’s heart?”

“I did. The effects should start showing soon.”

At Geom Mugeuk’s joke, Cha Iran smiled faintly.

The medicine was already working well enough.

Even at this moment, she was conflicted.

She had never imagined she would truly find herself worrying over such a thing. Betray the organization? Because she had been won over by the enemy?

It was not Geom Mugeuk’s words that had shaken her. Words could be fabricated as much as one liked.

The problem was that the person named Geom Mugeuk had shaken her.

Those clear, deep eyes that seemed to peer into her heart. His sincerity from another world.

And one more thing.

Ak Gunhak had played a large part. If this was someone that wonderful man liked? Then he must be someone extraordinary. Such feelings had influenced her.

Come to think of it, in regard to Ak Gunhak, the way she saw Geom Mugeuk and the way Geom Mugeuk saw her differed by exactly one word.

Because he is someone Ak Gunhak likes.

Because she is someone who likes Ak Gunhak.

Cha Iran asked in a calm gaze,

“Do you know what it means for an assassin to abandon a commission?”

At least to her, he knew better than anyone what it meant. After all, she had died as an assassin to the very last moment.

“What does it mean?”

“First, I would lose my reputation as an assassin. In truth, since it isn’t known to the world, there may be no reputation to speak of.”

“There must be a reputation you’ve built in your own heart.”

Geom Mugeuk understood her heart.

“There are practical problems, too. If I abandon a commission, I have to return twice the commission fee. This commission fee is so enormous that I would likely have to spend every bit of money I’ve earned in my entire life to pay it back.”

That was not the only problem.

“And they will try to kill me.”

Geom Mugeuk nodded. Yes, they would not let her go quietly. Just as they would not let Ak Gunhak go.

“On the other hand, what I gain is…”

After a brief pause, she imitated Ak Gunhak.

“Huh? Why are you saving me?”

Perhaps she found it funny even after saying it herself, because she laughed.

To that hollow laugh, Geom Mugeuk added one thing.

“There is something you gain in return.”

“What is it?”

“Me. Me, who will tell that man how hard you struggled. Even if he leaves, I will remain.”

For a moment, Cha Iran looked at Geom Mugeuk without saying anything.

Yes, in whatever sense, gaining the young cult leader of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult would certainly have value. But how long would this feeling last?

“At first, you may smile when you see me, but after seeing me once, then twice, every time you look at me like that, you’ll think of me as a traitor. That woman betrayed once, so she’ll betray again.”

Geom Mugeuk answered honestly,

“I’m sorry, but we won’t have many chances to see each other that often. I’m far too busy. We’ll simply maintain a good relationship because we see each other only after a long time. Perhaps someone will even come and say this to you. Are you that assassin from back then? Wake up from your dream. The young cult leader abandoned you.”

Somehow, it felt as if that would truly happen. But she was not offended.

“You don’t need to abandon the commission.”

Geom Mugeuk was being as considerate of her as possible.

“Just don’t forget your promise with me.”

It was the promise that, if the assassination failed, she would accept his commission.

Cha Iran slowly shook her head.

“If you thought me so shameless, then you misjudged me.”

Cha Iran closed her eyes for a moment. How much time passed like that?

She made up her mind.

“I knew there would come a day when I parted ways with them. That was why I always tried to keep my distance from them. I just didn’t know it would be this time.”

She made a decision that would change her life.

“I will abandon this commission.”

It was not betrayal, but abandonment. Not because she felt any special loyalty or gratitude toward them. Since she had dealt with them as an assassin, she would fulfill that responsibility.

Joy filled Geom Mugeuk’s face as he looked at her.

“Thank you. I will make certain you never regret making this choice.”

Now, she told Geom Mugeuk everything honestly.

“My original mission was to kill the Sado Alliance Leader at the celebratory banquet.”

Earlier, she had been unable to move under the Sado Alliance Leader’s aura. But that was when she had been an exposed assassin. Had she stood before him as the woman who had just become the greatest beauty under heaven, the situation would have been different.

“But when you appeared, a new mission came down.”

She took the box containing the bead from her robes.

“They told me to make you take this. But there was a condition. The order was not to give it to you intentionally from our side, but to make it naturally fall into your hands. I gave you two chances to take it, but you didn’t even touch it.”

On the day he had been dead drunk, and on the day she had changed clothes, Geom Mugeuk had not coveted the bead.

“I suppose this must also be returned to them.”

She slowly opened the box.

The bead’s red light was transparent, yet deep.

Around the things stretching through its interior like blood vessels, something shimmered.

Woooong!

The Secret Coffer reacted even more powerfully than before.

It was right then.

Srrrrrk.

The bead inside the box slowly began to rise into the air.

Chapter 709: You’re the One Who Should Flee
-------------------------------------------
Not once until now had the bead ever moved on its own.

But the Bigwe was reacting to the bead.

*Wooooong.*

‘It’s real energy.’

It was unmistakably the same energy the Bigwe had absorbed all this time.

But there was no way the bead those bastards had brought contained any goodwill.

“We must put it back.”

The moment Geom Mugeuk carefully raised the box, trying to return the bead—

*Fwoosh.* The bead shot toward Chairan.

Without even a moment to hesitate, Chairan caught it.

The thought that she shouldn’t catch the bead and the thought that it mustn’t break came to her simultaneously, yet her instincts made her receive it.

It was a bead she had carried in her bosom all this time, a bead she was supposed to return, so her instincts did not choose to avoid it.

Startled, Geom Mugeuk tried to draw his sword, but Chairan raised her hand, signaling that it was alright.

The bead rested on her palm. She had only ever seen it inside the box; this was her first time touching the bead like this.

She had expected a horrifying sensation, but it was not. An indescribable, mystical feeling transmitted through her palm. She did not know it, but it was Jeokjeong’s energy.

Geom Mugeuk extended the box toward her again.

When she quickly tried to place the bead back inside, this time too, the bead reacted first.

*Chararararak.*

A strand of red energy flowed out of the bead like thread and coiled around her wrist.

“I’m fine.”

For now, the energy was benevolent. It did not seem it would harm her at all.

The red thread wound around her wrist began slowly climbing up her arm from her wrist, as if exploring her.

Chairan raised her internal energy and slowly drew her dagger.

Geom Mugeuk also prepared to draw his sword at any moment, while his other hand was ready to snatch the bead from her hand.

If anything happened, he intended to sever the energy connecting the bead and her in a single stroke.

When the energy climbing up her arm passed her shoulder and tried to coil around her neck, she pushed it aside with her other hand.

She did not mind it wrapping her arm, but having it wrap around her neck felt wrong.

At that very moment—

The thread-like energy that had flowed from the bead swelled up as if enraged.

*Suuuuuuk!*

The energy that had wrapped around her arm extended further and coiled around her waist and thighs like a snake. It happened in an instant.

And it was still connected to the bead. As if its roots had to remain planted in the bead.

Even now, it was fine. The energy was still benevolent, after all.

The problem was that there was something inside that energy.

*Swish.*

Geom Mugeuk and Chairan saw it.

Something swimming quickly through the red energy.

*Swirl.*

This time, they could clearly see what had circled back around through the red energy.

To their surprise, it was a snake. A real snake was moving through the red energy that wrapped around her body like a snake.

It was a very small snake, the length of a finger, and its color was red like blood. The snake swam through the red energy as if through water.

As the red snake emerged, the nature of the red energy wrapped around her body began to change. The exceedingly soft red energy seemed to heave greatly once—

*Kwaaaaak.*

—and began to constrict her.

Chairan slashed at the energy with her dagger.

*Saaaak.*

But the energy was not cut. The blade simply passed through as if through smoke.

‘I have to catch that snake!’

She aimed at the swiftly passing snake. It was so fast that cutting it was not easy.

‘Next pass, I’ll cut it!’

*Shiiik! Slash!*

The dagger accurately cut the snake as it passed through the energy wrapped around her waist.

*Ssssss.*

But the severed snake instantly merged back together, returning to its original form and swimming through the red energy.

Geom Mugeuk spoke to her with a grave expression.

“It’s no use. That is a snake that is both real and fake.”

Geom Mugeuk knew what this was. Because among the things he had studied and investigated in advance upon learning his opponent was the Amheukgung, this martial art had been among them.

“That snake is a Biheolsa (飛血蛇).”

It was a term referring to that snake, and also a term meaning a secret art.

The Biheolsa of the Amheukgung.

It was a unique secret art of theirs that infiltrated the human body and controlled the mind.

The methods of controlling a person’s mind were largely divided into Seophonsul and Godoksul (蠱毒術).

But Biheolsa was a secret art that combined those two.

In short, a living secret art.

If gu poison infiltrated the brain to control a person, Biheolsa entered the heart to control them.

Gu poison did not work on those with poison immunity, and moreover, if one knew what kind of gu it was, it could be eliminated.

But Biheolsa was different. It could not be blocked even by Mandok Bulchim, and no antidote existed.

Furthermore, since it was not merely sorcery, it exerted powerful might even against opponents strong against sorcery.

Truly, a secret art that held the greatest effect against Geom Mugeuk had been unleashed.

Of course, creating a Biheolsa was a task so difficult it bordered on impossibility.

It was known to be so difficult that one could not create a single Biheolsa even with a lifetime of effort. The creation process was too arduous and required enormous funds, and even if one succeeded in creating it, it was said to be even more difficult to keep it alive.

It was said to be a secret art that only a prodigy of demonic martial arts could create, and yet—

surprisingly, that very martial art had appeared.

Chairan did not know exactly what a Biheolsa was, but one thing was certain.

“Don’t come near me! It’s a trap!”

There was no way they would try to kill her. She could tell instinctively. It was to lure Geom Mugeuk in.

They must have predicted this situation from the moment they entrusted the bead to her.

Even as this happened, the red energy wrapped more tightly around her body.

“I will kill you. Run away now!”

She thought that if this unknown energy were absorbed into her, she would kill Geom Mugeuk. She would be consumed by madness and wield tremendous internal energy. Since it was a scheme to deal with the Sogyoju, its power would be immense.

*Kkwak.*

Her expression grew more distorted. The red energy pressed against her body with enough force to crush it.

Then Geom Mugeuk said something unexpected.

“You are the one who must flee.”

Chairan could not understand what Geom Mugeuk meant.

The moment Geom Mugeuk approached her again, she recalled what Hwa Domyeong had said to her earlier.

‘You will be persuaded by the Sogyoju. Just as Akgunhak was persuaded.’

He had known that she would join hands with the Sogyoju.

‘This plan has all been calculated.’

Yes, he had definitely said that.

And he had calculated even this.

‘That the Sogyoju would save me too!’

Hwa Domyeong had undoubtedly entrusted her with the bead knowing that Geom Mugeuk would save her.

Knowing that Geom Mugeuk would not willingly absorb this bead. To make him absorb it inevitably.

The words she had spoken to Geom Mugeuk half-jokingly had become reality.

‘…I really was their tool.’

The reason her face was contorted was not merely from pain.

The gradually intensifying pressure was reaching its limit. She could not block it with her Hosin Ganggi. It felt as if her entire body would burst at any moment.

Chairan threw back at him the same words he had once said to her.

“I’ll go to hell first and wait for you.”

At the same time, without a moment’s hesitation, she plunged the dagger into her own neck.

*Shiiik.*

She had broken her own principle of never taking her own life.

In the moment of death, she was grateful that she was someone who did not fear death. It had been the same when she sensed death from the Sado Maengju, and it was the same with this death now. Yes, she thought, if it was this kind of death, it was not so bad.

But of course, Geom Mugeuk was not someone who would stand by and watch.

The dagger stopped before her neck. Geom Mugeuk had halted her dagger with Heogong Seopmul.

“…Let me die for you.”

Of course, it was a request that would not work on Geom Mugeuk.

“Have you forgotten what would happen then? Hey, you’re supposedly the Sogyoju of the Magyo, yet you couldn’t save a single woman who likes me? What the hell have you been doing? I say, my other half.”

Gazing into her eyes, Geom Mugeuk spoke.

“Believe in me.”

As soon as he finished speaking, Geom Mugeuk grasped the red bead in her hand.

At that, the red energy emanating from the bead vibrated even more intensely and constricted her.

Geom Mugeuk placed the bead into his bosom.

To Chairan, that sight looked as if he was taking the bead to his own heart. Because it looked as if he was saying he would make the sacrifice, she cried out anxiously.

“No! Please… no.”

But the bead did not touch his heart; it touched the Bigwe he kept in his bosom.

At that very moment—

The bead, which had extended its energy far, vibrated greatly once.

*Ssshhh.*

The energy wrapped around Chairan loosened and began returning to the bead.

The bead had begun retracting all the energy it had sent out. The red snake was also carried along with that retracting energy.

The Bigwe in his bosom vibrated violently and rose into the air. Geom Mugeuk’s body rose with it.

The Bigwe began to melt the bead’s energy as it always had. Though the energy had originally been moving in and out of the bead, now it began to melt it away without a trace, as if refining it anew.

Thus, the energy that flowed out from the Bigwe began seeping into Geom Mugeuk’s body. Geom Mugeuk accepted it without avoiding it.

The energy that passed through the Bigwe was completely absorbed into Geom Mugeuk’s body. The Biheolsa that had been dissolved in the energy merged back into the form of a snake and was absorbed into Geom Mugeuk’s body along with it.

After all the energy was absorbed, he felt the bead pop out of the Bigwe with a tap.

Geom Mugeuk knew.

That they had used Jeokjeong in order to plant the Biheolsa in him.

If they took control of his mind, they would probably try to retrieve all the energy in his body by any means necessary. They would never give up on this energy.

Anyway, thanks to this, Geom Mugeuk had even succeeded in obtaining Jeokjeong’s energy.

There was one problem: the Biheolsa that had entered his body!

The energies within his body simultaneously awakened and began rampaging. He could tell it was a rejection response to the newly entered Biheolsa.

Now the Biheolsa was also among the five streams of energy coursing through his blood vessels.

The Biheolsa, like a living creature, looked for any opportunity to somehow burrow into Geom Mugeuk’s heart.

Geom Mugeuk circulated his internal power, struggling to block it.

He suffered immense pain fit to steal one’s consciousness, yet Chairan did not faint.

She struggled to rise and tried to help Geom Mugeuk, but stopped.

“Sogyoju!”

She could feel it. That Geom Mugeuk was currently fighting that red snake inside his body. It was not a situation for an outsider to recklessly intervene.

“Overcome it!”

It was then—

*Tap.*

Someone suppressed her mahyeol from behind. And whispered in her ear.

“The Sogyoju can never win.”

The one who appeared behind her like a ghost was none other than Hwa Domyeong.

Having just been freed from the constricting energy and being focused on Geom Mugeuk, she could not block Hwa Domyeong’s surprise attack.

“So the Sogyoju really knows how to absorb energy.”

He made an expression as if he could not believe what he was seeing with his own eyes.

Hwa Domyeong’s gaze turned back to Chairan.

“Good work, friend.”

Chairan glared at him in silence. Hwa Domyeong opened his fan as if frightened and hid his face behind it. His words flowed from behind the fan.

“Because of the Cheonma Hoshingong, there was simply no way to implant the Biheolsa into the Sogyoju’s body through ordinary means. So we decided to cast a large bait as well.”

That bait was Chairan, and also the bead containing Jeokjeong’s energy.

“No matter that he is the Sogyoju, he cannot block the Biheolsa.”

Hwa Domyeong peeked his face out from behind the fan.

Various emotions were mingled in Hwa Domyeong’s eyes as he looked at her.

On the other hand, Chairan’s emotions looking at him were clear.

Contempt and rage.

“You brought up Akgunhak on purpose too, didn’t you?”

Hwado Myeong was the one who had first brought up the story of Akgunhak to her.

To naturally draw her closer to the Sogyoju through Akgunhak.

A satisfied smile formed at Hwa Domyeong’s lips. It was a smile of affirmation.

“The Sogyoju is not someone who would stand by and watch a person who abandoned a contract even at the cost of giving up everything die before his eyes.”

Chairan felt fury and despair. But now was not the time to blame herself.

“Look here, friend.”

“Sorry, but you still call me friend?”

“If not others, you shouldn’t do this to me, should you?”

“Didn’t I tell you earlier not to meet the Sogyoju?”

Is that all the excuse you could think of?

Chairan suppressed the urge to spit out those words.

“Why couldn’t you trust me? You pretended to refuse the contract while trying to kill them all. You should have given me a chance.”

Hwa Domyeong smiled at her. It was a clear sneer.

“This Sogyoju is truly something. To see you tell such lies for him.”

So the reason you fanned yourself so frivolously by my side all this time, calling me your friend, was for this day?

She held back the words she wanted to say once again.

“If it ends like this, I can’t close my eyes even in death. Let me carry out my last contract with my own hands.”

She intended to save Geom Mugeuk somehow. Even at the cost of her own life.

But it did not work on Hwa Domyeong.

“Don’t look at me. Look over there. It is the moment the history of the martial world changes.”

Geom Mugeuk stood with his eyes closed. His eyeballs moved violently beneath his eyelids. It was clear he was fighting a desperate battle against the Biheolsa inside his body.

“If the Biheolsa completely controls the Sogyoju…”

Words that should not have been spoken flowed out.

“The Sogyoju will kill the Sado Maengju.”

*Swoosh.*

Hwa Domyeong opened his fan and gently fanned her.

“Your contract is a success.”

Chairan looked at Geom Mugeuk, who was fighting that desperate battle with his eyes closed. His last words came to mind.

Believe in me.

I even gave up the contract because of you, so I have to believe. Yes, she thought, I can give up the contract, but I won’t give up on you.

“A successful contract, you say?”

Chairan asked with a captivating smile.

“I don’t have to return the money, right?”

Chapter 710: The One Who Will Pull Me Out of Hell
-------------------------------------------------
“You still haven’t given up on the Young Cult Leader, I see.”

Hwa Domyeong interpreted Cha I‑ran’s joke that way. She wasn’t someone who would crack a joke like, “I guess I don’t have to pay you back in this situation,” after all. Moreover, was she smiling so bewitchingly at him?

“Honestly, I was hoping you wouldn’t fall for it.”

Hwa Domyeong slowly walked over and stood before Geom Mugeuk. Geom Mugeuk, his eyes closed, was drenched in sweat.

“He’s holding out long. By now, the Blood Silkworm should have had time to settle in the Young Cult Leader’s heart.”

Swoosh.

Hwa Domyeong opened his fan and fanned Geom Mugeuk’s face.

Just then, Cha I‑ran’s voice came from behind.

“I’d like to finish the rest of the wine.”

Her demonic meridians had been suppressed, rendering her unable to move.

Hwa Domyeong sent a burst of finger wind to unseal her acupoints so she could move. Of course, her internal energy was still suppressed.

Cha I‑ran picked up the wine bottle on the table and poured it into her cup. It was the wine they had ordered together while dining with Geom Mugeuk. The two of them had rented a private room at the restaurant, and all of this had begun the moment they finished their meal.

Without turning around, Hwa Domyeong spoke.

“At least he had a splendid last meal.”

Those words carried the meaning that once this matter was over, he would kill them both.

When she did not respond at all to his words, Hwa Domyeong glanced back at her. Whether she had given up quickly or not, she neither begged for her life nor showed any will to fight.

She simply leaned sideways in her chair, drinking in silence as she gazed out the window.

She recalled the moment earlier when she had sat side by side with Geom Mugeuk in the manor garden, looking up at the sky.

Back then, she had never imagined that she would abandon the request.

She had inwardly smiled at the sight of the Divine Flower Pavilion Lord changing her fate without a moment’s hesitation, yet she herself was no different.

“What are you thinking about?”

At Hwa Domyeong’s question, Cha I‑ran refilled her cup and replied.

“I’m not thinking about anything.”

Cha I‑ran treated him indifferently. Just as he seemed to know her well, she too knew Hwa Domyeong well.

He was someone who always pretended to be shy, hiding behind his fan, yet he was a man who wanted to stand at the center of the world. At this moment of success, he would be hoping for her acknowledgment and praise.

But she wouldn’t give it.

Seeing her resigned demeanor, Hwa Domyeong finally brought up a story that would capture her attention.

“When Akgunhak recovered the secret chest…”

The cup that had been heading to Cha I‑ran’s lips froze in midair.

“The old man was truly overjoyed. That alone more than made up for his failure to kill Geom Mugeuk. But the secret chest he brought back was useless, producing no effect whatsoever.”

“He was outdone by the Young Cult Leader again.”

This was what Cha I‑ran was curious about.

“What was that red energy contained in the bead?”

The energy she had felt at first was truly pleasant. Though its nature had changed when the red serpent emerged.

“The power to prevent destruction.”

Once again, the word “destruction” flowed from Hwa Domyeong’s lips.

“From whom? From the Demon Lord?”

Hwa Domyeong nodded and looked at Geom Mugeuk.

“Today, it all ends.”

* * *

The Blood Silkworm traversed every blood vessel in Geom Mugeuk’s body as if it were its own home. It was as flexible as a water snake swiftly swimming through a narrow cave.

The power and vitality felt from the Blood Silkworm were completely different from what was seen with the eyes.

It was condensed with all sorts of elixirs and exotic herbs of the martial world, and the mysterious power of countless spiritual artifacts. He felt that if he could kill and melt it down, he would obtain energy far more potent than any elixir he had consumed thus far. If he could catch it, that is.

It was fast.

Ssssh.

It weaved through the blood vessels at high speed, aiming only for the heart.

Geom Mugeuk blocked the main blood vessels leading to his heart with his internal strength. His vigorous and pure internal energy wrapped around his heart as if to protect it.

The Blood Silkworm, having swum rapidly along the blood vessels, collided with the inner force guarding his heart.

Fwoom!

As the shock ran through his blood vessels, Geom Mugeuk clenched his teeth. He tried to grab the retreating creature with his internal force, but it was absurdly fast.

Ssssh.

Like a loach slipping out of one’s hand, it escaped Geom Mugeuk’s internal force and swam back into the blood vessels.

The problem was that he couldn’t afford to focus on this thing alone.

Inside his body, the five energies absorbed from the secret chest were rampaging through his blood vessels. Presumably, this was because of the Blood Silkworm stirring throughout his body.

He had tried to calm them somehow, but to no avail. He realized that only by completely melting away the Blood Silkworm could these five energies settle down again.

Thus, he had to wage a defensive battle to protect his heart amidst a literal raging storm, while also carrying out a capture operation.

Pow!

The second collision was even more powerful than the last. Geom Mugeuk clenched his teeth once more.

He thought that if he hadn’t strengthened his blood vessels with the Heavenly Meridian Strengthening Technique, and if he hadn’t built up even more vigorous and pure internal energy through his various fortuitous encounters, this single collision would have broken through.

He tried to catch it again this time, but the Blood Silkworm quickly escaped once more. This time, he sent his internal force in pursuit.

It was clever, and its will to survive was strong. Evading the pursuit of his internal force, it traveled between the countless blood vessels in his body, searching for a way to survive.

It mainly fled into blood vessels where internal energy did not flow naturally. It instinctively knew which way to escape.

On the third attack, Geom Mugeuk launched a surrounding operation.

He sent his internal force simultaneously to squeeze it from all sides, but this time it escaped the encirclement by using the five energies rampaging within his body.

The paths the Blood Silkworm took while fleeing mostly ran counter to the flow of true energy, and among them were meridian pathways he could have accessed but had never thought to explore.

‘Ah! So there was a path like this too.’

The paths it revealed in its desperate struggle to survive were, in equal measure, paths of new enlightenment for Geom Mugeuk.

Having circled around once more, the Blood Silkworm charged toward his heart again.

Four times, five times, ten times, twenty times…….

Perilous situations unfolded as well, but Geom Mugeuk held fast. The more it repeated, the more new meridian pathways Geom Mugeuk discovered.

The Blood Silkworm did not tire. But neither did Geom Mugeuk.

You were created with difficulty over several decades?

Sorry, but it’s the same for me.

‘So come at me as much as you want!’

* * *

Finally, Geom Mugeuk began to open his eyes.

Hwa Domyeong stood before him with an expectant expression.

Standing behind him, Cha I‑ran prayed fervently.

‘Please!’

The moment fate was decided!

A gasp of joy flowed from Hwa Domyeong’s lips, while a sigh of despair escaped from Cha I‑ran.

Geom Mugeuk’s two eyes were completely dyed in red energy.

“It worked! The Blood Silkworm has completely dominated the Young Cult Leader!”

Cha I‑ran clenched her fist. She had believed Geom Mugeuk would overcome.

‘You told me to believe in you!’

She despaired, while ecstasy filled Hwa Domyeong’s face.

The red energy that had flared in Geom Mugeuk’s eyes soon vanished.

Shortly after, his eyes returned to normal, and he gazed at Hwa Domyeong with a calm look.

Irrepressible emotion surged on Hwa Domyeong’s face. Everyone had failed to subdue the Young Cult Leader, yet he had succeeded in capturing him.

“We have been waiting for this one chance. You are merely a servant who ended up doing our work for us.”

Geom Mugeuk simply stared at him without revealing any emotion.

Hwa Domyeong coldly gave an order.

“Go and… the Demon Lord…”

At that moment, Cha I‑ran cut him off.

“If the Young Cult Leader kills the Demon Lord, a war will break out.”

“Indeed.”

Cha I‑ran spoke with contemptuous eyes.

“All to stop destruction? Go plug it with that mouth and fan of yours.”

Hwa Domyeong pretended to cover his mouth with his fan as he finished giving the order to Geom Mugeuk.

“Kill the Demon Lord and return to me.”

Geom Mugeuk slowly turned around.

Hwa Domyeong turned toward Cha I‑ran and spoke.

“Shall we have one last drink now?”

If the plan succeeded, this had been decided from the beginning. She would betray him, and a traitor was eliminated.

Cha I‑ran silently watched Geom Mugeuk’s back, visible beyond Hwa Domyeong’s shoulder.

She was frustrating to the very end.

Hwa Domyeong stared into her eyes. Into her eyes that looked toward the Young Cult Leader until the very end.

Just as he was about to kill her while looking into those eyes.

Hwa Domyeong saw. The reflection in her pupils. The glint of something flying toward him.

Shhhiiii.

Hwa Domyeong desperately twisted his body.

Puuuk!

The Black Demon Sword pierced his shoulder.

Had he not dodged at the last moment, it would have been a strike that pierced his heart.

Geom Mugeuk, who had been walking toward the door, soundlessly turned around and attacked him.

The move he used was the Dark King Step among the Wind God Steps.

Hwa Domyeong, his shoulder pierced, was engulfed in shock and terror.

“…I told you to kill the Demon Lord.”

He thought Geom Mugeuk had misunderstood the order.

That was why he couldn’t counterattack immediately. He had to figure out what had gone wrong and send him again. The Blood Silkworm, which had taken so much time and effort to create, was something that could never be made again in this era.

“I am your master…”

The moment his eyes met Geom Mugeuk’s, Hwa Domyeong’s entire body trembled as if struck by lightning.

The leisure within those pupils was something that ten Blood Silkworms could never create.

“Don’t tell me… the Blood Silkworm didn’t work?”

A bright smile formed on Geom Mugeuk’s face.

“The drug and the Blood Silkworm. I enjoyed them both.”

Having finished speaking, Geom Mugeuk drew his sword.

Fwoosh!

Blood sprayed from his shoulder. Geom Mugeuk simply let him try to staunch the wound.

“Get lost while I’m letting you live.”

Even though it was an absolutely advantageous situation, Hwa Domyeong didn’t believe those words. He thought it was a trick to catch him off guard and strike.

Geom Mugeuk told him the reason.

“Thank Lady Cha. Because she told me not to kill you.”

She had indeed told him not to harm Hwa Domyeong. But even Cha I‑ran had not known she was truly letting him live at this moment.

Hwa Domyeong looked at Cha I‑ran. She didn’t know Geom Mugeuk’s intention, so she only looked silently at Hwa Domyeong.

Shh.

As the fan unfolded, Hwa Domyeong disappeared.

It seemed like he was making her owe him a life debt, but that wasn’t the reason.

Once he confirmed Hwa Domyeong had completely disappeared from the room.

Clang.

Geom Mugeuk dropped the Black Demon Sword to the floor and vomited a gush of blood.

“Young Cult Leader!”

The moment she supported Geom Mugeuk.

Geom Mugeuk’s two eyes were beginning to turn red again.

Cha I‑ran grabbed Geom Mugeuk’s arm tightly.

“Come to your senses!”

Barely, Geom Mugeuk pushed back the red energy. The Blood Silkworm was still not fully under control.

“I only gave up half my heart.”

He said he had given it up, but in truth, he had turned half his heart into a prison to lure it in. It was the only way to catch it.

Because he was locked in a tense confrontation at his heart, he couldn’t execute the follow‑up move to kill Hwa Domyeong earlier. If he had, he would have completely yielded his heart to the Blood Silkworm. The first Dark King Step had also been a move he forcefully executed to save her.

Cha I‑ran could understand. Geom Mugeuk could have quietly escaped. He could have gone out as if to kill the Demon Lord and then received help from the Extreme Evil Soma or the Demon Lord to heal himself.

But he had forcefully used his internal energy to save her.

“Why on earth did you do this?”

With trembling hands, Geom Mugeuk released the seal on her internal energy.

“No need to be moved. Because now you must save me.”

Even as he spoke, Geom Mugeuk’s eyes were repeatedly invaded by red energy and then pushed back again.

“When I open my eyes again…”

Geom Mugeuk closed his eyes and spoke his last words. The red energy flowing between his closing eyelids looked like the gates of hell closing. There was only one person who could pull him out of that hell.

“Lady Soma must assuredly be before me.”

Geom Mugeuk intended to follow where the prophecy led. Having delivered his last words, Geom Mugeuk completely closed his eyes.

Cha I‑ran could tell. That the time given to him was not long.

Cha I‑ran sheathed the Black Demon Sword and hoisted Geom Mugeuk onto her back.

Only because it was Geom Mugeuk could she make such a joke.

“Commissioning someone without even paying the fee?”

She leaped straight out through the window.

At that moment, she saw. Someone on the roof of the building across the way looking down at her.

It was Hwa Domyeong, who had not yet left.

Cha I‑ran leaped away without a word.

From the sight of Geom Mugeuk on her back, Hwa Domyeong realized he had been deceived. That he hadn’t been spared, but rather, hadn’t been able to be killed.

Swoosh swoosh swoosh swoosh swoosh swoosh swoosh!

Behind him, martial artists revealed themselves by dispelling their concealment, dropping from the sky like heavenly gods descending. Their stealth and movement techniques were extraordinary, and their imposing spirit was immense.

“Stop them! We must never hand the Young Cult Leader over to the Extreme Evil Soma!”

The martial artists all leaped after her.

When Hwa Domyeong also tried to leap after them, his remaining subordinates restrained him.

“The wound is deep.”

Though the shoulder wound from the sword was deep, he had only pressed the meridian points to stop the bleeding.

Moving carelessly could result in greater injury, but he couldn’t just stay put.

“The Young Cult Leader’s condition is worse.”

Even if he gave up on killing the Demon Lord, he could not give up on Geom Mugeuk. The energies dwelling in his body had to be recovered by all means.

“Release the seal.”

* * *

Piiii, ba‑ba‑boom!

The Crescent Moon Masked Guest, who had been sitting by the window looking outside, heard the sound of fireworks from afar.

A peculiar firework sound.

It was the exact same firework sound he had heard when carrying out the operation to capture the Gahin Cult assassins.

The Crescent Moon Masked Guest looked back at the Extreme Evil Soma. The Extreme Evil Soma was meditating with his eyes closed.

“It’s the assassins’ distress signal.”

Behind the mask, the Extreme Evil Soma’s eyes flashed open.

As he stood from his seat, the Masked Guests who had been doing their respective tasks around him also all rose at once.

The Extreme Evil Soma looked at the Heavenly Flower Pavilion Lord, who had been drinking tea with Ian.

The Heavenly Flower Pavilion Lord slowly nodded. She could feel it fatefully. That the moment of the prophecy had finally arrived.

“Let’s go!”

As the Extreme Evil Soma leaped through the window and soared into the sky, all the windows opened and the Masked Guests also all leaped out in unison.

Beyond them in the sky, fireworks were exploding in the distance.

Chapter 711: What a Terrible Assassin
-------------------------------------
Darkness settled.

Chai Ran looked into the darkened alley with wary eyes. From within, she could feel a murderous intent holding its breath, and the air flowing around her foretold death.

Enemies were spread out all around.

Chai Ran carefully set Geom Mugeuk down.

Leaning his body against a wall, she lightly covered him with black cloth.

It was a concealment screen (暗幕), something assassins always carried to keep themselves from standing out in dark places like this. Thin and light, it folded down to the size of a palm.

Fortunately, the clothes she had worn today were the black martial attire Geom Mugeuk had said suited her. Easy to fight in, and inconspicuous in the dark.

‘It’s almost as if you knew something like this would happen.’

She moved silently into the darkness.

Inside the alley, a warrior stood leaning against the wall. He had not drawn his sword, lest the blade reflect light, and remained with his back against the wall.

Saaak.

With the sound of flesh parting, his throat was cut.

The one who had clamped a hand over his mouth and slit his throat was Chai Ran. At that moment, she had become a perfect assassin.

After quietly lowering the warrior’s corpse to the ground, Chai Ran was about to return to where Geom Mugeuk lay.

Shiiik!

A sword flew at her from the darkness. A warrior who had been hiding dispelled his concealment and revealed himself. From the start, the exposed warrior had been bait—a trap meant to kill her.

Chai Ran twisted her body by reflex and avoided the attack.

Paat!

The incoming sword grazed her arm.

Puk!

The dagger in her hand lodged in the warrior’s throat.

After laying the corpse down quietly once more, Chai Ran flew back toward where Geom Mugeuk was. She had dealt with her opponent, but had she been even a little slower, she might have been injured. That meant these were not opponents to be taken lightly.

‘They’re Hwa Domyeong’s direct subordinates.’

Their martial arts were one thing, but as befitting Hwa Domyeong’s men, their concealment techniques were no ordinary skill either.

She pulled away the concealment screen that had covered Geom Mugeuk.

A fierce heat emanated from Geom Mugeuk’s body as he lay with his eyes closed. No doubt, a desperate battle was raging inside him.

“I believe you can hear me. Hold on just a little longer.”

She hoisted Geom Mugeuk onto her back. Even with him on her back, she needed both hands free, so she tore her clothes and secured him tightly to her body.

The fastest way to Sinhwabang was to use lightness skill, leaping from roof to roof, but that method would make her too conspicuous, and every attack would focus on her. She could not fight like that while carrying Geom Mugeuk.

Once again, she began moving through the darkness.

Like the finest assassin, her movements betrayed neither breath nor footfall, not even the slightest aura. It was as if a shadow itself were moving through the dark.
