Baek Jagang, leader of the Sado Alliance, stood on the roof of a building that afforded a clear view over all of the Shinhwa Gang.

Behind him, Bi Sain reported the results of the investigation.

“According to our findings, not only Young Gang Leader An Hupyeong, but the other martial artists of the Shinhwa Gang as well, seem to have been unaware of this matter.”

Perhaps that was only natural. They would not have carelessly leaked such an enormous plan as the assassination of the Sado Alliance Leader. It must have been a secret known only to Yeom Sageom and Cheol Hando, who had died together with An Cheonggwang, the leader of the Shinhwa Gang.

“How shall we deal with An Hupyeong?”

As the direct bloodline of the Shinhwa Gang Leader who had plotted this affair, he could be made to answer for the crime. Even if he had no guilt, guilt could be created.

But Baek Jagang did not bother to do so.

“Leave him be. If he knew nothing, there is no need to punish him.”

Bi Sain considered it a wise decision.

An Hupyeong was not a man with enough venom in his heart, nor enough boldness, to seek revenge for this.

Rather than showing the cold-heartedness of condemning even blood kin for a great crime, it would be better in many ways to show the Sado Alliance Leader’s broad magnanimity.

In truth, An Hupyeong’s troubles would begin from now on.

If he inherited the Shinhwa Gang as things stood?

Could this immature wastrel, who had lived like a good-for-nothing, truly protect the Shinhwa Gang in this harsh martial world of the unorthodox factions? It would likely not be easy.

Just then, the voice of In Gung, captain of the guards, came from the air.

“Young Cult Leader Geom has come.”

“Have him come.”

Bi Sain bowed to Baek Jagang.

“Then I shall withdraw.”

Thinking that his master would have things to say to Geom Mugeuk alone regarding this matter, Bi Sain stepped aside.

A short while later, Geom Mugeuk ascended to the place where Baek Jagang waited alone.

“I greet the Alliance Leader.”

After greeting him politely, Geom Mugeuk conveyed his gratitude.

“Thank you for entrusting this matter to me.”

Since it had been a plan to kill him, Baek Jagang could have handled everything himself from beginning to end. Moreover, this was the territory of the Sado Alliance.

“Do you know why I left this matter in your hands?”

“For what reason?”

It was not because he found it troublesome, nor because Geom Mugeuk had informed him of the assassination plan.

Baek Jagang, who had been looking down at the Shinhwa Gang, turned to Geom Mugeuk and said,

“Because I respect you.”

Sensing that he meant it sincerely, Geom Mugeuk also bowed his head in sincerity.

“Thank you truly for thinking so highly of someone as lacking as I am. I, too, have always respected you, Alliance Leader.”

No shiver ran over his ears.

Naturally, Baek Jagang’s gaze toward Geom Mugeuk softened.

“You’ve worked hard.”

“Lord Soma did everything in this matter. I slept soundly and came back.”

Baek Jagang quietly stared at Geom Mugeuk.

“It seems you slept well.”

Amazingly, Baek Jagang had read the change in Geom Mugeuk. Geom Mugeuk was, quite literally, becoming stronger every time they met.

This young cult leader before him made a man who thought it was time to step down feel that he ought to begin anew. He was certain the Murim Alliance Leader felt much the same. Perhaps even the Demon Cult Leader was no different.

“Did you find out why they tried to kill me?”

“I am sorry, but I could not.”

Baek Jagang thought perhaps that, too, was only natural. The moment he heard those words that sounded like a prophecy, this had ceased to be a simple assassination plot.

“What do you think of that nonsense?”

If the Sado Alliance Leader does not die, the murim will be destroyed.

By calling it nonsense, Baek Jagang revealed that he had no intention of destroying the murim.

It is nonsense.

He could have said that for Baek Jagang’s sake.

“It likely isn’t nonsense.”

Baek Jagang’s small eyes flashed keenly.

“I believe there is certainly some meaning contained in those words.”

The master of Cheonhwaru had said it sounded like a prophecy. That meant that, in some way, the Sado Alliance Leader was deeply connected to what would happen in the future.

“Then does that mean you believe those words?”

“That you will destroy the murim, Alliance Leader? No, there’s no way. If that happened, the murim would truly be destroyed.”

He knew it was something said to please him, but he could not help feeling better at the young cult leader’s words.

“I do not believe those words at all.”

“Then why did you say they were meaningful?”

“They are people with twisted convictions. They are the descendants of Cheonui Palace, who believe themselves to be executors of Heaven’s will. And among the three palaces of Cheonui Palace, they are descendants of the Dark Palace, known to be the most wicked. You know, do you not, that three hundred years ago they actually started a war to conquer the murim?”

Geom Mugeuk interpreted this prophecy from their point of view.

“If you remain alive, Alliance Leader, perhaps they cannot achieve whatever it is they dream of. Perhaps they interpreted your saving the murim, in reverse, as its destruction.”

Baek Jagang smiled faintly. In the end, it was not simply nonsense, but nonsense they themselves took seriously.

“I have no intention of destroying the murim, but I have no intention of saving it either.”

“That remains to be seen.”

Even at these words, no shiver ran over his ears. Geom Mugeuk truly believed that he might save the murim.

“Do not be offended by that prophecy. It means you stand at the center of this incident to that extent. But I believe one thing is certain. There is something they mean to gain by assassinating you, Alliance Leader. Since there is already a clear successor, it is not the power of the Sado Alliance, but something else.”

Geom Mugeuk looked at Baek Jagang and asked,

“What is it they want, Alliance Leader?”

Baek Jagang gave no answer.

“The answer is certainly with you, Alliance Leader.”

* * *

Bi Sain was looking at the finals stage of this tournament.

Just then, Geom Mugeuk flew in with lightness skill and landed on the empty stage.

“What a pity. I wanted to see Ian become the greatest beauty under heaven on this stage.”

Bi Sain had known he would come looking for him. If he had come to meet his master, then he would come to see him as well. Sure enough, even without arranging where to meet, he had found him on his own.

“If you regret it, hold it again yourself.”

At Bi Sain’s words, Geom Mugeuk sat down on the edge of the stage.

“A Greatest Beauty Under Heaven tournament held by the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult, huh. Would anyone even dare participate? There’d be so few contestants that even if you put on makeup and entered, you might make it to the main round.”

Bi Sain let out a laugh. Most of his laughter was at such absurd remarks, but still, he often laughed in front of Geom Mugeuk.

“Will you return to the cult now?”

“I should. Is it because I met the Alliance Leader? I miss my father terribly.”

He had told Cha Iran they should rescue Ak Gunhak and let him leave, but he could not simply do so without thought.

He did not even know where he was, and even if he did, this was not something that could be handled so easily.

If he believed in Ak Gunhak, he had to wait. If their bond continued, then surely there would be a call of fate.

“Ah, by the way, do you know where the place with the best atmosphere and scenery around here is?”

“Why do you ask?”

Although this was the territory of the Sado Alliance, Geom Mugeuk, who had been all over the Central Plains, would know better than he did. He wondered why he was asking him, but there was a scheme behind it to tease him.

“I’m going to help you do what you’re good at.”

“What I’m good at? What is that?”

Bi Sain’s unease slowly rose. He would rather have heard an answer like, Killing people!

“You know. The thing you’re good at doing secretly from your friends.”

Just as Bi Sain’s sense of crisis rose even higher, Geom Mugeuk said as if whispering,

“That secret, hidden affection of yours. Love in secret!”

In that instant, Bi Sain was flustered. He knew Geom Mugeuk was referring to Han Seol, the young palace mistress of the Ice Palace, but Bi Sain flatly denied it.

“I don’t know what you mean.”

He had said he would tease him about it for a long time, and now it had begun.

“I’m busy, so I’ll just leave for today.”

Geom Mugeuk lightly launched himself into the air as he spoke. Even as he left, he teased him.

“Ah! You have to go if I’m to pass on the secret technique to you.”

* * *

Seventeenth Gyo was crossing a narrow wooden bridge.

The bridge stretched across the water toward a pavilion in the middle of the lake.

The scenery around her was beautiful beyond compare. But she could not enjoy the wildflowers swaying by the lakeshore, nor the blue-green lake sparkling like jewels.

It was Cha Iran who had ordered her to go to the pavilion. Without any explanation, she had singled her out and sent her here. Ah, she had said one thing: dress well before going. For some reason, it felt as if she were heading out on an assassination.

And so Seventeenth Gyo arrived at the pavilion.

When she climbed up into the pavilion and saw the person waiting there, she froze on the spot. It was none other than the Crescent Moon Faceless Guest.

He, too, seemed surprised, as if he had not known she would come.

For a while, an awkward silence and atmosphere flowed between them.

Seventeenth Gyo could tell.

Cha Iran had realized that she had given her heart to this Faceless Guest. Had Fifth Gyo told her? Or had someone seen and reported the two of them greeting each other while they were clearing away corpses?

In any case, this must be Cha Iran’s warning. Not to give her heart to a man.

After taking a deep breath, Seventeenth Gyo broke the silence. Thinking she might never see this person again, she conveyed her honest feelings.

“Thank you for saving me twice. When I saw earlier that you were alive, I was truly happy.”

She had been truly delighted when Cha Iran decided to rest in the shadow of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult. But now, even if they were in the same place, she would surely never be able to acknowledge him.

She took a dagger from her bosom and gave it to him.

“This dagger has meaning to me. I’d like to give it to you as thanks.”

She was speaking boldly, but her voice was trembling. To feel this way toward someone whose face she had not even seen.

“And please understand if I pretend not to know you from now on.”

The Crescent Moon Faceless Guest only listened to her in silence.

“Then I’ll be going now.”

It was just as she lowered her head in farewell.

Someone crossed the lake like the wind and flew into the pavilion.

Holding a bottle of liquor in his hand, it was none other than Geom Mugeuk.

“I bought liquor for the two of you. Since you probably can’t drink strong liquor, I bought something with a fine fragrance. When people first meet and things are awkward, having this between you helps the mood, doesn’t it? You have no idea how far I ran to buy this. But what’s with the atmosphere? Did you already fight?”

At Geom Mugeuk’s appearance, both Seventeenth Gyo and the Crescent Moon Faceless Guest were startled.

Seventeenth Gyo asked with a surprised expression,

“Young Cult Leader, why have you come here?”

“What do you mean, why? I’m the one who called you here. I asked Miss Cha to do it. I asked her to call you here.”

Seeing her bewildered expression, Geom Mugeuk said,

“Have you forgotten already? Didn’t I say I would arrange a place for you to express your thanks? So you could meet him in person and give him the dagger.”

“!”

How could Seventeenth Gyo have forgotten? But she had never imagined the young cult leader would truly arrange such a meeting, and so she had never thought this place had been prepared by Geom Mugeuk.

‘He truly prepared this place for me?’

Seventeenth Gyo’s face flushed. Unaware of that, she had told the other party all her feelings far too honestly.

She could not bring herself to look at him.

“I misunderstood. I’m sorry.”

She was so embarrassed that she wanted to jump into the lake right then and there.

At that moment, the Crescent Moon Faceless Guest opened his mouth for the first time.

“The Young Cult Leader even bought liquor.”

Seventeenth Gyo lifted her head and looked at him.

“You should at least have a cup before you go.”

A small wave of emotion spread through her eyes.

The Faceless Guest cupped his fist respectfully toward Geom Mugeuk and conveyed his gratitude.

“Thank you for arranging this place, Young Cult Leader.”

In this moment, the Faceless Guest seemed so perfectly composed that Seventeenth Gyo became terribly curious about what sort of face lay beneath that mask.

“Then have a talk, you two.”

Leaving the two of them behind, Geom Mugeuk quietly withdrew.

In an instant, Geom Mugeuk crossed the lake and landed by the shore. Cha Iran was waiting there. Before the two of them, he had been an extremely dignified young cult leader.

“Miss Cha, among assassin martial arts, is there perhaps a secret technique that lets you breathe underwater and eavesdrop on people? Ah! I’m dying of curiosity about what those two are talking about! Let’s go listen in on them!”

When he had asked her to call Seventeenth Gyo, Cha Iran had been surprised. She was surprised that he had really let the two of them meet, but what surprised her even more was that he had not forgotten this matter. The young cult leader of the Demon Cult had not forgotten the affairs of mere subordinates?

Cha Iran turned her gaze toward the pavilion.

“What will you do if those two really elope in the middle of the night?”

“That would be nice, but in reality, they’ll probably keep it in their hearts and long for each other all their lives.”

“Wouldn’t that be even more unfortunate?”

At that, Geom Mugeuk looked at her and abruptly asked,

“Are you more unfortunate because you hold that person in your heart?”

“!”

She was not. In her life, the feeling of liking him occupied a fairly large space in the small realm of happiness she possessed. Even if it was a feeling she held alone.

“If you devote yourself to our cult, those two will be able to live happily without eloping in the night.”

“Are you telling me to become a demon cultist because of them?”

Then unexpected words flowed from Geom Mugeuk’s mouth.

“No, I’m telling you to become a demon cultist for my sake.”

For an instant, Cha Iran was startled. She had never expected him to say such a thing.

“You would become an assassin corps directly under the young cult leader. Rumors would spread throughout the murim. They would say the young cult leader of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult commands the greatest assassin corps in the murim! And an assassin corps made up of truly beautiful women, at that!”

Cha Iran looked at Geom Mugeuk.

“Do you think you can handle me?”

A brief silence passed. She thought he would wave his hands and say he was joking, but Geom Mugeuk looked at her with a serious expression.

For a very brief moment, Cha Iran wondered what it would be like to live such a life.

This young cult leader was someone who made her seriously consider even such an absurd proposal.

“You said that once we eliminated them, you would let me leave as I pleased.”

“Wouldn’t it be far too regrettable to simply let a talent like you go?”

She should have dismissed it by telling him not to spout nonsense. Instead, Cha Iran asked again,

“That man fought in order to leave. Then what are you fighting for?”

After a brief pause, Geom Mugeuk answered.

“At least one thing is certain. It is not for the unification of the murim or the destruction of the murim.”

Geom Mugeuk revealed his honest feelings to her.

“I want to fish with my father, spend time and play with the Demon Supremes, drink, read books, and travel. I want to live like that.”

Geom Mugeuk looked toward the pavilion beyond the water sparkling in the sunlight.

“And help those two elope in the night. And also…”

Lastly, looking at Cha Iran, he added,

“Find your love for you.”

Cha Iran could not tell how much was sincere and how much was a joke, but one thing was certain. If she stayed with this person, a great change would come to her life.

Geom Mugeuk deliberately let out a sigh.

“To me, living a life like that feels more difficult than uniting the murim.”

Geom Mugeuk’s gaze toward her deepened.

“Can you handle someone like me?”

Chapter 717: Hold That Hand Tight
---------------------------------
You ask whether I could handle you?

Cha I-ran silently gazed at Geom Mugeuk.

He was a man who said that a life spent fishing with his father was harder than unifying the murim.

He was a man who would arrange such a place for a subordinate—one who was not even his own, but a mere assassin.

He was a man who ran far off to buy wine, hoping the two of them would elope in the night.

He was a man who reached out his hand to someone who had tried to kill him. And that man’s identity was the Young Cult Leader of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult.

In all her life as a martial artist, she had never seen anyone like him. How could she possibly handle him?

“You don’t need an assassin corps, do you?”

She thought that if this Young Cult Leader before her truly wanted such a life, then he would not like assassins.

Geom Mugeuk readily admitted her guess.

“That’s right. I don’t need an assassin corps. I need you.”

“Why me? You must have countless experts who can carry out your orders.”

“To be honest, at first I was concerned because I liked Ak Gunhak. Then I admired your courage in boldly abandoning the commission. And now you’ve saved my life.”

In truth, that alone was reason enough, without needing any further explanation. But there was more.

“You’re beautiful enough to contend for the title of the greatest beauty under heaven, and your martial arts are excellent as well. And more than anything, there is one reason I like you.”

Cha I-ran wondered what it was. It felt as though everything had already been said. No, so much had been said that it was almost embarrassing.

The decisive reason Geom Mugeuk gave was something she had never imagined.

“Because you are a leader who says that if someone has enough strength to bite down on a poison pill, then they should clench their molars and struggle to live until the very end.”

“!”

“I want someone like that as one of my people.”

Cha I-ran could now understand clearly why Ak Gunhak had not killed this man.

No, she even began to think that perhaps, as Ak Gunhak had said, this man had been the one who had not killed him.

Of course, that did not mean she could accept the proposal on the spot.

“Would someone like me truly be necessary in that ordinary life you seek?”

“Did I not say earlier? That life feels harder to me than unifying the murim.”

Even after eliminating all the forces behind the scenes, that would not be the end.

His promise with his father.

If he could not persuade his father, war would break out. Geom Mugeuk still believed preventing that would be more difficult than eliminating the people behind it all.

“I’m not trying to deny the life you’ve lived as an assassin. If you walk with me and come to think that this is not your path, then I will truly let you go.”

It was an offer he made because he needed her, but it was also for her sake. Since their fates had already become entwined, he hoped she would try living a life that was not that of an assassin.

In his life before regression, she had lived her entire life as a nameless assassin. If she was someone who could silently endure such a life, he believed she could live another life just as well.

“If your subordinates weigh on your mind, then give them the choice.”

He meant that those who had no desire to devote themselves to the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult should be set free.

“Give me time to think.”

“Take your time. In any case, for now, we must return to the main cult together.”

Geom Mugeuk’s gaze turned again toward the pavilion on the lake.

Sipchilgyo and the Crescent Moon Mumyeongaek were still there. He had thought they would soon end things because it would be awkward, but whatever the two were talking about, they remained there still.

When Geom Mugeuk used the Divine Eye Art to look, he saw Sipchilgyo laughing. What on earth could that blunt Mumyeongaek have said to make an assassin laugh?

“Ah! I’m dying of curiosity. I need to hear what those two are saying! Are you sure there’s no secret assassin technique for eavesdropping from far away?”

* * *

That night, lost in all sorts of thoughts, Cha I-ran could not fall asleep.

In the end, she left her quarters to get some fresh air.

She was not the only one who could not sleep late into the night.

In the courtyard of the inner compound, Ian was looking up at the night sky.

When Cha I-ran walked toward her, Ian greeted her first.

“You haven’t gone to sleep yet?”

Ian’s gaze toward Cha I-ran was infinitely gentle. There was good reason for it.

“I didn’t know earlier. That Miss Cha had risked danger for the Young Cult Leader’s sake.”

Ian bowed respectfully to Cha I-ran.

“Thank you so much for saving the Young Cult Leader.”

At that deeply courteous greeting, Cha I-ran could tell. This woman truly cared for the Young Cult Leader.

Young Cult Leader, you are greedy indeed. To have someone like this and still try to take me in as a subordinate.

“I did it for myself as well, so there’s no need to mind it so much.”

Even so, Ian’s eyes were full of gratitude, so Cha I-ran looked up at the sky and changed the subject.

“What were you looking at so intently?”

“No matter how busy you are, look up at the sky once a day! That is something the Young Cult Leader always says. I forget it every day, so there are many nights like this when I end up looking at the stars late.”

After staring at the stars like that for a while, Cha I-ran suddenly asked.

“What sort of person is the Young Cult Leader?”

Perhaps because the question was sudden, Ian could not answer easily.

“I asked too abruptly, didn’t I? If you don’t want to say, you don’t have to.”

That was not why Ian had not answered.

“No. It’s not that I don’t want to answer. It’s just that he’s someone difficult to define in a single phrase.”

Cha I-ran understood Ian’s words. Yes, Geom Mugeuk was not someone who could be explained easily simply because one had known him for a long time.

“The Young Cult Leader made me an offer. He said he wanted to take me as his subordinate.”

Ian nodded with an expression that said she had expected it.

“Did the Young Cult Leader perhaps tell Miss Ian?”

“No.”

“And yet you’re not surprised.”

“Because I think I would have done the same if I were the Young Cult Leader.”

After looking at Ian for a moment, Cha I-ran said,

“You’ve learned something bad from the Young Cult Leader. Saying the things the other person wants to hear.”

“Is that a bad thing?”

Ian stared directly at her and said,

“It means you’re making an effort, doesn’t it? At the very least, it means you thought in advance about what the other person might want to hear. If those words cause harm to the other person, then it would be bad. But becoming the Young Cult Leader’s subordinate—I don’t think that is a bad thing.”

Cha I-ran felt a slightly different impression from Ian than what she had seen until now. When Ian spoke of matters related to the Young Cult Leader, light shone in her eyes.

“Are you perhaps afraid of your past life changing?”

At Ian’s question, Cha I-ran gave no answer. Yes, if she was hesitating, that would be the greatest reason.

After living the life of an assassin, she was now to become the blade of the Young Cult Leader of the Demon Cult? The words that she would readily do so did not come easily. No matter how special Geom Mugeuk felt.

“I lived my whole life as the Young Cult Leader’s guard. It was work I loved, and I had never even thought of any other life.”

Whether she had resolved herself today or not, Ian spoke far more than usual. At first, it was because if Geom Mugeuk wanted her, she wanted to help him even in this way.

But as the conversation continued, her sincerity came out before she knew it.

“But the Young Cult Leader changed that life for me. Now I am not the Young Cult Leader’s guard, but the captain of the Ghost Shadow Unit. Do you know what I felt through that process?”

“What did you feel?”

Ian’s gaze, turned once more toward the stars in the night sky, deepened.

“A life spent doing what you love is, rather than enjoyable, truly arduous.”

That was precisely what she had felt after quitting her duties as a guard and becoming captain of the Ghost Shadow Unit.

“Because it’s something you love, you want to do it well. And because you want to do it well, you have to work truly hard. You have to grit your teeth and endure things you hate. I thought that if you did something you loved, it would always be enjoyable, but it is such a difficult thing. It is difficult because you love it.”

Ian’s gaze, which had been looking at the stars, turned to her.

“Do you know what I think these days when I open my eyes in the morning? Ah! I don’t have to protect anyone today. I only have to take good care of myself and live. You have no idea how much relief that thought gives me. It was an emotion I never knew at all when I was actually doing guard work. I also learned this time that there are some emotions you can only feel after you stop doing that work.”

Those words stood in contrast to the way she had so diligently protected the Lord of Cheonhwaru. But that effort, and the words she spoke now, were both sincere.

At Ian’s honest words, Cha I-ran thought inwardly. What would a life without commissions be like for her? Would she, too, feel that sort of relief like Ian?

“Try feeling what that state of mind is like at least once. Even if you end up living as an assassin again, I am certain it will help you, Miss Cha.”

Cha I-ran could feel that Ian sincerely wished for her to become Geom Mugeuk’s subordinate.

“Why are you saying all this to me? If I become the Young Cult Leader’s subordinate, there’s nothing advantageous in it for you, Miss Ian.”

“If you mean that the Young Cult Leader’s affection might be divided.”

Ian smiled and added,

“Then you still do not know the size of our Young Cult Leader’s heart. His heart would have room to spare even after holding the entire world.”

“Is it not Miss Ian’s heart that is great?”

“I am only pretending it is.”

Ian bowed her head in farewell, then walked away.

After walking some distance, Ian turned back and said,

“Oh, and don’t worry that you may become unable to leave. The Young Cult Leader is someone who somehow always tries to send the people around him away.”

At first, he had also told Ian to leave for the sake of her own happiness.

And Cha I-ran already knew that as well. He was someone who tried hard to let Ak Gunhak go.

All of Ian’s words today had ultimately been for the sake of saying this.

“So when he holds on to you, grip that hand tightly.”

* * *

The next morning, Sinhwa Hall was in the midst of preparations for departure.

Geom Mugeuk and his party were preparing to return to the cult.

Several carriages had been readied. Apart from the carriages for the leaders, the remaining carriages carried the wounded.

The uninjured Mumyeongaek and the assassins of the Gain Sect all rode horses and guarded the carriages. Every one of them wore bamboo hats and veils.

Before Geom Mugeuk departed, Bi Sain came to see him. It was to say farewell and to express his gratitude.

Thanks to that, the assassins of the Gain Sect were able to witness the rare sight of the heir to the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult and the heir to the Evil Dao Alliance standing side by side, conversing.

Bi Sain expressed his thanks.

“I am grateful again this time.”

Had he not informed them in advance about this incident as well, there was no telling what might have happened to the leader of the Evil Dao Alliance.

“For this matter, I should be the one thanking you. Because the Alliance Leader entrusted me with this task, I gained a great deal.”

Bi Sain truly liked this honesty of Geom Mugeuk’s. Living in the murim, one came to realize it. People tried in every possible way to take credit, and in every possible way to make others indebted to them.

But Geom Mugeuk was different. There was no distortion in his debts and grudges.

“I still believe that the plot aimed at the Alliance Leader is ongoing. So you must pay close attention.”

At Geom Mugeuk’s words, Bi Sain nodded. Until the full story of this incident was revealed, he intended to guard Baek Jagang together with the Thirteen Wolves of the Evil Dao.

“If Tongcheongak obtains any information related to the Alliance Leader, I will send it at once.”

“Thank you. We will respond in coordination as well.”

The two faced each other and exchanged reassuring looks. Seeing the mischief rising in Geom Mugeuk’s eyes, Bi Sain spoke first.

“Don’t do it. Just go!”

Of course, Geom Mugeuk was not someone who would simply go.

“You need to be this quick-witted to have a secret romance.”

After teasing Bi Sain to the very end, Geom Mugeuk climbed into the carriage. Sticking his head out of the carriage window, Geom Mugeuk called out loudly to everyone.

“Now then, let’s go home!”

* * *

The ceiling was high.

Large stone pillars supported the domed ceiling.

What was painted on the walls and ceiling were constellations. Countless stars had been drawn there as though the night sky itself had been moved inside, and among them, the stars that held special meaning in martial studies were depicted most prominently.

And in this place, there were two things Geom Mugeuk would have recognized at once had he come here.

First, in a corner of the space, there was a large secret chest. The very object Ak Gunhak had carried back had been stored here.

The second was the pattern painted on one wall of the space.

It was the very pattern Geom Mugeuk had seen beneath the Huanglong Martial Hall.

Six round circles surrounding the secret chest in the center.

From four of those circles, a faint light was emerging.

Black, blue, white, and yellow.

Astonishingly, those colors were the colors of the beads Geom Mugeuk had absorbed.

Before them sat a middle-aged woman. Filled with a calm yet sacred aura, she was the descendant of the Divine Maiden Palace and the current Palace Mistress of the Divine Maiden Palace, Jia Yi.

Before her was placed a large bowl, and it was filled with blood.

In the darkness before Jia Yi, an old man sat. The old man’s back was straight, and his two hands rested calmly before his dantian. Even in the darkness, his two eyes shone, and his black pupils were a darkness that pressed down upon darkness itself.

Just then, the secret chest painted on the mural behind Jia Yi seemed to glow.

Whoooosh.

The fifth circle began to shine red.

Jia Yi spoke in a calm voice.

“The energy of the Red Path has appeared in the world.”

Astonishingly, this mural was announcing that the energy inside the bead had emerged outside.

Then the old man in the darkness spoke in a low voice.

“Now the Young Cult Leader will absorb even the energy of the Red Path.”

His voice had a deep resonance, like the sound of an ancient bell.

“The Secret Blood Thread must have entered the Young Cult Leader’s body riding upon the energy of the Red Path.”

At Jia Yi’s words, the old man in the darkness nodded.

For this matter, they had used the Secret Blood Thread, which could only be used once in a generation. If this succeeded, their past failures and sacrifices could all be made up for.

How much time passed like that?

Sssssss.

The bowl of blood placed before Jia Yi began to turn black. Looking down at it, Jia Yi’s eyes widened in shock.

“The Secret Blood Thread has died.”

At her report, the old man’s eyes in the darkness shone intensely. Unable to withstand the energy shooting forth from him, Jia Yi closed her eyes and lowered her head.

“If the Secret Blood Thread has died, then this matter has failed as well.”

At the old man’s words, Jia Yi could only look down at the blackened water with trembling eyes. She still could not believe it.

At that moment, a low murmur of concern flowed from the old man in the darkness.

“In the end, must we awaken him?”

At that, Jia Yi prostrated herself before the old man.

“You must not. Without the Six Paths Primordial Qi, he cannot be controlled. If you release him as he is……”

Whatever else might be uncertain, Jia Yi was certain of this one thing, and so she added firmly.

“This murim will become a sea of blood.”

Chapter 718: When Cornered, Take Off Your Mask
----------------------------------------------
The carriage raced vigorously toward the main compound of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult.

Geom Mugeuk, Geukak Soma, the Heavenly Flower Pavilion Lord, Ian, and Chairan rode in the lead carriage. Originally, they would have split between three carriages, but so many were injured that they had squeezed into one.

Geukak Soma and the Heavenly Flower Pavilion Lord sat side by side, while Ian, Geom Mugeuk, and Chairan sat across from them.

Chairan was able to see an unexpected side of Geom Mugeuk on this carriage journey. It was the sight of him working.

From Eunwol, letters continuously arrived for him.

The missives that had ceased while fighting the enemy now resumed; with him free, the backlog of intelligence was reported to him. What had happened in which region, which master had died at whose hands, what transactions were being completed in the merchant caravans. Gowol was sending only the choicest information.

Fwoosh.

Each time he read a letter, he channeled his Scorching Yang energy and burned it. For contents requiring a reply, he immediately wrote a response and sent it off.

Sometimes he exchanged telepathic messages with Geukak Soma regarding intelligence. It was her first time seeing him work so seriously, and she couldn't quite adjust to it.

Moreover, she came to realize just how vast Geom Mugeuk's experience truly was.

"There's a place called Gwanho Banjeom in that village. It's an eatery run by brothers, and their fish dishes are truly exquisite. Next time you have the chance, you must try it. In the next village, there's a physician called Gu who is incredibly skilled. If you're injured around here, rush to that village."

But that wasn't all. He even knew every backroad and hidden corner.

"Rather than that road, if you turn left and take the lower path, you can get there much faster."

He even gave directions to the Faceless Guest driving the carriage in this manner.

"I'm truly envious of your sharp eyes for the road. My own sense of direction is so poor."

When the Heavenly Flower Pavilion Lord expressed her admiration, Geom Mugeuk spoke with a smile,

"Doesn't the Pavilion Lord see roads far more important than the ones I see?"

The Heavenly Flower Pavilion Lord broke into a pleasant smile. Yes, there are people who make you want to strike up a conversation even if you only exchange a single word with them.

Before the sun set, Geom Mugeuk ordered the carriage to halt.

"Now, let's rest here today."

They were moving while avoiding villages whenever possible. With so many of them, they were bound to attract attention, so they intended to make their way back by camping, inconvenient as it may be.

Leaving a space in the center, they arranged the carriages in a circle, then turned the horses loose in the field to rest.

They lit a large bonfire in the center and prepared places to sleep. The Faceless Guests and assassins were all adept at everything, moving smoothly of their own accord without needing to be told.

"Now, tonight I shall show off my skills."

Geom Mugeuk rolled up his sleeves and stepped forward. Chairan whispered to Ian with an expression that seemed to say, 'Surely the Young Cult Leader isn't good at cooking?'

"It's not going to be a dish we have to force down out of loyalty, is it?"

At Chairan's worry, Ian spoke highly of Geom Mugeuk's culinary skills.

"If you taste it, you'll find loyalty you never knew you had sprouting on its own."

Chairan thought it was an exaggeration. She knew Geom Mugeuk was talented in many ways, but she couldn't believe he was good at cooking as well.

The Faceless Guests and female assassins who had gone out to find provisions returned having hunted a wild boar and gathered vegetables and fruits.

Geom Mugeuk looked for help with the cooking.

"Now, someone who's cut meat and vegetables before, not just people!"

They merely glanced at one another and no one stepped forward. Volunteering to play assistant to the Young Cult Leader of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult was no trivial matter.

"Then it can't be helped. I shall choose just two people to assist me."

Geom Mugeuk chose one from the Faceless Guests and one from the assassins. Naturally, the two were the Crescent Moon Faceless Guest and Sipchilgyo.

"Now, you two, go wash those vegetables and trim them for me."

They could tell Geom Mugeuk had deliberately chosen them because the three of them had met at the pavilion.

Though they had laughed and chatted at the pavilion, they now began working without so much as glancing at each other.

Just then, a telepathic message from Chairan arrived.

—You're too tenacious.

—I must hear what they are talking about!

Chairan shook her head with an expression that said there was no stopping him.

However, Geom Mugeuk's fixation was merely talk; he focused on the cooking. There were far more than just one or two mouths to feed.

Geom Mugeuk began handling the wild boar.

Shhk, shhk, shhk!

His hands moved with blinding speed. In an instant, he trimmed the fur, split the belly, removed the entrails, and drained the blood. Geom Mugeuk was showing off his true skills for the first time in a long while.

After handling the meat, he began making a secret seasoning marinade.

Chairan learned for the first time that there was no seasoning absent from the Young Cult Leader's leather pouch.

He repeatedly coated the meat with the seasoning he had made.

"It should really be aged for the best flavor, but we haven't the time."

Geom Mugeuk stretched out his hand. The energy emanating from his hands enveloped the meat.

"Ah! So that precious martial art is being used to make the marinade seep into the meat."

Geom Mugeuk replied to Ian's jest, which sounded like a sigh.

"Since it's going into your mouths, it's being put to its most precious use."

Geom Mugeuk picked up a log that had fallen on the ground and addressed those around him.

"Now, now, prepare to applaud!"

Having drawn all eyes to himself, he tossed the log into the air.

Whoosh.

The Black Demon Sword was drawn with lightning speed.

In an instant, dozens of sword beams flashed, surrounding the log in the blink of an eye. Then, something showered down.

Thud-thud-thud-thud-thud-thud-thud!

What fell into Geom Mugeuk's hand was, amazingly, dozens of chopsticks.

It was a feat so effortlessly accomplished that one might think they could do it too.

But those who witnessed it firsthand stood with jaws dropped. They were people who would know that this was by no means a feat achievable by just anyone.

They had just watched chopsticks being made with a realm of mastery they might only see once in their entire lives.

Geom Mugeuk did not stop there. He scattered the chopsticks in every direction.

Swishhh.

The chopsticks flew like scattered hidden weapons, landing precisely before each person.

Chairan looked down at the chopstick held in her hand with a startled expression.

The chopsticks were uniform in size and thickness. They were so perfect that one could almost believe he had switched them for purchased goods after dazzling their eyes with sword light.

The Faceless Guests erupted in applause. The assassins joined in as well.

To their sincere admiration, Geom Mugeuk waved his hand with a boastful air, as if it were nothing.

And so ended Geom Mugeuk's cooking, which had been a rampant display of all sorts of secret techniques.

"Eat without feeling burdened. It's not an opportunity that comes often."

The Faceless Guests were one thing, but the assassins of the Gain Sect truly agreed with those words. When in their lives would they ever taste the Young Cult Leader of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult's cooking?

Amazingly, the food was truly delicious. Chairan felt she had never tasted such delicious meat, even at an inn.

But what truly surprised her was neither the exquisite taste of the meat nor the chopsticks he had made by hand.

Geom Mugeuk distributed the food he had made to each person separately. On large, cleanly washed leaves, he placed meat, rice, and vegetables and handed them out. Like a supply veteran dispatched to a battlefield, he served everyone and spoke. Chairan was surprised by what he said.

"I prepared them separately, thinking you are all people who prefer solitude."

It was his consideration, allowing them to take their meals and eat quietly wherever they pleased.

Chairan imagined. If she had been camping and eating with her subordinates?

She probably would have eaten the food her subordinates brought her. She would have eaten with the First or Second Elder. She wouldn't have given the other subordinates a second thought. She would have figured they would fend for themselves.

Yet Geom Mugeuk had even noticed what kind of people the Faceless Guests and assassins were. It was something she herself had never thought of, despite having been with them for so long.

"Hold on to those chopsticks. When I become the Cult Leader later, they'll fetch an incredible price."

After seeing to the Faceless Guests and assassins, he next prepared food for Chairan, the Heavenly Flower Pavilion Lord, and Ian, before finally preparing food for himself and Geukak Soma.

"The three of you, please enjoy your meal. I will eat separately with Lord Soma."

And so, Geom Mugeuk and Geukak Soma flew to a distant hill and made their seats.

Once they were alone, Geukak Soma slowly removed his mask and rested it atop his head. He was mimicking what Geom Mugeuk often did when he wore a mask.

How could Geom Mugeuk forget to praise Soma's face once more?

"Should you ever find yourself cornered by a female enemy, use the strength of your final palm strike to tear off your mask. Then an opportunity to turn the tables will arise."

The Young Cult Leader's praise was always pleasant to hear, so Geukak Soma smiled radiantly.

The two ate together. Geom Mugeuk was overjoyed that Geukak Soma had removed his mask before him. He was so glad to share the joy of eating and drinking together with him.

When they finished their meal, Geukak Soma spoke the words he had been unable to say.

"When I stabbed at the Young Cult Leader's heart…."

The honest confession that followed.

"I was frightened."

Geom Mugeuk fell into thought in silence for a moment, then:

"I am sorry. I was not worried in the slightest."

"What if I had made a mistake?"

Having spoken, Geukak Soma put the mask he had rested on his head back on. At least during this hypothetical moment, he could not control his expression. He had not realized it when he stabbed, but afterward, that moment kept recurring in his mind.

Having sensed Geukak Soma's heart, Geom Mugeuk calmly conveyed his own feelings.

"I simply had this conviction. I may die at any time, but I will not die by Lord Soma's dagger. I may not know who will kill me, but at least it will not be Lord Soma. Isn't that obvious! Thinking so put my heart at ease."

Darkness began to settle around them. They could have lit a small campfire, but they did not.

They savored the darkness and silence. Geom Mugeuk, who laughed and chatted cheerfully in front of others, was now incomparably calm and quiet when alone with Geukak Soma.

Geukak Soma knew. He knew that this was Geom Mugeuk's true self. He might even be a more taciturn person than himself.

That was why he wanted to let him rest quietly, if only when they were together.

Geukak Soma did not speak a single word until they left.

* * *

The carriage was now one day away from the main compound of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult.

Today, Geom Mugeuk prepared his final meal for them.

"Now, regrettably, my fantastic cooking ends today."

Everyone was disappointed, but one person was the most dismayed: Sipchilgyo, who had continued to assist with cooking alongside the Crescent Moon Faceless Guest since the first day.

Thanks to Geom Mugeuk, she had been able to make memories washing and trimming vegetables together with the Crescent Moon Faceless Guest.

'Thank you, Young Cult Leader.'

She was sincerely grateful for the Young Cult Leader's thoughtfulness.

Just then, Chairan approached Geom Mugeuk and asked,

"Aren't you tired?"

At her words, all eyes focused on her. She had spoken provocatively where all could see.

The entire journey, Geom Mugeuk had treated everyone with energy. He had cooked for them with his own hands and loosened the mood with jokes and banter.

The Faceless Guests had always liked the Young Cult Leader, but now it was evident the assassins felt goodwill as well.

"I've had no time to tire from the fun of scoring points with all of you."

If that had been an act to score points, they were witnessing the greatest actor in the Central Plains. No one considered it an act.

"And with my internal energy overflowing, what would tire me?"

After a brief pause, she asked,

"I meant, aren't you tired of people?"

It was not a question limited to this occasion.

She cherished her subordinates, but she had no faith in people, which was something else entirely.

Would anything change just because he fed them so sincerely? When the time came to betray, they would apologize even as they betrayed.

"Aren't I cheating?"

"What do you mean?"

"Young Cult Leader of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult. I'm saying these seven characters are a shield that protects me. There are no situations that would be difficult or tiresome for me."

It was his way of saying he would never be worn down by people.

But Chairan knew. She knew those seven characters contained more than just a shield.

Within them was also the blade of desire—that wanted to dominate, to show off, to indulge. It was also the blade of a primal desire to reign through terror.

He did not swing it. Yes, perhaps the very reason she had come this far, that she was standing here having this conversation, was because he had never swung that blade even once.

He had used that blade to carve chopsticks.

Geom Mugeuk turned the question back with a deep look in his eyes.

"Aren't you tired?"

A faint sigh escaped Chairan's lips.

She straightened her back and looked up at the sky.

After gazing silently at the sky, she finally reached her decision.

"Until now, the bridge we crossed was a bridge of death. From now on, I shall build a new bridge to cross."

Chairan respectfully bowed to Geom Mugeuk.

"I, Chairan, dedicate this body to loyally serving the Young Cult Leader."

The female assassins lined up behind her all bowed as one.

"We shall loyally serve the Young Cult Leader."

On the way here, they had already decided to act according to Chairan's wishes.

Geom Mugeuk smiled radiantly and raised Chairan to her feet. His face was filled with joy.

"Thank you. Truly, thank you."

Chairan looked at Ian standing before her. Her face, too, was filled with joy.

"Someone once said, 'When they reach out, hold that hand tight.'"

* * *

The next day, the carriage arrived at the main compound of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult.

Chairan and the assassins were astonished by the overwhelming scale of the headquarters.

The carriage entering the headquarters came to a halt. Chairan and the assassins who disembarked were amazed by the sight before them.

There, all the Faceless Guests of the Valley of Villains had come out to wait. Having seen but ten Faceless Guests before, they were overwhelmed once more by the countless masks filling their vision.

No! In truth, Sipchilgyo was the most startled. She felt that if she were mixed in among them, she would never find the Crescent Moon Faceless Guest again.

At that moment, Chairan was looking elsewhere. Among the giant demonic statues erected around them, one caught her eye.

One demonic statue stood with its hand stretched forward.

Looking up at that giant demon's hand, Chairan could truly appreciate what kind of hand it was that she had grasped.

Beside her, Geom Mugeuk stood shoulder to shoulder with her, looking up at the demonic statue.

"Chairan, welcome home."

Chapter 719: You Just Haven’t Tried My Cooking Yet
--------------------------------------------------
Geom Mugeuk called her name for the first time.

Cha Iran looked at him with a startled face. Geom Mugeuk, as if none of it had happened, treated her with the same decorum as before.

“From now on, you must get acquainted with those demon ghost statues.”

Cha Iran recalled what Geom Mugeuk had said a moment ago.

*Cha Iran, welcome home.*

She had never been under anyone’s protection until now.

A life lived on her own strength ever since she came of age.

But hearing those words from him, she felt a strange sensation. She couldn’t say why, but she felt as though someone was protecting her. It felt like a true adult patting her head and telling her she had done well to come home.

“Now that I have become the Young Cult Leader’s subordinate, please speak comfortably.”

“Let’s not. Let’s keep doing as we have until now.”

“Why?”

“Shouldn’t a leader sometimes have an uncomfortable subordinate so that he doesn’t let his guard down?”

Geom Mugeuk said this while looking at Ian.

“If I have a subordinate who is unconditionally on my side, I also need a subordinate like you.”

In truth, it was because of Ak Gunhak. One never knows what the future holds, and there was a chance she could end up with him later. Out of consideration for him, Geom Mugeuk could not carelessly speak down to her.

Taking her as a subordinate was also the surest way to protect her.

“Please do as you’re comfortable.”

Cha Iran looked around at her subordinates.

They looked tense and intimidated, unlike usual. Naturally so. No matter how experienced they were as assassins, how could they not shrink under the overwhelming pressure exerted by the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult?

The endlessly towering buildings, the terrifying demon ghost statues erected between them, and the fierce-looking demonic figures moving about in every direction.

Not to mention the countless Faceless Guests standing before her eyes.

“You heard the Young Cult Leader, right? This is our home now.”

“Yes!”

The assassins answered energetically.

She had come trusting Geom Mugeuk, but her subordinates had come trusting her. She was the one who had to protect them.

Then Geom Mugeuk spoke to her.

“Now that we’re home, we must greet Father first. Let us go together, Miss Cha.”

Cha Iran was startled. He wanted her to meet the Demonic Cult Leader? The Heavenly Demon?

“Why me?”

“When one returns from an excursion, offering greetings is only proper. Since Miss Cha has now become my subordinate, should you not offer your greetings as well?”

It was a reasonable point, so Cha Iran could not refuse.

She left her subordinates waiting there and followed Geom Mugeuk, Geukak Soma, and Ian toward the Heavenly Demon Hall.

The closer she got, the more her heart pounded. She had never trembled this much even when going out on difficult assassinations.

As they walked, Cha Iran asked cautiously.

“What does the Cult Leader think of assassins?”

“He despises them.”

He answered so decisively that she truly thought she had misheard.

“How much?”

“He despises them very much.”

“Please be more specific.”

The answer made her think she would have been better off not asking.

“I cannot say for certain, but probably the second most in the world?”

The Heavenly Demon was the second most likely person in the world to hate her, and she was going to see him? Yet even so, she couldn’t help but ask.

“Then who is the first?”

“Those who use poison. Our poor Poison King.”

With the first place being so specific, she could tell he truly despised assassins second-most in the world.

To the flustered her, Geom Mugeuk piled on further.

“Isn’t that rather comforting?”

How could he say it was comforting when she was so worried and ill at ease?

“Since he will dislike you anyway no matter how hard you try to look good, you can just comfortably give up. Is there anything more exhausting than trying to win someone’s favor?”

Cha Iran shook her head in disbelief. Yet on the other hand, his words were strangely convincing.

“Rather, Ian will be even more ill at ease. These days, Father dotes on Ian.”

Ever since she had become the adopted daughter of Gwonma, Father had treated Ian much more gently.

Hearing this, Ian sighed.

“I wasn’t thinking anything of it until just now, but hearing that has made me feel uncomfortable.”

Having pulled in Cha Iran and Ian, now Geom Mugeuk dragged in Geukak Soma as well.

“But Soma-nim is comfortable, aren’t you? You’ve definitely been marked by Father.”

He was the one who had stood on Geom Mugeuk’s side alone before Father.

Then Geukak Soma said something unexpected.

“I am not comfortable either.”

Geukak Soma added, as if he sincerely meant it.

“Separate from being marked, I always strive to look good before the Cult Leader.”

Geom Mugeuk and Geukak Soma smiled with their eyes.

Geom Mugeuk knew. Father liked people with deep loyalty like Geukak Soma.

He was never someone who would hate another just because that loyalty was not directed at himself. That was why Geukak Soma could comfortably speak of being marked.

And because Geukak Soma knew that as well, he was surely making an effort.

As they conversed, they arrived at the Heavenly Demon Hall.

No one guarded the place. Only rows of pillars shaped like evil ghosts supporting the high ceiling stood in line, and at the end was a massive door.

But Cha Iran could instinctively feel it. This space was by no means empty. Extreme danger lurked everywhere, the kind one would only realize upon entering uninvited.

As expected, when the four arrived, the door opened on its own. It meant someone’s watchful eyes were upon them.

Upon entering the Heavenly Demon Hall, the first thing Cha Iran saw was a red carpet. And at the end of that path of blood, she saw a man sitting upon the throne.

*So that is the Demonic Cult Leader.*

Even from afar, his presence could be felt. It was an intense presence she had never experienced in her life.

Usually, when martial arts reached the pinnacle, one hid their very presence. But now, the Demonic Cult Leader seemed to be saying:

*Why should I hide?*

The four walked the path of blood and arrived beneath the throne.

Geom Mugeuk greeted his father, who sat guarding his place unchangingly, in a loud voice.

“Father, have you been well? I have returned safely.”

Their gazes intertwined in the air.

Geom Ujin saw his son’s accomplishments at a glance.

“You have had gains.”

Geom Mugeuk said, looking back at Geukak Soma.

“Thanks to Soma-nim, I obtained a great fortuitous encounter.”

He had not yet given a detailed report regarding Bihyeolsa. No matter that it was his father, he thought Father might be startled by news that his son’s heart had been pierced, so he planned to tell him in person.

When the credit for the fortuitous encounter was turned to him, Geukak Soma spoke calmly.

“I merely helped briefly as I was ordered.”

After speaking so humbly, Geukak Soma politely paid his respects.

“I have returned, Cult Leader.”

“You’ve worked hard.”

Indeed, what filled Geom Ujin’s gaze toward Geukak Soma was trust. If he had not trusted him, he would not have sent him out alone with the Young Cult Leader.

Next, Ian greeted him.

“Ian has returned from my excursion.”

As Geom Mugeuk had said, the gaze with which Geom Ujin looked at Ian was gentle.

“Did you have a good trip?”

“Thanks to your concern, I have returned safely.”

Finally, Geom Mugeuk introduced Cha Iran.

“This is the person who became my subordinate on this excursion.”

Cha Iran politely paid her respects.

“I am called Cha Iran. It is truly an honor to meet such a noble person.”

Of course, Geom Ujin had already received a report on her from the Tongcheongak.

Cha Iran spoke in a trembling voice.

“Though I was born an assassin, from now on I wish to live a new life.”

But Geom Ujin’s expression held no trace of welcome. He did not even hide his displeasure toward her.

At those cold eyes, Geom Mugeuk spoke to Cha Iran.

“Perhaps you might be first rather than second on Father’s list of those he hates.”

Geom Ujin’s gaze, which had been on Cha Iran, turned toward his son. There was a definite reason for the father’s coldness.

“What would you have done if that woman’s goal was to infiltrate our cult?”

Geukak Soma looked at Geom Mugeuk, and from the other side, Ian looked at him as well.

The moment Ian heard Geom Ujin’s words, her heart sank. What Geom Ujin had just said meant the assassination could still be ongoing. It was something Ian had never considered.

“What if that woman has wormed her way into the cheap sentimentality of yours that courts death?”

Recently, Father had refrained from using the word “cheap” when talking about his son’s sentiments.

Now Father was asking again, using the expression “cheap sentimentality.” He was reprimanding him.

Cha Iran turned her head to look at Geom Mugeuk.

Rather, she agreed with Geom Ujin’s words. An assassin was someone who would use any means to kill their target.

If she had truly taken a commission to kill Geom Ujin, she might have approached by gaining Geom Mugeuk’s trust just like this.

While everyone was curious how he would explain, an irresponsible answer flowed from Geom Mugeuk.

“I hadn’t thought that far. If that were the case, Father, I’m sure Uncle Hwi would protect you.”

In fact, he had thought about it. How could he not, when bringing an assassin into the main cult?

But this time, he knew for certain that her target was the Sado Maengju.

Because there had been a prophecy.

Even though he had reported all of this process to his father, the fact that Father was speaking like this was not just about this matter, but a warning to be careful in the future as well.

He was worried that his son might ruin things by not remaining calm. And since his opponent happened to be an assassin, he was using this opportunity to emphasize it properly.

Normally, even if Cha Iran was displeasing, he would have simply nodded once and let it go. That was Father’s way of dealing with people he disliked.

It was precisely because he had a heart that worried for his son that such questions arose.

“What made you choose the Young Cult Leader?”

After a moment of thought, she gave an unexpected answer.

“The chopsticks.”

At Geom Ujin’s questioning gaze, Cha Iran explained.

“When we were making camp, the Young Cult Leader cut a log to make chopsticks for me. Looking at those chopsticks, I thought that if it was this person, I could trust him and follow him.”

She did not avoid Geom Ujin’s gaze, which seemed to pierce through to her very heart.

“And the meal the Young Cult Leader cooked, eaten with those chopsticks, was simply too delicious.”

Cha Iran said no more, as if those were all her reasons. Could it be that not explaining in exhaustive detail was what pleased Father?

From Father’s lips, which had been cold the entire time, words she had never expected flowed out.

“It is probably because you have not tried my cooking.”

At those words, Cha Iran’s eyes widened in surprise.

Geom Mugeuk laughed, speaking for his father’s feelings.

“If you had truly come as an assassin, you would not have given such a ridiculous reason.”

He added one more thing.

“You must never eat Father’s cooking. If you do, you will pledge your loyalty to Father instead of me.”

At his son’s flattery, which he had not heard in a long time, one corner of Geom Ujin’s mouth rose. He did not speak at length to Cha Iran.

“Welcome.”

* * *

When Geom Mugeuk and Geukak Soma headed to the Heavenly Demon Hall, the Faceless Guests mingled.

The Faceless Guests who had come out to greet them approached and greeted the returning Faceless Guests. These ten Faceless Guests who had returned from their excursion were experts ranked from First to Tenth, so many Faceless Guests followed them.

Sipchil Gyo stole a glance at the Crescent Moon Faceless Guest. Several Faceless Guests had also come to greet the Crescent Moon Faceless Guest. At times they were strange to the point of being bizarre, but at times like these, they also seemed like proper martial artists with proper senior-junior relationships.

Watching that scene, Sipchil Gyo’s heart was heavy.

Until coming here, she had held some hope. While washing vegetables together as the Young Cult Leader’s assistant, her feelings for him had grown even more.

But now that she had come to the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult and saw him among the other Faceless Guests, a foreboding feeling arose that her relationship with him might be over.

Then someone spoke quietly beside her.

“It’s just a coincidence.”

She turned to see it was O Gyo. Having recovered somewhat from her injury, she could now walk on her own.

“It’s just a passing feeling.”

She sighed at the sight of Sipchil Gyo being chosen as the Crescent Moon Faceless Guest’s partner. This was someone she needed to keep her distance from, yet they had become entangled.

She didn’t know that Geom Mugeuk had intentionally paired the two of them together. No, even if she had known, she would have said the same thing.

“If you get entangled with a man like that, you’ll become miserable.”

“Do any of us have some great fate?”

“You are different.”

O Gyo sincerely worried for Sipchil Gyo. Calling an assassin pure would make for a strange statement. Yet in her eyes, Sipchil Gyo was exactly the person that strange statement described.

How could she not worry when such a girl was losing her heart to a demonic figure, and one from the Valley of Evil at that?

At that moment, Geom Mugeuk, Geukak Soma, Cha Iran, and Ian returned. The Faceless Guests, who had been scattered about at will, instantly formed ranks.

“Then please rest, Young Cult Leader.”

“Yes, thank you, Soma-nim.”

The two exchanged farewells.

As Soma walked away, all the Faceless Guests followed behind him.

Geom Mugeuk spoke to Ian.

“Guide Miss Cha to her quarters.”

“Yes, I will.”

Since he had sent word ahead, their quarters had already been prepared. He had ordered that each of them be given separate rooms so everyone could rest comfortably.

Geom Mugeuk finished speaking and soared away in a flash.

“Who could he be in such a hurry to see?”

At Ian’s words, Cha Iran recalled what Geom Mugeuk had said. That he would be too busy to see her often. It seemed the abandonment had already begun.

Ian led Cha Iran and the assassins toward their quarters.

Sipchil Gyo could not leave that spot, staring at the Faceless Guests walking in the distance. The moment the Crescent Moon martial artist slipped from view, she had no idea where he was.

Sipchil Gyo let out a sigh without realizing it. O Gyo, standing behind her, spoke.

“Let’s go too. This place is so vast that one wrong move and you’ll get lost.”

Getting lost among demonic figures was not something she wanted to think about.

It was precisely when Sipchil Gyo was about to turn around and follow O Gyo.

Among the Faceless Guests walking in the opposite direction, one Faceless Guest pushed through his companions and emerged.

Surprisingly, it was the Crescent Moon Faceless Guest.

He took out the dagger Sipchil Gyo had given him as a gift. Sipchil Gyo despaired. Now that they had returned to the cult, he was undoubtedly trying to return the dagger to end their relationship.

But that wasn’t it.

The Crescent Moon Faceless Guest cut his own finger with the dagger and drew a long diagonal line on his mask with the blood.

Intense emotion swept across Sipchil Gyo’s face. She understood. The Crescent Moon Faceless Guest had drawn a line on his mask to make it easy for her to recognize him.

*This mask with the line drawn on it is me.*

After drawing the line, the Crescent Moon Faceless Guest turned and ran back toward the Faceless Guests.

From behind Sipchil Gyo, who watched that figure with trembling eyes, O Gyo spoke.

“Sipchil, no. He’s a real madman!”

It was the moment a new life began in earnest for someone, and a new love began in earnest for another.

Chapter 720: No Need to Come Anymore
------------------------------------
“No matter what anyone says, I came to see you first, Elder. Even if my heart were stabbed, I would come to see you first, and even if Father scolded me, I would come to you first. Are you pulling the Doma act again? Aren't you tired of it? Then what are the other demon clansmen for? No matter how disappointed you may be, Elder comes first for me!”

Outside the window, Geommugeuk raised his voice, while Hyeolcheon Doma sat before him reading a book.

Geommugeuk had parted ways with Geukak Soma and rushed straight to Hyeolcheon Doma’s residence.

“You’re pretending to read right now, but I know full well the words aren’t entering your eyes. I also know you’re holding back because you’re too shy to hug me when you’re so glad to see me. Just stop holding it in and express it! Isn’t life something where no one knows what will happen tomorrow?”

Hyeolcheon Doma, who had been reading, raised his head.

“Are you done?”

When Geommugeuk nodded, Hyeolcheon Doma removed the cotton he had been using to block his ears.

“That’s too much! I was stabbed straight through the heart and came back alive!”

When Hyeolcheon Doma did not believe him, Geommugeuk tore open his chest.

“Look at this!”

Hyeolcheon Doma’s eyes, which had glanced over casually, snapped wide open. Where mischief had flickered mere moments earlier, surprise now reigned completely.

“What is that wound?”

Hyeolcheon Doma was so startled that Geommugeuk quickly covered the wound with his clothes.

“No, it’s nothing serious. Just a light scratch.”

In that instant, Hyeolcheon Doma’s aura exploded.

“You masked bastard!”

Hyeolcheon Doma sprang to his feet. He was about to go find Geukak Soma immediately, intending to hold him responsible for failing to protect Geommugeuk properly.

For if he were told right now that Geukak Soma had stabbed him, it was obvious he would fly into a rage before hearing any explanation.

“If it weren’t for Soma-nim, I would have died.”

Hyeolcheon Doma looked at Geommugeuk as if glaring. It had truly been a long time since he had seen Hyeolcheon Doma this angry. That was right—this was what he was originally like. He had simply forgotten because Hyeolcheon Doma had treated him so gently all this time.

“This time, Soma-nim wiped out all the enemies! It’s true!”

That was how he soothed Hyeolcheon Doma’s rage.

Hyeolcheon Doma sat back down.

“Pathetic! At your level, isn’t it about time you stopped getting stabbed by swords?”

“There were too many beauties, so I got distracted.”

Hyeolcheon Doma began reading his book again. Was it because he had seen the wound? This time, he spoke first.

“Have you already lost interest in reading?”

“I was too busy; there was no time to read.”

“Excuses. If you set your mind to read, you’d read even while fighting.”

After reading for a brief moment, Hyeolcheon Doma raised his head. Wondering what Geommugeuk was doing when no words were spoken, he saw that Geommugeuk was leaning against the wall by the window, looking up at the sky.

How long had he been like that? Geommugeuk suddenly spoke.

“From tomorrow, I will enter closed-door training.”

As Hyeolcheon Doma raised his head, Geommugeuk, who had been looking at the sky, turned to face him.

“It may take quite a while.”

Geommugeuk felt the Great Perfection of the Ten Stars drawing near with great strides.

Great Perfection was flickering right before his eyes.

He planned to greet his father and then enter closed-door training immediately.

And once he entered, he intended not to emerge until he achieved Great Perfection.

In his heart, he felt he could achieve Great Perfection and come out in just a few days, but that was merely wishful thinking. It was impossible to know whether it would take a month or several years. Still, he had hope that thanks to the Sicheonbisul, it would not take an absurdly long time.

Hyeolcheon Doma precisely read Geommugeuk’s current situation.

“You stand before Great Perfection?”

“Yes.”

Even though he had asked the question himself, Hyeolcheon Doma was inwardly shocked by the affirmative answer.

To think that at such a young age, he was already on the verge of achieving the Great Perfection of the Guhwamagong! Had there ever been a Cult Leader in history who achieved Great Perfection so quickly? He would not have believed it had it been anyone but Geommugeuk.

“I’ve gotten the feeling that it is not far.”

For some reason, Hyeolcheon Doma neither offered congratulations nor told him to work hard. He simply gazed at Geommugeuk in silence.

Just then, someone entered.

“Young Cult Leader!”

The one who ran over happily was Seodaeryong, who had come to train after finishing his duties.

“When did you return?”

“Just a short while ago.”

“I missed you!”

Seodaeryong ran to embrace Geommugeuk.

Geommugeuk lightly dodged him and peered into the basket in his hand. Inside were liquor and food.

“What is this? It looks delicious!”

“You can’t have this!”

Seodaeryong hurriedly turned to protect the basket, then brought it over to Hyeolcheon Doma.

“This is a dish Lady Dana prepared for Master.”

It was indeed a dish made by Dana. She often cooked for Hyeolcheon Doma, Seodaeryong’s master. The food made with healthy ingredients was a sincere dish one could never find in an inn.

“I told her she didn’t have to send them.”

“I just made a little extra while cooking for us. Please enjoy it.”

Hyeolcheon Doma knew very well that it was not so. Most of the dishes brought were things he liked.

“Tell her I will enjoy it.”

“Yes, Master.”

Hyeolcheon Doma took the food and went inside the house. Dana’s cooking always satisfied him, so he wore a contented smile.

And so, as only the two of them remained in the courtyard, Geommugeuk subtly asked.

“How are Lady Dana and her siblings?”

“Yes, thanks to you, they are doing well.”

Dana as well as her two younger siblings, Danbi and Danyeon, had adapted well to the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult. Danbi served as an execution martial artist, and Danyeon as a warrior of the Gwiyeongdae, each fulfilling their roles well.

Dana was immersed in martial arts training while looking after Seodaeryong. She was someone who would gladly do any task in the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult, even cleaning horse dung, but for the time being, she had judged that taking care of Seodaeryong and her siblings was more important.

Especially since Seodaeryong was carrying out the affairs of Hwangcheon Gak on top of martial arts training—such a grueling schedule—that she looked after his health. Thanks to that, Seodaeryong often heard these days that he was looking well.

“Is Lady Dana that wonderful? Does the mere thought of her make you smile?”

Seodaeryong blossomed into a full smile. The answer was contained in that smile.

“Why don’t you get married?”

“Only when you go, Young Cult Leader, will I go. Please clear the way! Let the carriage behind pass!”

“There are many roads; why come to a blocked road and shout for it to move?”

“The only path I walk is the one you walk, Young Cult Leader. I will follow that path for the rest of my life.”

“Fine excuses.”

Geommugeuk narrowed his eyes and looked at Seodaeryong with a suspicious gaze.

“Why are you looking at me like that?”

“You lack confidence, don’t you?”

“Oh my, what do you take me for? I am Seodaeryong, your right hand. Seodaeryong!”

“Yes, my right hand who has never even properly dated a woman.”

In the end, Seodaeryong sighed and revealed his honest feelings.

“Honestly, I don’t know. Whether I’m allowed to get married. Whether I can feed and support that woman.”

“If the Hwangcheon Gakju of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult says such things, then no one could ever marry. And why are you worried about supporting her? If you marry Lady Dana, she’ll probably be the one supporting you.”

Given Dana’s refreshing and decisive personality, it was entirely plausible.

“Stop that and tell me the real reason you’re hesitating.”

How could he deceive Geommugeuk’s eyes? In the end, Seodaeryong revealed the real reason.

“I’m scared I won’t be able to become a good father.”

Geommugeuk hadn’t known Seodaeryong harbored such worries. No matter how close people are, the depths of one’s inner path run deep indeed.

“To worry about such things before even getting married?”

But he could not say that it was just like him. Because it was something Geommugeuk could not be confident about either.

“I never experienced a good father. I don’t know how to become one.”

Seodaeryong truly wanted to know.

“Should I just do the opposite of our father?”

“Would that work?”

“Wouldn’t it?”

Seodaeryong let out a sigh. Geommugeuk let him sigh to his heart’s content. Since long-suppressed emotions had come out, it wouldn’t matter if the ground caved in a little.

“Even if you had met a good father, I think it would be separate from you becoming a good father.”

“Why?”

“Just as you and Father are different, the child born will also be different from you. The way a good father did things wouldn’t work exactly the same. I think this isn’t something you learn by watching, but something you figure out by looking at your child and finding the method that suits that child.”

“!”

“It’s just like stepping onto the dueling platform without knowing who your opponent is. You step on thinking, no matter who the opponent is, it’s going to be terribly hard.”

Seodaeryong’s expression relaxed.

“So in the end, it’s all hard?”

“Look at you, look at me. How could raising such sons be easy?”

At those words, Seodaeryong’s face, which had been drooping, became comfortable.

Hyeolcheon Doma, who had been sitting by the window drinking, interjected.

“If family are enemies, would children be any different?”

Geommugeuk and Seodaeryong laughed together.

Geommugeuk pointed out one fact.

“And you don’t need to worry about that from the start.”

“Why?”

“Lady Dana will make you into a good father.”

At that, Seodaeryong smiled brightly.

“Young Cult Leader, how do you know so well without having experienced it yourself?”

“It’s me. Your Young Cult Leader.”

“Of course, it’s our Young Cult Leader, who knows everything under the heavens!”

The two laughed together.

“That I can have such happy worries is all thanks to you, Young Cult Leader.”

“As long as you know. Pay me back in money later.”

“I will continue to pay you back only with my heart forevermore.”

Geommugeuk turned away.

“You’re leaving already? We finally met; stay and enjoy yourself longer!”

To the disappointed Seodaeryong, Geommugeuk spoke.

“You won’t be seeing me for a while.”

The moment the words fell, Hyeolcheon Doma emptied his wine cup by the window and spoke.

“Come out tomorrow.”

It was an unexpected remark. Even though he had clearly said he would enter closed-door training, he told him to come out.

Geommugeuk answered without asking the reason.

“I will see you tomorrow.”

When tomorrow came, he would find out.

* * *

But even when tomorrow came, he did not know the reason.

The next day, Hyeolcheon Doma did not tell him why he had been called. He simply sat in the same spot as yesterday, reading his book.

“Is there something you wish to command me?”

“No.”

“Or something you wish to say?”

“No.”

“Have you been lonely these days?”

“This room is full of friends.”

He meant that books were his friends.

“Could it be that these days you feel inexplicably irritated, like you want to torment someone, such feelings?”

Hyeolcheon Doma snorted.

“If not, then why did you call me here?!”

No matter how much he asked from the side, Hyeolcheon Doma would not tell him.

Of course, he could not say, “If there is nothing for me to do, I shall take my leave,” and simply go. Because his opponent was Hyeolcheon Doma.

That afternoon, Seodaeryong dropped a bombshell.

“I told Lady Dana.”

“What?”

“That I want to get married.”

Geommugeuk was surprised, and so was Hyeolcheon Doma.

“Really?”

“You told me to?”

“I didn’t tell you to. No, even if I had, would you go and do it the next day?”

“What good would worrying do? Your words are right, Young Cult Leader, and you’re smarter than me. And I’ve come to want to step onto that dueling platform.”

“Didn’t you say you’d follow only my path your whole life? The carriage hasn’t even cleared the road yet?”

Seodaeryong lowered his head as if ashamed, but he said what he had to say.

“I’m much older, aren’t I? The carriage blocking the road is me. A cart full of manure.”

“So what did Lady Dana say?”

“She said she was bewildered because she heard it while eating.”

Geommugeuk asked with a dumbfounded expression.

“You said that while she was eating?”

“I’m regretting it plenty, so please don’t scold me for that part. The words just came out while she was eating. That I wanted to sit across from her and eat like this for the rest of my life.”

Seodaeryong clutched his head.

“Ah! I should have recited a poem while saying it.”

Geommugeuk spoke with a smile.

“No. Rather, you did well.”

“It really was the right thing to do, right? Please tell me it was.”

To Seodaeryong, who was worried he had made a mess, Geommugeuk spoke with a smile.

“You did well. And that cart is not full of manure, but jewels. You will be able to become a good father.”

Seodaeryong teared up. He had thought there would never be marriage in his life.

“Since you’re doing it anyway, make it the best marriage possible. I’ll ask Father to officiate as well.”

“The Cult Leader?”

Seodaeryong’s eyes widened in surprise.

“A martial artist who marries with the officiation of the Heavenly Demon—you’ll likely be the first.”

It was the kind of thing that would normally make Seodaeryong shriek and play the clown, but now he spoke cautiously.

“If I ever get married, for the officiant…”

Seo Dae-ryong looked at the Blood Heaven Blade Demon and said,

“I would like to ask you, Master.”

For a moment, the Blood Heaven Blade Demon flinched in surprise. He had never imagined the boy would pass over the Cult Leader as officiant and ask him instead.

“Master, please.”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon snorted.

“What a fine thing to ask of a master who has never even married.”

With that, the Blood Heaven Blade Demon turned sharply and went into the house.

At the sight, Seo Dae-ryong went pale.

“Did I make a mistake?”

Though he had grown close to his master, Seo Dae-ryong still found him difficult and was always careful around him.

Geom Mugeuk watched the Blood Heaven Blade Demon’s back and tried to imagine his expression. Might there not be a smile at the corners of his mouth?

“No. He’s probably in a good mood right now.”

He must have gone inside because he could not control his expression.

Just then, the Blood Heaven Blade Demon’s unexpected words came from within.

“If you yearn for something too desperately, it only grows farther away.”
